- **A GC EXPLANATION FOR A WALL DELTA IS REFUTED BY THE PAUSE *BUDGET*, REGARDLESS OF SIGN, AND ONE `-Xlog:gc` PROCESS PER ARM SETTLES IT (round 893).** Warm GC here is **~92-98 ms of a ~5.4 s rebuild = ~1.7%** on both arms, so nothing GC-shaped can explain an 8% effect — and the faster binary in fact took MORE pauses (86 vs 68) after 2.2 M copied map entries were removed. Removing allocations is not a GC lever at this scale (round 801's "an allocation count is not a cost", one instrument over).
- **A MEMO HIDES ITS *MISS* COST FROM EVERY SECTION PROBE IN THIS REPO, WHICH IS WHY A MEMOIZED FUNCTION IS A PLACE TO LOOK FOR UNBRACKETED WORK RATHER THAN EVIDENCE THAT THE WORK IS BOUNDED (round 870).** `getTypeParamInfo` answers **96.2%** of its 28,663 questions from cache and the surviving 3.8% cost **82 ms = 1.16% of a warm rebuild**, because each miss iterated **2,992,718** symbols to find **2,184** module-flagged ones — the miss's cost lands in whichever ordinary checker predicate happened to ask first (`checkTypeArgCount`, `isUnresolvedGenericType`), so no pass row names it. The general shape to look for: **a miss body that re-derives a property of the FROZEN binder tables per call, when only the key varies** — hoist the table-derived part (`buildModuleSymbolScanIndex`), leave the key-dependent probe live, and the row is CONTROLLED because the population cannot move.
- **A `--serve` REQUEST PAYS ~2 ms OVER AN IN-PROCESS `BenchMain` REBUILD, AND THE ~279 ms A CLIENT ADDITIONALLY WAITS IS A FRESH CLIENT JVM, NOT THE DAEMON (round 871).** Bracket a request three ways — client wall ⊃ server `CompileResponse.elapsedMs` ⊃ the compiler's own `time:` line, all three already printed by the shipping binary — before theorising about daemon overhead; `runCli`'s whole envelope (argument parse, mode ledger, diagnostic formatting, stdout capture, JSON encode) is 1–3 ms warm. So **a warm figure taken with `BenchMain` IS the daemon figure**, and the client cost is the native thin client's job. `scripts/round871-serve-ladder.sh`, `docs/perf/warm-serve-request-attribution.md`.
- **A PARSE MAY BE SHARED ACROSS PROGRAMS — THE AST IS WRITTEN ONLY BY `indexSourceFile` AND `Parser` IS PURE IN `(source, fileName, flags)` — BUT ANY CROSS-REQUEST REUSE MUST BE KEYED ON THE **CONTENT**, NEVER ON AN mtime/size/stat (round 871).** `RealLibSnapshots` has done this for lib files since M2.1(c) and `CrawlParseCache` now does it for program files; the crawl reads every file on every request anyway, so the bytes are in hand and staleness is not expressible. **And know WHERE a mis-keyed hit bites: the compilation core re-checks content at `ParsedSource` (INV.1(e)), so a wrong hit there degrades to a redundant parse and a CORRECT type-check — the place with no second gate is the CRAWL, which has already used the stale tree's `moduleSpecifiers` to decide WHICH FILES EXIST.** The wrong answer is a missing FILE, not a wrong type, and only a pin whose edit ADDS AN IMPORT sees it. BINDING is still per-consumer (symbol merging mutates its inputs).
- **INTERLEAVING A "PARSE" ARM WITH A "NO-PARSE" ARM REQUEST-BY-REQUEST INFLATES THE PARSING ARM BY ~6x — BLOCK THEM (round 871).** One `--parseCacheOff` request dropped into a run of cached ones read **896 ms** of crawl WALL against a blocked-arm median of 138 ms, because the parser had not executed for several requests and was no longer hot. The usual ABBA-rotation advice assumes both arms exercise the same code every draw; when an arm's whole point is that some code does NOT run, rotation de-optimises it.
- **A NEW CLI FLAG THAT IS NOT IN THE USAGE TEXT FAILS `CliModeRestoreTest` *BEFORE* ITS `ledger.restore()`, WHICH LEAVES EVERY SWEPT MODE ARMED FOR THE REST OF THE TEST JVM — AND THE SYMPTOM IS TEN FAILURES IN SIX UNRELATED CLASSES PLUS A CRASHED TEST PROCESS (round 874, ~40 min).** Its sweep derives the flag list FROM the usage text, so an undocumented flag is an unreached flag; the `unreached.isEmpty()` assertion sits above the restore, so `--frontEnd`, `--flowCensus` and the rest stay ON, and a census `ArrayList` then takes appends from the compile thread of every later test — `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index 1 out of bounds for length 0` inside `ArrayList.add`, i.e. a data-race signature with no data race in the round's own change. **The tell is that the failures are all in probe/census tests and reproduce ONLY when `CliModeRestoreTest` is in the same run**; document the flag (usage text + `ACCEPTED_FLAGS`, and `BEHAVIOUR_CHANGING` if it changes a decision) and they all go away.
- **A LEAF PROFILE MUST BE READ BY MECHANISM AS WELL AS BY ROW — round 874's whole finding is invisible row by row and is 6.95% by family.** The INV.4 reach classifiers (`spine*Status`/`*Edge`/`*Reached`/`tav*`) are **458.8 ms over 66 owners** of which the largest is 0.57%, i.e. below every candidate floor while being the biggest thing in the warm compile; round 868's own win had the same shape and was caught only because its three rows happened to be adjacent and share a name prefix. `scripts/round874_compare.py` does the aggregation, and its other job is round 870's denominator trap — a share is a share of WALL TIME, so quote ms/rebuild or an unchanged cost "rises" after every win.
- **ON tsc's OWN SOURCES THE WHOLE 46-DIAGNOSTIC OUTPUT IS NETTED BY *ONE* PASS (`checkSpine`), SO ANY PER-PASS OR PER-PARTITION DIAGNOSTICS EQUIVALENCE TAKEN ON A DASHBOARD PROFILE COMPARES AN ESSENTIALLY EMPTY POPULATION — AND ALL EIGHT PROFILES ARE THAT SAME CODEBASE (2026-08-23, (INC.17) census, measured by `PassTiming.diagNetByPass` reading 46 against the build's own 46).** `scripts/partition-equivalence.sh` inherits it: 78 files, 46 rows, one emitter — so a change that silently stopped 204 of the 205 partition-reading passes from emitting would print EQUIVALENT. What actually carries such work is the 13k-baseline CORPUS, which has no partition; there is no instrument here exercising many EMITTING passes and a PARTITION at once ((INC.18)). Read a green profile-side partition sweep as a control, never as coverage.
- **…AND `python3 scripts/cost_gate.py 2>&1 | grep <counter names>; echo "exit=$?"` READS THE ***GREP's*** STATUS, SO A **FAILING** GATE PRINTS A PLAUSIBLE TABLE AND `exit=0` (2026-08-27, (CHK.62)).** The verdict lives in the exit code and a trailing `COST GATE FAILED` block that a column-filtering grep drops, and the `<== OVER TOLERANCE` markers sit on lines a per-counter grep may not select. Redirect to a FILE and read `$?` from the gate itself; same shape for `huge_methods.py`.
- **A CANDIDATE PRICED IN DIAGNOSTIC *ROWS* IS NOT PRICED — the flow LOOP-JOIN arm was ranked in rows for three consecutive rounds (8 -> 3 -> 1, i.e. "one row from landing") and measures a **~20x COUNTER blowup** the first time `cost_gate.py` is pointed at the ARM rather than at a commit (2026-08-28, (CHK.68)): `globals.lookups` +1,891%, `typeNode.cacheable` +5,951%, `typeOfExpr.calls` +116%, ~3.5x wall.** The signature of the mechanism is a FLAT population beside inflated calls (`spine.nodes` +0.00%, `typeOfExpr.distinct` +0.98% against `calls` +116%) — the same questions re-asked, i.e. a memo some new guard silently disabled. Cost-gate an unlanded arm before quoting its row count as a price; the wall-clock tell arrives first (a grid that suddenly exceeds its usual runtime).
- **COST GATE (COST.1, round 717): anything that touches the checker runs `python3 scripts/cost_gate.py` alongside the suite.** It runs the compiler profile with `--passTiming`, extracts the DETERMINISTIC counters (getTypeOfExpression calls, narrowWalks, spineNodes, typeNode cacheable/bypassed, globals lookups) and diffs them against `docs/perf/cost-counters.txt`, failing above ±2%. Counters, never wall time — a loaded box swings ±13% and cannot see a 70 ms regression (round 713 added +11.5% getTypeOfExpression calls for one diagnostic and no gate noticed). An increase is not a veto but an accounting demand: justify it in the session note and `--update` the baseline in the SAME commit. Needs `build/bench/tsc-project-*` (create once with `scripts/bench-compile-tsc.sh --project compiler --no-emit --no-log`), and must NEVER run while `jvmTest` is in flight.
- **A `<clinit>` CAN BE OVER THE 8,000-BYTECODE LIMIT, AND ONLY ITS *COLLECTION* CONSTANTS ARE IN IT (round 820).** A Kotlin `private const val` of a primitive/`String` is emitted with a `ConstantValue` attribute and costs the static initializer NOTHING, so ~200 dispatch tags in `Checker`'s companion were free while 50 `setOf`/`mapOf` literals held all 10,339 bytecodes. It shrinks ONLY by hoisting those literals into functions it calls, and they must be **TOP-LEVEL** (`CheckerKt`, an `invokestatic`) — a companion `private fun` is an instance method on `Checker$Companion`, reached through the very static field `<clinit>` is installing. **The constraint that decides what can move: a `private` companion member is INVISIBLE to a top-level function in the same file**, so a literal whose initializer reads one (e.g. `LIB_MIN_TARGET`'s `+ TYPED_ARRAY_NAMES.flatMap { … }` tail) can only move in part. **And the hoist's whole risk is a WRONG-SET SUBSTITUTION**: same-typed builders (five return `Set<String>`) are interchangeable to the type checker, so a mixed-up call site compiles clean and silently changes which names the checker believes exist — `ClinitConstantHoistTest` is the only thing that sees it. Nothing here is priceable: a static initializer runs once, at class load.
- **THE COLD A/B IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO A STEADY-STATE-ONLY EFFECT, AND (JIT.1) IS THE MEASURED PROOF: the huge-method arc reads `+0.08%, B wins 3/5, NOISE-DOMINATED` cold and **-33.6%, 4/4 pairs, 1.505x** WARM (round 845, r802's parent vs r821's close, arms verified at 19-over-limit vs 0 in the bytecode).** So a method crossing 8,000 is a **warm-path** defect: `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` protects the daemon/`--serve`/AOT artifact far more than the CLI, and any change that grows a hot method must be A/B'd with `ab-warm.sh`, never `ab-interleaved.sh`. Corollary for the ~1% quiet-box sd rule: it is calibrated for the +-1% regime and may be overridden EXPLICITLY when the effect is tens of times the arm sd - say so, never silently.
- **A METHOD OVER 8,000 BYTECODES IS NEVER JIT-COMPILED AND NOTHING IN THIS REPO EXCEPT `scripts/huge_methods.py` CAN SEE IT (rounds 802/803).** HotSpot's `DontCompileHugeMethods` is a product flag defaulting to true, so such a method runs INTERPRETED for the whole process: the corpus measures no cost, `cost_gate.py`'s counters do not move, and `-XX:+PrintCompilation` prints no "too large" line (the compile is never *proposed*). Splitting `forEachChild` measured **-3.93%, 5/5 pairs** on its own. **So before "just adding an arm" to a big `when`, run the census — and split by a CONTIGUOUS key range** (each part stays one tableswitch), **keeping the hottest keys in the ENTRY function** and dispatching continuations with one compare, never a fall-through chain. **JIT GATE (JIT.1)(f): a round that touches compiled code runs `python3 scripts/huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` next to `cost_gate.py`, and `HugeMethodLimitTest` runs the same census inside the suite — the SECOND instrument on purpose (the script reads a `javap` rendering, which is a parse away from the truth; the suite test reads `Code` attribute lengths and also FAILS on a STALE `KNOWN_OVER_LIMIT` entry).** The census is 0 as of round 821 and (JIT.1) is CLOSED, so the gate's whole job is to fail the moment a NEW method crosses 8,000. **Never raise the limit to make a red gate green; split the method.** **AND THE CENSUS IS `-core`-ONLY BY DEFAULT (`--classes`), SO IT IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO EVERY OTHER MODULE** — a green run on a round that added compiled code outside core is a control, not a gate (round 909; the tell is a `classes scanned` count identical to the previous round's). (Wiring it into Gradle's `check` is owner-gated as (JIT.3).)
- **EXTRACTING A REGION THAT `continue`s THE CALLER'S LOOP: WRAP IT IN A ONE-ITERATION FRAME, `for (x in listOf(xIn)) { <the region, verbatim> }` — naming the frame variable what the region already called it (round 819, `Transformer.transformToCommonJS`).** A `continue` cannot survive extraction into a member function, and rewriting it to `return <holder>` edits the moved text at exactly the sites hardest to verify; a single-element loop makes `continue` mean what it meant before (abandon the rest of THIS item) and leaves the control-flow token census unchanged on both sides. **Decide WHICH regions need it with a brace-depth scan** (`scripts/tcjs_split_verify.outer_continues`), never by eye — a by-eye census of one arm said four and the scan said five — and assert both directions (every region with a caller-loop `continue` is framed, no other one is). The frame is load-bearing: `listOf(xIn, xIn)` type-checks and processes every item twice.
- **A RECORDED COUNTER BASELINE IS A CLAIM ABOUT A BUILD, NOT ABOUT A COMMIT — when the gate fails with no source change, REBUILD THE COMMIT THAT RECORDED THE BASELINE before believing either number (round 776).** Round 760's rebaseline recorded `typeNode.bypassed 104,162 -> 95,909`; its own commit, rebuilt, measures 104,162, and ablating the veto it credited moves the counter by ONE resolution — five later rounds re-measured against that unreproducible state and inherited it. A recorded delta is not evidence; a rebuild is.
- **A JVM CONSTRUCTOR CANNOT DELEGATE ITS PROPERTY INITIALIZERS, SO `Checker.<init>`'s REMAINING HEADROOM IS SPENT BY ADDING *FIELDS*, NOT PASSES (round 814, (JIT.1)(d)).** After the split, **5,538** of its 8,000-byte budget is the class's **494 `private val` initializers** and only ~34 is the dispatch sequence — every `pass("name") { … }` line now lives in an `initSetupPasses`/`initDeclarationOnlyPasses`/`initCheckPasses1..8` helper (and its lambda is already its own method, because `pass` is non-inline). ~200 more fields put `<init>` back over HotSpot's `HugeMethodLimit`, where **nothing but `scripts/huge_methods.py` can see it**. Two invariants the split adds: those ten calls must stay IN SOURCE ORDER inside their `declarationOnly` guard (run 8 ends with two diagnostic RETRACTIONS that only work after run 1 emitted), and `HugeMethodLimitTest.methodCodeSizes` must keep the LARGEST `Code` length per method NAME — Kotlin emits a 72-byte synthetic `<init>` bridge for default arguments, so a last-wins map reports `<init>` as 72 and passes the limit pin vacuously.
- **PROGRAM ORDER IS THE CRAWL'S ORDER, AND `typeNode.bypassed` / `mapped.keyed` ARE A FUNCTION OF IT (round 776) — `ProjectCompiler.walk` SORTS, and removing the sort silently makes those two counters a property of the filesystem.** Which file first touches a shared type node decides whether INV.5(c) calls the resolution own-file or foreign: three orders of the same 78 files move them ~1% with the AST and every diagnostic bit-identical, so an order change looks exactly like a cost regression and shows up in NO output diff.
- **UNDER `--workers N` A LAZILY-INITIALIZED *SHARED* SINGLETON IS MINTED FROM WHICHEVER WORKER TOUCHES IT FIRST — i.e. AFTER that worker rebased — so its Symbol/Type id lands INSIDE the other workers' id space and the id-keyed relation caches silently confuse it with a worker-local type (round 825; this WAS the `--workers` race).** Nothing in the sequential prefix (crawl/parse/Binder) reads an intrinsic, so `TypeKt.<clinit>` ran in a worker and `anyType.id` measured **1,000,000,005**; the guard is now a DISJOINT id slice per worker plus a pre-touch, both in `runInDeepStackWorkers` (DeepStack.jvm.kt), pinned by `ParallelWorkerIdSpaceTest`. **Any process-wide mutable cache a `Checker` reaches must also be warmed before the workers spawn** (`RealLibSnapshots.prewarmParsedLibFiles`) — a plain `HashMap.getOrPut` from N worker threads is a data race with no exception to find it by.
- **A `--workers` WORKER RUNS ITS OWN ASSIGNED WORK ~50% SLOWER THAN THE SAME WORK RUNS SEQUENTIALLY, AND *THAT* — NOT DUPLICATION — IS THE LARGEST LOSS IN THE PARALLEL PATH (round 879).** The spine is essentially fully partition-scoped (w0 and w1 spend 10.5 and 7.7 s of non-bind work on 3,151 k and 2,275 k assigned chars = a 1.36 ratio against a 1.385 char ratio), yet at the sequential rate of 2.02 ms per k-char w0's share plus its bind should be ~8.2 s against **12.3 s measured**. It is what makes the fitted R rise with N in every ladder here. **Round 878's "D = 9.4-11.2 s, each worker redoes 40-45% of the compile" is RETRACTED** — that fit drew its points from DIFFERENT worker levels, where contention differs (the same single file costs 13,503 ms at w4 and 15,885 ms at w6), so the slope absorbed the contention and dumped it into the intercept; fitting WITHIN a level gives D ~ 3.7 s. **Do not spend workers past 4** (RSS +5% at w4, +75% at w6, and the critical worker gets SLOWER).
- **THE ALIASING IN `mergeSingleSymbol` IS *LOAD-BEARING*: BECAUSE `globals[name]` **IS** THE BINDER'S OBJECT, EVERY READER REACHING A SYMBOL VIA `BinderResult.locals` / `nodeToSymbol` SEES THE MERGED DECLARATIONS FOR FREE — COPY IT AND THEY GET THE UN-MERGED ORIGINAL (round 884, corpus: `extendGenericArray`, `extendGenericArray2`, `jsExportMemberMergedWithModuleAugmentation`).** So tsc's `cloneSymbol` alone is NOT portable here; what makes it work there is the FORWARDING TABLE beside it (`mergedSymbols[source.mergeId]` + a `getMergedSymbol` every reader passes through), and **tsgo's variant is the one to copy — `map[*ast.Symbol]*ast.Symbol` PER-CHECKER, keyed by identity, because tsc writes `mergeId` onto the shared symbol and that is the one thing a shared bind cannot allow.** Ours should be id-keyed (`IntKeyMap`; the clone carries the original's id deliberately, so a fresh id never shifts the sequence). Priced: an extra probe on all **748,522** `globals.lookups` is ~0.24-0.35% of a warm rebuild against the **-5%** that unconditional `--shareBind` is worth. `--mergeClone` has the clone half, OFF by default; the three failing tests are the cheapest map of the read sites that need forwarding. **Both reference implementations are ON THE BOX** — tsc at `build/bench/tsc-project-*/src/compiler/checker.ts`, tsgo at `typescript-go-repo/internal/checker/checker.go` (tag `typescript/v7.0.2`).
- **A LIB GLOBAL NAME DECLARED TOP-LEVEL IN A *MODULE* FILE IS MODULE-SCOPED, AND THE TWO SETS THAT SAY SO — `init:mergeSharedKeepNames` AND `computePerFileVisibility`'s `nonModuleVisible` — ARE **ONE OBSERVABLE**: SEEDING EITHER WITH THE LIB KEYS RESTORES A DEFECT, AND SEEDING ONLY ONE IS *WORSE* THAN SEEDING BOTH (2026-08-26, (CHK.49), measured).** With both seeded, one module's `interface Text` was ADOPTED into the DOM `Text` symbol and EVERY file saw the fusion (round 884: `globals[name]` IS the binder's object) — silently in the dangerous direction, since the module type then answered the lib's members. With only the merge retired it is **969** errors on the compiler profile (round 510's recorded 861 FPs); with only the visibility half, nothing changes. Population: every lib global — **185** names for a plain `es2020` project, **2,242** with `dom` — and all five declaration forms (`interface`/`type`/`class`/`enum`/`const`). **The VALUE meaning must be restored separately** (`libValueBehindTypeOnlyShadow`): tsc resolves per MEANING, so `interface Map<K,V>` in a module hides the lib TYPE and leaves `declare var Map` reachable.
- **…AND A SECOND CHANCE PLACED BELOW `fileLocalTypeMapFor` IS DEAD BY CONSTRUCTION, BECAUSE THAT MAP IS KEYED BY THE FILE'S OWN DECLARATIONS — i.e. BY THE VERY SHADOW IT IS TRYING TO SEE PAST (2026-08-26).** The first cut read `0 RED` on eleven pins and `46` on the profile while `Date.now()` in a module declaring `interface Date` had grown a fresh TS2339; only the ablation said so. Anything in `getTypeOfIdentifier` that must OVERRIDE a file-level declaration goes just below `currentLocalTypes` / `currentParamBindingNames` (genuine inner bindings, which DO shadow in both meanings) and above the type map.
- **A LIBRARY BASELINE THAT IS BYTE-IDENTICAL MAY BE A STATEMENT ABOUT ITS `lib` SETTING RATHER THAN ABOUT THE DEFECT — knip SHADOWS FIVE LIB NAMES (`File`, `Performance`, `Plugin`, `Report`, `caches`), EVERY ONE DOM-ONLY, AND ITS tsconfig SAYS `"lib": ["esnext"]` (2026-08-26).** So its 49 -> 49 across (CHK.49) is a control that we honour `lib`, not evidence that the shadow defect is rare. (CHK.32)'s "a library baseline is a claim about a dependency SET" now has a second axis: it is also a claim about the compilerOptions.
- **`resolveHeritageBaseSymbol`'s Identifier root WAS a raw `globals` consult, so a namespace-qualified `implements`/`extends` target resolved ONLY while the namespace's NAME collided with a lib global (2026-08-26, fixed).** The general trap survives the fix: **a shape that works only for a lib-colliding name is working by accident** — rename the identifier before believing any such repro, which is what separated (CHK.49)'s own regression from `bluebirdStaticThis`'s pre-existing gap.
- **A MERGED `interface`'s SAME-NAMED MEMBER IS ONE `Symbol` CARRYING ONLY THE *LAST* BLOCK'S DECLARATION — `mergeSingleSymbol` ADOPTS — SO `Symbol.declarations` IS NOT A MEMBER'S WHOLE DECLARATION LIST, WHERE tsc's IS (round 928, measured: `both` declared in two `interface Merged` blocks answers `[decl2]`).** Overloads and an accessor pair are fine (one container), so the defect only shows across BLOCKS. Anything needing every declaration of a member must reconstruct it from the OWNER symbol's own `declarations`, each of which is a container to scan (`Checker.typeCaptureMemberDeclarations`); the failure is silent and in the dangerous direction — a rename that looks complete and rewrites one block.
- **TypeScript 7.0 SHIPS AS THE `typescript` PACKAGE ITSELF (`latest = 7.0.2`), NOT AS `@typescript/native-preview` — THAT CHANNEL IS RETIRED AND STOPPED AT `7.0.0-dev.20260707.2`; THE BIN IS NAMED `tsc`, NOT `tsgo` (round 884).** The two bench references therefore share a package NAME (`typescript@6` vs `typescript@7`) and **cannot coexist in one `node_modules`** — `bench.yml` installs them into separate npm roots and resolves whichever bin the spec provided. Switching the column is series-safe: 7.0.2 measures **1,774 ms** against the dev build's 1,788 on the compiler profile with the same 65 diagnostics. Local copy: `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc` (gitignored).
- **ON AN ALL-MODULE PROGRAM THE CHECKER MUTATES *ZERO* BINDER-OWNED `Symbol`s — `--bindMutationCheck` reads `checked 15580, changed 0` on the compiler profile, IN THE SAME RUN as `mergeSingleSymbol: adopts 406, mutates 175` (round 882).** Both hold because those merges land on **LIB** symbols (`bindRealLibs` / `init:mergeLibGlobals`), which are in no `BinderResult` — so `PartitionCheck`'s "a worker must never reuse an already-checked bind" is true of the LIB tables, not of the program's, and **a bind is shareable across workers for an all-module program as-is**. **THE SCOPE IS THE CAVEAT**: the zero holds only because INV.3(d) keeps a module's locals out of `globals`; a program with global SCRIPT files does mutate binder output (the pin's positive control drives exactly that and asserts the arm sees it — round 849, a zero from a blind instrument reads like a real negative). The condition is cheap to test at runtime, so **gate a shared-bind path on the program's SHAPE rather than blocking it on the immutability refactor**. Instrument note: fingerprint identity-keyed on `Symbol`, which is sound because `Symbol` is a plain class and NOT a `data class` (round 471's deep-`hashCode` hazard is about AST nodes).
- **THE PARALLEL PATH IS `wall(N) = 1,447 ms + 7,717/N` WARM, AND THE 1,447 IS A PER-WORKER FIXED COST **SHARING CANNOT REDUCE** — SO `docs/parallel-caching.md` PHASE 1 ("resolve the lib slice once, freeze it, share it read-only") BUYS **ZERO WALL**, however redundant the N copies look (round 880).** The fit is `worker = fixed + r x assigned chars` over the WARM per-worker rows (w0 3,892 ms for 1 file / 3,151 k chars, w1-w3 3,157-3,310 for 25-26 files / 2,275 k): `r` = 0.776 ms/k-char against a sequential 0.565, i.e. **+37% contention**, and `fixed` = 1,447 ms = the re-bind (515) plus ~930 ms of program-wide checker work. N=4 predicts 3,376 against 3,035-3,232 measured; N -> infinity gives a **4.35x ceiling** contention keeps out of reach. **GC and the JIT are both measured and both acquitted**: GC pauses are 734 ms at w1 / 1,338 at w4 and **heap-INSENSITIVE** (`-Xmx4g` 14,896 vs `-Xmx8g` 14,865 ms), and the effect persists WARM, where the JIT ramp is over. Only two things move the floor — making the work partition-scoped, or doing less of it, and the latter is the (WARM.*) single-thread arc, not a concurrency lever.
- **…AND THAT LAW HAS A PRECONDITION IT DOES NOT STATE: "CONCURRENT DUPLICATION IS FREE" ASSUMES *CORES ARE ABUNDANT*, WHICH ON THIS BOX THEY ARE NOT — `--shareBind` (bind ONCE, hand every worker the same `BinderResult`s) measures **-5% WARM at w4**, replicated in two ABBA batches with the rotation reversed, all four `on` runs faster than all four `off` runs (round 883).** Four binder threads run beside four JIT compiler threads on 8 cores, so four simultaneous whole-program binds cost MORE wall than the 515 ms one bind costs, and collapsing them returns the difference — i.e. this is the +37% per-worker contention term being collected, not a bind saving. **The prediction was written into the KDoc BEFORE measuring and said zero; state the prior first, because that is what makes a refutation legible.** Sharing is OPT-IN because soundness is a property of the PROGRAM, not the compiler: safe while nothing merges a program symbol into `globals` (INV.3(d) gives that for an all-module program and not otherwise). Id safety is structural — the shared bind runs on the CALLER thread, so its symbols sit below every worker's rebased slice (>= 1e9). **A shape gate that would make it default must REUSE the checker's own merge predicate rather than re-derive it**; a re-derivation wrong in the permissive direction corrupts silently.
- **HOISTING CONCURRENT DUPLICATED WORK INTO THE SERIAL PREFIX CANNOT WIN, AND USUALLY LOSES — `wall = D + A/N` WHETHER D RUNS IN ONE WORKER OR IN ALL N, SO MOVING IT TO THE PREFIX LEAVES `D + A/N` AT BEST (round 879, which retracted its own queue item on this arithmetic before building it).** Measured: the ~445 tail passes are **4,342 ms** of a sequential compile but only ~1.3-2.7 s of w4 wall, because they are largely partition-scoped ALREADY (~1.1 s duplicated per worker + ~98 ms per ASSIGNED FILE — with the tail off, w0 with 1 file drops 1,194 ms while w1 with 26 drops 3,651), so hoisting them would have added 4.3 s of serial time to save ~1.9 s per worker: **a net ~2.4 s LOSS**. **A count of `binderResults` loops (321 of them) is a fact about the source and says nothing about what removing them returns** — price the prize first (round 758). The PassLab (`build/pass-lab.txt`, `disable <pass>`) answers this class of question with NO recompile.
- **AND VERIFY A `--workers` CLAIM IN *EMIT* MODE, NOT ONLY `--noEmit` — every `--workers` capture taken in this project before round 878 was check-only, which round 738's `skipEmitOutputs` gate makes structurally blind to transform/emit.** The check is one `--outDir` build per arm plus `diff -r` (w1 vs w4: 78 files each, clean).
- **THE WALL OF A PARALLEL PHASE IS THE SLOWEST WORKER, SO A PARTITION'S max/mean IS A *CEILING*, NOT A TAX — AND `checker.ts` IS **31.6% OF THE COMPILER PROFILE**, WHICH IS WHY `i % workers == w` CAPPED `--workers` AT 2.10x / 2.63x / 2.41x AT 4 / 6 / 8 BEFORE ANY CHECKER WORK (round 877).** That is also the whole explanation of "w8 is worse than w4", recorded three times (round 826 cold: 1.242x vs 1.361x) and never explained: at w8 one bucket drew the giant AND its ordinary share. `balancedFilePartition` is longest-processing-time-first and takes the ceiling to **3.16x at every level >= 4** — which is then the REAL ceiling of FILE-level parallelism on this program, since one file being 31.6% of the input means no assignment beats 1/0.316; raising it further needs sub-file parallelism, not a better scheduler. **Its weight is source LENGTH, an explicit proxy for checking cost** (exact, free, and known before any checking — a true cost measure is not); a wrong proxy costs balance, never correctness. **Its order must stay TOTAL (size desc, ties on `fileName`)** or the partition stops being a function of the program and diagnostics start depending on scheduling.
- **EVERY `--workers` NUMBER IN THIS REPO IS *COLD* AND EVERY WARM NUMBER IS *SEQUENTIAL* — THE TWO REGIMES FIRST MET IN ROUND 876, AND THEY DISAGREE: w4 IS **1.531x** WARM AGAINST ROUND 826's **1.361x** COLD.** Measure a parallel change with `BenchMain <proj> <warmup> <iters> <tiers> <emit> workers<N>` (or `WORKERS=N scripts/ab-warm.sh`), never by inferring a warm answer from a cold ladder: cold, worker threads compete for the very cores the JIT ramp is running on, and a cold A/B can show the removal of real work as a wash. **Set the level ONCE per process** — round 867's shared-branch-profile law is sharper for worker levels than for anything it was written about (two levels in one JVM share every compiled method in the binder and the checker), so comparing LEVELS is two invocations of the harness, never two arms of one run.
- **A REDUNDANT COMPUTATION WHOSE RESULT IS *DROPPED* IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY GATE HERE — NO DIAGNOSTIC, NO EMITTED BYTE, NO `cost_gate.py` COUNTER MOVES, ONLY THE WALL (round 876: `cpcBindAndCheck` bound the whole program on the SEQUENTIAL prefix of every `--workers` compile and read the result nowhere — 515 ms warm, ~11% of a parallel rebuild).** Two consequences. **The pin for such a thing must be a COUNT, not a time** (`FrontEnd.sequentialFileBinds`, written only from the caller thread, asserted exactly 0 under workers) — 515 ms sits under the run-to-run spread of a parallel compile, and round 868 already says a timed assertion is a coin flip. **And `+0.00%` from `cost_gate.py` is the EXPECTED answer, not a green light**: the gate exercises the sequential path, where a parallel-only change is a strict no-op — read it as a control that the OTHER path is untouched, and get the actual verdict from `--frontEnd`'s phase rows.
- **A `--workers N` CLAIM NEEDS A DIAGNOSTIC-COUNT DISTRIBUTION OVER >=5 RUNS PER LEVEL — one `--listAll` capture is a coin flip and is how rounds 754 and 824 each shipped a false green.** The detector is `grep -c 'error TS'` over repeated fixed-worker-level runs (~25 s each); `--partitionCheck` is a STATIC partition model and cannot see a race by construction.

- **THE JDK 25 AOT CACHE HAS *NO* INVALIDATION — A STALE ONE SILENTLY RUNS THE PREVIOUS BUILD'S BYTECODE (exit 0, `OK — 0 errors`, where the uncached run gives `NoClassDefFoundError`), SO `scripts/xtsc`'s FAIL-SAFE CHECK IS THE ONLY THING BETWEEN A REBUILT JAR AND A WRONG ANSWER (rounds 828/832).** The cache is passed to the JVM only when a recomputed manifest — sha-256 of every classpath entry IN ORDER, the JDK build, OS/arch, and the cache file's own digest — matches byte for byte; **mtime is deliberately absent from the fingerprint** (round 828's decisive experiment used a FRESH mtime, and a rewrite that preserves one is what a stat check waves through). Do not "simplify" that to a size/mtime compare, do not add an opt-out flag, and do not use `-XX:AOTMode=on` (auto mode is what degrades a bad cache to a normal run). `AotCacheGuardTest` fails on exactly that mistake; contract in `docs/perf/aot-cache.md`.
- **`build/libs/*.jar` IS NOT BYTE-REPRODUCIBLE, SO *ANY* BUILD — INCLUDING A NO-OP ONE AND INCLUDING `./gradlew jvmTest` — INVALIDATES THE AOT CACHE, AND A CACHED ARM MEASURED AFTERWARDS IS SILENTLY UNCACHED (round 842).** `jvmJar --rerun` with zero source change yields a different sha256 AND size (5,639,210 -> 5,639,200 B), and every classpath entry is content-hashed into the fingerprint, so the guard correctly reads `SKIP no-cache-file` — fail-safe, never a wrong answer, but a "cached" arm then runs at plain speed and its ladder still looks plausible (round 842's first `--serve` A/B was an A/A this way). **Train AFTER the last build, and NEVER measure a cached arm without `XTSC_AOT_VERBOSE=1` printing `aot USE …`** — that line is the only thing separating the two.
- **…AND A SECOND BATCH REPLICATES THE *MEASUREMENT* DRAW, NEVER THE *TRAINING* ONE — AN A/B ON AN AOT TRAINING CONFIGURATION NEEDS >=2 INDEPENDENTLY TRAINED CACHES PER ARM (round 842).** Two caches trained by an IDENTICAL `xtsc-aot train` differ by up to **+322 ms (2.4%), 10/12 paired**, and the ordering REPLICATES across batches hours apart, so it is a persistent property of the cache FILE that re-running can never average out; rounds 840(c)/(d) each cited two batches as evidence while re-using the same two cache files. No observable predicts it — five identical-command draws span 212,992 B and the LARGEST cache is second-slowest, which is why 840(d) § 10.3's "+82 KB" reading is struck. Harness: `scripts/aot-draw-variance.sh`.
- **TWO ARMS THAT SHARE ONE COMPILED METHOD ARE NOT INDEPENDENT ARMS — THE ONE THAT RUNS FIRST WRITES THE BRANCH PROFILE THE OTHER IS COMPILED AGAINST, AND A BRANCH PROFILED AS *NEVER TAKEN* COSTS AN UNCOMMON TRAP WHEN THE OTHER ARM TAKES IT (round 867: per-arm sd 16-38%, one process's control arm the batch's cheapest and its treated arm the dearest, by 2x).** It bites any A/B whose two arms are the same code driven by different DATA (a mask, a mode field, a flag), which is exactly how `BenchMain`'s tiers are built. **Give each arm its own JVM** — sd fell to 2-5% with nothing else changed — and remember the first instrumented rebuild in a process is also the one that WARMS a method the uninstrumented loop never touches, so discard a leading throwaway. `scripts/round867-warm-amp2.sh`.
- **`PostCheckerPartitionTest`'s `residue * 4 < post` is TIMING-FLAKY UNDER LOAD and is the one pin in this suite that reddens without a regression** — it bounds a residue over a **272 us** region by four timestamp pairs, and a single scheduler hiccup blows it. Re-run the class alone before believing it (round 867: red in the full suite, green in isolation, from a change it cannot reach).
- **A SIGN-CONSISTENT PAIRED BATCH IS NOT A RESULT — REPLICATE IT IN A SECOND BATCH, BECAUSE A ~5% PER-ARM SPREAD MANUFACTURES 5/5 SWEEPS (round 840(c)).** Batch 1 read an AOT arm as 5/5 wins, median −1,161 ms (−6.6%) on the check-only path, in a rotated interleave with the winner in the *disadvantaged* position 4 times; batch 2 read 3/6 wins and +192 ms, and a third batch 2/4 — the effect was zero. The genuine effect in the same runs (emit, −1,132 ms) replicated in all three batches. Rotation and within-rep pairing control the drift; only replication controls the *draw*, and a second batch of the two arms costs one more run block.
- **A KOTLIN-DAEMON DEATH ANNOUNCES ITSELF AS `Using fallback strategy: Compile without Kotlin daemon` AND THEN BURNS MINUTES FAILING (round 812: 3m56s, inside a 683 MiB Gradle daemon).** The upstream line is `Connection to the Kotlin daemon has been unexpectedly lost` (round 811's `GC overhead limit exceeded` in a second costume). Kill the run when you see the fallback line; recovery is `./gradlew --stop` plus a graceful bracket-pattern `pkill -f 'KotlinCompile[D]aemon'` (never `-9`), then rebuild.
- **A SCRIPTED `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` TRIM THAT SLICES "FROM THE OLDEST `### Round` HEADING TO THE NEXT `### `" DELETES THE WHOLE QUEUE — THE OLDEST LIVE NOTE IS THE **LAST** `###` IN THE FILE AND THE QUEUE BELOW IT CARRIES NO HEADING OF ITS OWN, SO THE SLICE RUNS TO EOF (2026-08-27, 441 KB -> 106 KB, recovered by `git checkout --` because the file was committed).** Bound the slice by the FIRST QUEUE ITEM (the first line matching `^- \[` after the LAST `### Round`) and ASSERT the `- [ ]`/`- [x]` counts are unchanged before writing — **(CHK.70) re-committed this exact mistake (452 KB -> 102 KB) with this entry already in the file, so a length check is not enough; assert the queue itself survives.** Same family as the entry below, and the same reason it was survivable.
- **`open(path, 'w').write(<expr>)` TRUNCATES THE FILE BEFORE `<expr>` IS EVALUATED, SO A RAISING EXPRESSION LEAVES IT AT ZERO BYTES (round 879 emptied `PLAN-PHASE-5.md` this way; recovered by `git checkout --` because it was committed).** Python evaluates the receiver first, and the scripted doc edits in this repo are exactly the shape that bites — a long f-string with an `s.index(...)` inside it. Build the new content into a VARIABLE first, then open and write it; or use the Edit tool, which cannot half-apply. And it is a second argument for the standing rule that a round's docs are committed before the next scripted rewrite touches them.
- **THE SCRATCHPAD DIRECTORY IS SHARED BETWEEN SESSIONS, SO A WAIT LOOP KEYED ON A FILE'S *EXISTENCE* (`until [[ -f out.json ]]`) CAN MATCH A PREVIOUS SESSION'S FILE AND RETURN INSTANTLY WHILE THE REAL JOB IS STILL RUNNING (round 946, ~40 min).** The stale `pristine_sweep.py` output found that way was three rounds old and read as a plausible before-arm (334/776 instead of 297/773), i.e. it failed in the reassuring direction — a stale measurement looks exactly like a fresh one, and single-fixture spot checks on the SAME binary disagreeing with the full run is the only tell. **Delete the output file before launching, or wait on the PROCESS (`tail --pid=<pid> -f /dev/null`), never on a marker that could already exist.**
- **A `run_in_background` GRADLE RUN OUTLIVES THE COMMAND THAT STARTED IT, SO A SECOND ACTOR SEEING "completed" CONCLUDES THE SHELL IS FREE WHILE THE FIRST BUILD IS STILL LIVE — AND THE VICTIM'S SIGNATURE IS `NoSuchFileException: …/binary/in-progress-results-generic.bin` OR `Gradle build daemon disappeared unexpectedly` (2026-08-25, four suite runs lost).** **THAT IS A SECOND AND, ON A BOX WITH FREE RAM, MORE LIKELY CAUSE OF THE SIGNATURE THIS FILE ATTRIBUTES TO THE OOM-KILLER** — check `free -m` FIRST: 12 GB free refutes the memory reading and leaves a concurrent build as the hypothesis. One gradle invocation at a time, per BOX, not per agent.
- **`./gradlew --stop` CAN REACH A *LATER* INVOCATION'S DAEMON (`Gradle build daemon has been stopped: stop command received` mid-build), AND `pkill -f 'GradleDaemon'` KILLS THE INVOKING SHELL exactly as the documented `KotlinCompileDaemon` case does (2026-08-25).** Round 851's "stop before the gate, never after" is not enough — the stop outlives its own command, so never issue one while any build could start; the bracket rule is GENERAL (`'GradleDaemo[n]'`, `'KotlinCompile[D]aemon'`), and the tell for the self-kill is a compound command that dies at the pkill with exit 144 and no output.
- **NEVER write `nohup ./gradlew … &` INSIDE a `run_in_background` Bash call — the double-detach makes the tool report success immediately while the run lives on, and the next command's `rm -rf build/test-results/jvmTest` then deletes the results dir out from under it (round 831, ~40 min).** The victim run dies with `NoSuchFileException: …/binary/in-progress-results-generic.bin` **after its tests have already completed**, so an empty results dir plus a `BUILD FAILED` looks exactly like a build that never ran — while the log in fact carries the full `N tests completed, M failed`. **Read the log before believing an empty results dir**, and pass the command to `run_in_background` bare.
- **…AND A `| tail`/`| grep` ON THE GRADLE RUN DESTROYS THAT LOG IN EXACTLY THE KILLED-RUN CASE THE RULE ABOVE EXISTS FOR (round 840b) — the filter buffers until the pipeline EXITS, so a run that dies mid-flight leaves a ZERO-BYTE log: no `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, no `tests completed`, no `fallback strategy` line, nothing to tell a killed run from one that never started.** Note this file's own "How to run tests" incantation is `… | grep -a "tests completed"` — that form is for a FOREGROUND run you watch; for a backgrounded one redirect the FULL output and grep the FILE afterwards.
- **Do NOT poll for a Gradle run with a `while kill -0 $(pgrep -f "GradleWrapperMain.*jvmTest") ...; do sleep N; done` wait-loop — it never exits.** `pgrep -f` matches against the FULL command line, and the wait-loop's OWN command line literally contains the string `GradleWrapperMain.*jvmTest` (inside the pgrep pattern), so it matches ITSELF → `kill -0 <self-pid>` always succeeds → the loop sleeps forever, orphaned (harmless/idle, but never terminates and clutters the process list; clean up with `kill <pids>`). There is no need to poll at all: launch the suite with the Bash tool's `run_in_background: true` and react to the `<task-notification>` that fires when it completes (the harness re-invokes you). If you must poll for something, pick a pattern that cannot match the poller itself (e.g. a marker file's existence). Two long-lived JVMs (the Gradle daemon + `KotlinCompileDaemon`, ~hours uptime) are NORMAL idle build daemons — leave them; they speed up builds and auto-shut-down after their idle timeout. **The pkill variant is WORSE (round 466): `pkill -9 -f KotlinCompileDaemon` KILLS THE INVOKING SHELL ITSELF (the -f pattern matches the bash -c command line containing the literal), so everything after it in a compound command silently never runs — the tell is a Bash call that "completes" with no output and no side effects. Use a bracket pattern that cannot match itself: `pkill -9 -f 'KotlinCompile[D]aemon'`.**
- **A `when` ARM THAT IS `options.copy(field = …)` ON A WIDE DATA CLASS COSTS ~160 BYTECODES AT ITS CALL SITE, so a dispatch table's size is the ARM COUNT times the DATA CLASS's FIELD COUNT (round 815, `applyDirective`: 85 one-line arms = 13,694 bytecodes, over HotSpot's 8,000 `HugeMethodLimit`).** Kotlin compiles a named-argument `copy` into a `copy$default` call carrying the whole argument vector plus the default bitmasks, so source that reads as one line is not one line of bytecode — **adding directives to `applyDirective` walks it back over the limit at ~160 each** (per-run headroom is in `docs/perf/setup-phase-and-huge-methods.md` § 19). Check with `scripts/huge_methods.py`, never by eye.
- **POST-MODULE-SPLIT, A BARE `./gradlew jvmTest --tests '*Foo*'` FAILS THE BUILD EVEN WHEN THE TEST PASSES — every module runs `jvmTest`, and the four that do not contain `Foo` each die with `No tests found for given includes` (round 886).** The core module compiles and its tests run GREEN in the same invocation, so the `BUILD FAILED` is pure module-routing noise and reads exactly like a real failure. Qualify the task: `./gradlew :xemantic-typescript-compiler-core:jvmTest --tests '*Foo*'`. (The unfiltered whole-suite form is unaffected — that one must stay unqualified to cover all four modules, and its XMLs must be summed with the `*/build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml` glob.)
- `project.exec {}` is not available in Gradle 9 Kotlin DSL task `doLast` blocks — use `ProcessBuilder` directly instead.
- **REGISTER A GRADLE TASK AS `val x = tasks.register("x") { }` (`tasks.register<Sync>("x")` when typed) — THE DELEGATED `val x by tasks.registering { }` IS DEPRECATED IN GRADLE 9.6 AND SCHEDULED FOR REMOVAL IN GRADLE 10, AND THIS BUILD IS KEPT WARNING-CLEAN (2026-08-23, swept: 7 sites over core/daemon/kir).** The delegated form DERIVES the task name from the property name, so a conversion must repeat that name VERBATIM — a task name is a contract for `scripts/*.sh` and the CI workflows, and a typo registers a second task while the old name silently disappears. Drop the `val` where nothing references the provider (`kirNativeCompile`), or Kotlin trades the deprecation for an unused-variable warning. The whole census costs no build: `./gradlew <task> --dry-run --warning-mode all` configures every project and prints each site with its line number.
- **A GENERATED SOURCE DIRECTORY GOES INTO A SOURCE SET AS THE TASK *PROVIDER* — `kotlin.srcDir(generateFoo)`, NEVER `kotlin.srcDir(layout.buildDirectory.dir("generated/foo"))` (2026-08-23).** A bare path leaves Gradle unable to infer the producer, so a hand-written `dependsOn` covers only the tasks it names — and the SOURCES JARS are not compile tasks, so they read an undeclared task output and Gradle 9 FAILS the build. It hides until you publish: `build` is green, `publishToMavenLocal` dies in `jvmSourcesJar` (and then the metadata `sourcesJar`). The provider carries the dependency to every consumer, which makes the compile-task `dependsOn` wiring redundant — and since the generators are registered BELOW the `kotlin { }` block, the `srcDir` call lives next to the task it names, not in `sourceSets { }`.
- **A POSIX `fork`/`exec` SPAWN MUST `dup2` /dev/null ONTO fds 0/1/2 BEFORE `execvp`, OR THE DAEMON HOLDS ITS CLIENT'S STDOUT OPEN AND `xtsc … | tail` NEVER RETURNS (round MOD.5, measured).** The client exits, the compile finishes, and the shell waits forever on a pipe whose write end the detached daemon still owns — `--serve` works, the output is correct, and only running the client THROUGH A PIPE shows it (a unit test on `spawnDetached` passes). The JVM twin gets this free from `Redirect.DISCARD`; the native one is hand-written in `Platform.native.kt` and needs the double fork (`setsid` between them, `waitpid` the intermediate, `_exit` not `exit`) as well.
- **A CLIENT'S FIXED COST IS THE JVM, NOT ITS CLASSPATH — the `-api`-only thin client is 278 ms against the fat dispatcher's 287 ms, and the Kotlin/Native one is 7.0 ms against a 0.9 ms fork+exec floor (round 872).** Class loading is lazy, so the 5.6 MB compiler jar a daemon-served request never touches costs nothing; the module split's value is that it makes a NATIVE binary affordable, never that it shrinks a JVM start. `scripts/xtsc` routes `--daemon` to the native client and falls back to the JVM **on exit code 3 only** — that code means "the request never ran", so falling back on any other non-zero code compiles and reports twice.
- **…AND SWAPPING A CLIENT ARM CHANGES SEMANTICS UNLESS YOU STOP IT: the thin client STARTS a daemon when it finds none, so the launcher must pass `--no-spawn` or `--daemon` quietly replaces an in-process compile with a long-lived JVM nobody asked for — measured, it spawned one from inside the 14,000-test suite and left it running (round 872).** `AotCacheGuardTest`'s dispatcher case is what caught it; a client arm added later needs the same guard.
- **A MODULE SYMBOL HAS NO TYPE IN THIS CHECKER (`getTypeOfSymbolWorker` HAS NO `SymbolFlags.Module` ARM), AND GIVING IT ONE IS BLOCKED BY THE STATIC SIDE OF A CLASS — NOT BY ALIAS RESOLUTION (2026-08-29, (CHK.73), measured).** `import * as fs from "fs"` types as `any` for three independent reasons: the `ImportDeclaration` arm of `resolveAlias` lacks (CHK.30)'s mandatory `resolveImportTargetFallback` leg, there is no Module arm, and an AMBIENT module (`declare module "fs"`, which is all of `@types/node`) resolves to no FILE — `import x = require(…)` already takes a `globals[specifier]` second chance and `import * as` does not. Fixing all three makes the binding and its members type exactly as tsgo, and still costs **4 corpus baselines in MEANING**: a module object exposes an exported class as its CONSTRUCTOR, and this checker types a class value as its INSTANCE type, so a ctor-less class has no construct signature to match `new () => Model`. **A general Module arm costs 21** (the internal-module family). Do not re-attempt without the static side; and note the round-409 TS2315 flood is NOT the hazard here, since an ambient module is not a relative barrel.
- **THE BENCH PROFILE'S `node_modules/@types` IS EMPTY — CLAUDE.md's "only `build/bench/tsc-project-*` carries the real `@types/node`" IS STALE (2026-08-29).** Anything measuring a `types: ["node"]` shape must fetch it: `PATH=tools/node/bin:$PATH npm install --no-save @types/node@20` in a scratch project (network works; `tools/node/bin/npm` needs `node` ON THE PATH, since its shebang is `/usr/bin/env node`). The tell that you have no types is `unresolved imports: N (e.g. 'fs')` plus a TS2688, which reads exactly like the defect under investigation.
- **ALL EIGHT DASHBOARD PROFILES ARE ONE CODEBASE WITH AN ATYPICAL *SHAPE* — tsc's OWN SOURCES, **78 FILES AVERAGING 128 KB** — SO A COST THAT IS PER-**FILE** RATHER THAN PER-**BYTE** IS STRUCTURALLY INEXPRESSIBLE ON EVERY ONE OF THEM (2026-08-30, (INC.57): a QUADRATIC front-end row survived ~950 rounds).** `extractRelativeImports` rebuilt a set of every program file name on entry and is called twice per file: `2 x 78^2` is twelve thousand probes and vanishes, where the same code on an application-shaped project (thousands of ~1 KB files) read **18.9 / 76.3 / 331.6 ms at 601 / 1201 / 2401 files — 4x the cost for 2x the files**, 20% of the incremental floor. **THREE INDEPENDENT QUADRATICS WERE FOUND THIS WAY IN ONE SESSION, IN THREE SUBSYSTEMS, TAKING A 2,401-FILE PROJECT'S PER-KEYSTROKE FLOOR 1,653 -> 279 ms**: the emit-order import scan ((INC.57)), `checkJsxImportResolutions` ((INC.58)) — **709.7 ms of a 774.7 ms floor pass table (92%) on a project with NO JSX in it**, where (INC.54)(a) had ranked it at **1.2 ms** from the tsc profile, i.e. **600x** — and `parsedSourceFiles.filter { it.key !in transformOrder.toSet() }` ((INC.59)), whose `.toSet()` sat INSIDE the lambda and which runs in the **`--noEmit`** path, i.e. 175 ms per keystroke preparing an emit order that is never used. **THE SECOND AND THIRD WERE FOUND BY RE-READING THE FLOOR AFTER THE ROUND BEFORE THEM, NOT FROM THE QUEUE: the ranking changed three times in one session and each new top row was one no queue item had named**, so re-decompose after every round that moves the floor rather than inheriting an ordering. **So the law bites RANKINGS, not just prices: any per-pass ordering taken on a dashboard profile must be re-taken on the many-small shape before one of its rows is opened.** **Before pricing ANY front-end or floor row, ask whether the profile's shape can express the cost at all**; generate the other shape with `scripts/gen-many-small-project.py` and read it with `scripts/floor-decomposition.sh` — the instrument that finds this class is to **divide the floor pass table by FILE COUNT at two program sizes** (an honestly O(program) pass reads a constant µs/file; a quadratic one doubles). Same law as (INC.9)'s "a cost prior does not transfer across REGIMES", on a new axis. Both fixes together took that project's per-keystroke floor **1,653 -> 366 ms**.
- **THE INTELLIJ HOST RUNS *ONE COMPILER THREAD PER `tsconfig.json`* IN ONE JVM, SO EVERY PROCESS-GLOBAL CACHE HERE IS REACHED CONCURRENTLY — A SHAPE NO FIXTURE, PROFILE OR CORPUS BASELINE PRODUCES (2026-08-31, (INC.67), found by reading `xemantic/xtsc-intellij-plugin`).** Its `XtscService` keeps a `ConcurrentHashMap` of `XtscSession`s, each with its own single-thread executor. `RealLibSnapshots.parseCache`/`declIndexCache` and `RealLibResolver.resolutionCache` therefore publish **copy-on-write behind `@Volatile`** — a reader sees the old complete map or the new complete one — and a NEW process-global cache must do the same; `prewarmParsedLibFiles` is NOT the mitigation (it covers `--workers` inside ONE compile and says nothing about two sessions). A lost race costs a RECOMPUTATION, which was always possible (`getOrPut` on a `HashMap` is not atomic either) and is benign **for the mirror of round 471's reason**: the identity sets these feed compare `Node`s STRUCTURALLY, so two parses of the same lib text are interchangeable to every consumer. A per-INSTANCE cache (`ModuleResolver`'s) needs none of this.
- **A *BLOCKED* PAIRED RUN MANUFACTURES A REPRODUCIBLE DELTA IN A REGION WITH NO CAUSAL PATH TO THE CHANGE, AND A LARGER PER-ARM DRAW COUNT DOES NOT FIX IT — 12 DRAWS PER ARM READ **+2.70 ms** ON `include/exclude regex match` (which calls no `PathUtil.normalize`) AND **+3.39** ON ITS CONTAINING ROW, BOTH OF WHICH *INVERTED* TO -0.24 / -4.51 UNDER ABBA ROTATION (2026-08-31, (INC.68)).** Three `before` processes run before three `after` ones is enough: the box drifts, and the drift lands entirely on whichever arm went first. **The tell is a stable delta in a region you cannot connect to the edit** — treat that as evidence the ORDER is the variable, never as a mechanism to hunt. Rotate arms across PROCESSES (round 867: each arm needs its own JVM) even when both arms are separate class directories rather than two flags.
- **`PathUtil.normalize` IS A FIXED-POINT FUNCTION FOR ~80% OF WHAT THIS COMPILER ASKS IT — 9,584 OF 11,935 CALLS PER FLOOR BUILD — SO ITS FAST PATH IS LOAD-BEARING AND ITS PREDICATE (`PathUtil.isNormalized`) MUST STAY CONSERVATIVE IN ONE DIRECTION ONLY (2026-08-31, (INC.68)).** A false NEGATIVE costs the old allocating path; a false POSITIVE returns an un-normalized path, which resolves to a DIFFERENT FILE and per (CFG.1) is invisible to every diagnostic channel here. **The corpus cannot see this at all** (it materialises no directory), so the instruments are the 8-profile grid, `output.programFiles` in `cost_gate.py`, and `PathNormalizeFastPathTest`'s value pins over the ACCEPTED population against a transcribed reference — a second implementation, because a differential whose two arms are one function cannot see a fast path.
- **A PIN'S *ENVIRONMENT* IS PART OF ITS SPECIFICATION, AND TWO WAYS OF FORGETTING THAT BOTH BIT IN ONE ROUND (2026-08-31, (INC.67)).** A pin that compares two successive reads of a process-global by IDENTITY assumes a QUIESCENT process — the suite publishes into these maps from elsewhere, so it passed in isolation and failed in the full run; state the property that survives concurrency instead (a map already handed out never grows). And CLAUDE.md's "never put a `SourceFile` subexpression in a power-assert assertion" is not theoretical: a cache map holding `SourceFile`s inside `assert(...)` turned the failure into an **`OutOfMemoryError` in the diagram builder**, masking the real cause. Compute booleans into locals first.
- **A ROTATED BATCH OF A *COMPOSITE* QUANTITY STILL CANNOT SEPARATE TWO OF ITS TERMS — THE INCREMENTAL FLOOR WALL CARRIES A ±20 ms CONCURRENT TERM, SO IT CANNOT RESOLVE A 7 ms SEQUENTIAL CHANGE HOWEVER MANY DRAWS IT IS GIVEN (2026-08-31, (INC.72), which RETRACTS two wall figures from its own session).** Two rounds read the rotated floor wall at ~3x what their pass row explained and the gap was queued as a mechanism; the per-PHASE instrument over the SAME two binaries then attributed **init-block pass dispatch -14.81 ms** — exactly what the rows said — while the UNTOUCHED crawl swung **+18.01** in the same run (its `read+decode` sum, elapsed-with-suspension across `Dispatchers.Default` workers, moved 147.8 -> 249.9). **For a checker-side floor change the receipt is `FrontEnd`'s phase row plus the deterministic population count; the floor wall is a sanity check.** (INC.68)'s law is the step before this one: a BLOCKED batch invents a delta and rotation removes it; rotation does NOT remove a real, large, unrelated phase. `FloorAbMain`'s `fe` mode is the two-binary phase decomposition (`scripts/inc69-floor-ab.sh` runs the wall arms).

- **`FrontEnd.READ` PRICES THE CRAWL'S *THREAD HANDOFF*, NOT THE READ — IT BRACKETS A `withContext`, SO IT IS ELAPSED-WITH-SUSPENSION, AND SERVING **33,350 READS FROM MEMORY** MOVED THE CRAWL'S WALL BY **NOTHING** ON A 2,401-FILE PROJECT WHILE HALVING IT ON tsc's 78 HUGE SOURCES (2026-08-31, (INC.56)).** The read is a **BYTE** cost (10 MB of UTF-8 decode is real; 2,401 reads of ~1 KB over 16 workers are ~1-2 ms), and the row's per-FILE call count is what makes it read as a per-file one. So a crawl candidate priced off that row is priced off a LOCATION: the fix that pays on BOTH shapes removes the HANDOFF (`Vfs.readTextIfResident`, answered for content already in memory), not the read. (INC.64)'s law one hop later.
- **`Vfs.retainRead` IS CALLED FROM THE CRAWL'S SINGLE-THREADED FOLD AND `readText`/`readTextIfResident` MUST STAY READ-ONLY — populating a retention map from `readText` is round 825's data race, because the crawl reads from N concurrent workers (2026-08-31, (INC.56)).** Same discipline and same fold as `CrawlParseCache.store`; the natural design (retain what you just read, where you read it) is the wrong one. `OverlayVfs`'s `retained` and `contents` are additionally DISJOINT by construction (`put` drops the retained entry, `retainRead` refuses an overlaid path), which makes the lookup ORDER between them a measured REDUNDANT guard — round 927's pair, recorded rather than claimed.
- **A PASS ROW IS NOT A GATEABLE POOL: (INC.74)'s ~10 ms OF "WHOLE-PROGRAM WALKERS" IS **~1.2 ms** ONCE READ — TWO ARE ALREADY PARTITION-SCOPED AND WHAT REMAINS IN THEIR ROW IS THE COLLECTOR ROUND 609 FORBIDS GATING, ONE IS PURE COLLECTOR, AND THREE WERE REFUSED BY EARLIER ROUNDS (2026-08-31, (INC.77)).** An (INC.20) MIXED pass keeps its `binderResults` collector loop and shows its FULL cost in the row, so ranking gating candidates off `--passTiming` over-counts the pool by ~8x. **And narrowing the wrong loop is silent: `checkCrossFileUseBeforeDeclaration`'s `fileIdx` comes from `binderResults.withIndex()` and is compared against the indices its COLLECTOR recorded, so a mechanical rewrite to `checkedResults.withIndex()` renumbers it and makes a use-before-declaration verdict a function of the PARTITION.** Read what an index MEANS before narrowing the loop that produces it — the sibling of CLAUDE.md's "a `for (result in binderResults)` loop is not evidence of a tail walker". **RESOLVED 2026-09-01 ((INC.89)(b)), AND THE FIX IS THE GENERAL RULE: WHEN A PASS'S VERDICT COMPARES ORDINALS OF ITS OWN ENUMERATION, THE EMITTER IS NARROWED BY A `continue` *AFTER* `binderResults.withIndex()` — NEVER BY CHANGING THE LOOP HEAD**, because filtering after the enumeration cannot renumber while re-heading always does (ablated: re-heading invents a TS2448 no unpartitioned build produces, and only ONE of seven pins can see it). What licenses the narrowing at all is that the row is anchored at the USE file and non-null, so `getDiagnostics()` was already discarding it — check both of those before narrowing any emitter, since a `fileName = null` row SURVIVES the partition filter.
- **THE CRAWL'S FAST DRAIN SERVES A FILE FROM THE PARSE CACHE, SO ITS GATE MUST STAY THE CONTENT-AND-FLAGS `lookup` — A PATH-KEYED `peek` SERVES THE **PREVIOUS KEYSTROKE'S TREE** AND NO COUNTER, ORDER PIN OR CORPUS BASELINE NOTICES (2026-09-01, (INC.85)).** The one instrument that sees it is a fixture with an UNSAVED BUFFER — resident, carrying NEW bytes, over a stale cached tree — and the obvious fixture does not have that shape: an `edit()` that drops retention makes the file non-resident, the fast drain is never entered, and the staleness arm reads 0 RED as a DEAD arm. `CrawlAdaptiveDrainTest`; `Vfs.hasResidentContent()` (defaulted `false`, so an un-opted-in `Vfs` takes the old path and probes nothing) gates the classification, and `OverlayVfs` answers it from `retained` and NOT from `contents` — O(open editors) against a wave's O(program) can never pay, which its KDoc records so it is not "fixed" back.
- **THE WHOLE-PROGRAM REGEX LAW EXTENDS FROM SOURCE *TEXT* TO FILE *NAMES*, AND `docs/perf/whole-program-regex-census.md` CENSUSES ONLY THE TEXT SCANNERS — SO A NAME-SCANNING REGEX IS OUTSIDE IT AND UNCENSUSED: `checkMissingTypesReferenceExports`' `(?:^|/)node_modules/…` IS ALTERNATION-ROOTED, CARRIES NO BOYER-MOORE LITERAL, AND WAS ATTEMPTED AT EVERY POSITION OF ALL 2,401 FILE NAMES FOR **3.30 ms OF A 4.51 ms `FrontEnd.POST_DIAGS`** ON A PROJECT WITH NO `node_modules` AT ALL (2026-09-01, (INC.87)(a)).** The fix is the usual one — an EXACT literal pre-gate (`endsWith("/package.json")`, implied by the pattern's own `$`-anchored tail) with the regex kept LIVE as the decider — and it generalises: hoist any `Regex` out of a per-compile body, where it is recompiled every call. **AND A `package.json` IS NEVER A PROGRAM INPUT UNDER `ProjectCompiler`** (its own comment, `ProjectCompiler.kt:353`), so any pin for a mechanism reading one out of `parsed.files` must use the multi-file `@Filename` harness — through `ProjectCompiler` it reads an empty diagnostic list and, written as an ABSENCE assertion, is green forever.
- **A `Vfs` WRAPPER THAT DOES NOT OVERRIDE `listEntries` INHERITS THE INTERFACE DEFAULT — `list(path).map { VfsEntry(it, isDirectory(it)) }` — AND SO HANDS (INC.60)'s WHOLE SAVING BACK: `OverlayVfs` DID EXACTLY THAT ON THE SHIPPED PATH OF EVERY `Project` BUILD, AT **19.54 ms AGAINST 6.34** OVER 50 DIRECTORIES / 2,451 ENTRIES (2026-08-31, (INC.76)).** CLAUDE.md already said this about a COUNTING Vfs in a test; it is the same trap in production and it is SILENT, because the two paths agree on every answer and differ only in syscalls (`SystemVfs.isDirectory` is kotlinx-io's `metadataOrNull`, up to five `stat`s). **Any new wrapping `Vfs` must override `listEntries` or use `Vfs by delegate`** — and the general law: a defaulted interface member added for SPEED is a silent regression waiting for the next wrapper. The instrument that finds it is a row measured STANDALONE against the same row measured IN THE BUILD, with the gap treated as a fact to explain (`GlobListProbeMain`); the pin is a DIFFERENTIAL against the default body, since a wrong entry KIND drops a file from the program or adopts a directory as a root and (CFG.1) says nothing here notices. Note `isDirectoryCalls == 0` after a build is FALSE and correctly so — a build asks about specific PATHS — so that cost pin is a complexity claim at two program sizes.
- **A ROW WHOSE KEY IS AN AST *DATA CLASS* IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY ROUND 471 — `enclosingImportIndex`'s STRUCTURAL `hashCode` IS **76.5 ns, 0.72 ms, 14%** OF ITS 4.7 ms ROW, WHILE **~3.4 ms IS THE INSERT PLUS A `MutableList` AND A `Pair` ALLOCATED PER KEY** — because the census says every `getOrPut` MISSES (9,401 specifiers, 9,401 distinct keys) (2026-08-31, (INC.81)).** Price the hash in ONE timestamp pair over a second pass of the same population before touching a key whose structural semantics are load-bearing. **And `multiFileKeys=0` — not one key reached from two files — is the statement that NO REAL PROJECT EXERCISES THE MULTI-ENTRY PATH**, so a one-entry representation needs a fixture to pin its promotion: `ImportSpecifier`'s data-class components include `pos`/`end`, so two BYTE-IDENTICAL importers spell one key. Same law as (INC.57): a row no queue item names is found by re-taking the ranking, not by reading the queue.
- **A TWO-DRAW READ OF AN INSTRUMENTED ROW CANNOT SEE A **23%** CHANGE IN THAT VERY ROW, AND IT FAILS IN THE *REFUTING* DIRECTION — (INC.80) read 6.26/7.22 before against 6.22/7.45 after and was being written up as refuted when six draws per arm ROTATED ACROSS PROCESSES read **6.41 -> 4.95 ms, 3/3 batches, both rotation directions** (2026-08-31).** (INC.68)'s law is usually quoted about a blocked batch INVENTING a delta; it hides a real one just as easily. Two class dirs differing in one file plus an ABBA loop is the instrument, and it costs one extra build. **And the tempting explanation for "the microbenchmark says 6x and the build says nothing" — that the allocating arm pays GC the build's population never pays (round 801) — was MEASURED AND REFUTED here: a 2 GB young gen made BOTH arms slower.** Corollary for `PathUtil.join`: a module specifier's `..` is exactly what (INC.68)'s `isNormalized` must refuse, so a fast path for the already-normalized case never covered the crawl at all.
- **A PER-COMPONENT REFUSAL CAN BE RIGHT ABOUT ITS COMPONENT AND WRONG ABOUT THE PROGRAM — (INC.73)(a) CALLED THE CRAWL'S `exists` PROBES IRREDUCIBLE ("one per distinct resolution") AND **THE ROOT-FILE GLOB HAD ALREADY LISTED EVERY ONE OF THOSE FILES, OFF THE SAME `Vfs`, ~20 ms EARLIER IN THE SAME BUILD**: 2,351 syscalls -> 0 (2026-08-31, (INC.79)).** Before inheriting a floor refusal, ask WHO ELSE IN THE BUILD ALREADY KNOWS THE ANSWER. `ModuleResolver` now memoizes `exists`/`isDirectory` per build (no new assumption — (INC.65)'s answer memo is strictly stronger and has the same one-build lifetime) and is seeded from the glob; **the seed may only say YES**, because a file can exist and be EXCLUDED from the program, and a seed read as an authoritative file list resolves a real import to nothing. **And keying such a memo by anything less than the WHOLE path is a wrong PROGRAM that every count pin passes** — the basename-keyed ablation reddened only the counters until a pin asking for a file that exists in ONE of two directories was added.
- **AN *ACCEPTING* REGEX CANNOT BE SHORT-CIRCUITED BY A REFUSAL FILTER, SO A PER-ITEM REGEX ROW DOMINATED BY THE *MATCHING* PATTERN NEEDS AN EXACT SHORTCUT AND NOT A PRE-GATE — THE ROOT-FILE GLOB'S EXCLUDE HALF WAS **191 ns/candidate** AND ITS INCLUDE HALF **2,239**, AND THE QUEUE'S OWN "refuse most candidates cheaply first" WAS AIMED AT THE HALF THAT WAS ALREADY CHEAP (2026-08-31, (INC.78)).** `GlobMatcher` answers the `<literal>` + `**` segment + bare `*` leaf + literal tail shape from the head and the tail, keeping the regex as the ORACLE (round 792's shape) — and the equivalence is exact ONLY for a boundary-aligned head and a path with no doubled separator, because `(?:[^/]+/)*[^/]*` matches every remainder EXCEPT one with an EMPTY SEGMENT; both cases fall back to the regex rather than to a second rule guessing at them. **Gate it with a DIFFERENTIAL against that regex, never a green suite** — (CFG.1): a wrong root-file set has no diagnostic channel here. **And the perf receipt is a COUNT (`FrontEnd.globRegexEvals`, 4,802 -> 0), because the same one-process wall ratio read 12x / 5x / 3x over four processes of one binary.**
- **A PIN ABOUT A FILE CHANGING ON DISK CANNOT GO THROUGH `Project.diagnostics()` — (INC.46) NARROWS THE CHECK TO THE FILES THE HOST *REPORTED* AS DIRTY AND REUSES THE PREVIOUS ROWS FOR THE REST, SO AN UNREPORTED CHANGE IS INVISIBLE THROUGH IT WHATEVER THE `Vfs` DID (2026-08-31, (INC.56), two pins re-written after their first run).** It fails as a defect in whichever arm ran first, which is the confusing direction. Force the whole-program build (`Project.files`) so the only variable left is the read.

- **A FAMILY CAN HAVE *ZERO* CORPUS COVERAGE AND STILL BE LOAD-BEARING — `moduleInterfaceNames` / `multiFileModuleTypeNames` (rounds 471/513) ABLATE TO **0 RED** OVER THE ~13k BASELINES AND REDDEN **3 OF THE 8 PROFILES** (+2 rows each: harness, server, services) (2026-08-31, (INC.73)).** That is where their evidence came from in the first place — round 471's was the SERVICES self-compile (an un-memoized `isLibPhantomMemberOfModuleInterface` doubled it, 39 s -> 77 s) and round 513's is tsc's private-codefix `Info` shape, a name declared as an interface in two module files. **So a green corpus under an ablation is not "this is dead"; ablate AND grid before concluding anything about a mechanism's coverage**, and expect the answer to be somewhere for a reason its own round note usually records.

- **`SystemVfs.exists` IS *ONE* SYSCALL — 1.02x `java.io.File.exists`, MEASURED — SO (INC.60)'s FIVE-STAT FINDING IS SPECIFIC TO `metadataOrNull` AND DOES NOT GENERALISE (2026-08-31, (INC.73); `ExistsProbeMain` re-takes it).** And `ModuleResolver`'s probe ladder is already optimal for the ordinary shape: **2,351 `exists` + 10 `isDirectory` per build for 2,351 distinct resolutions**, i.e. exactly one probe each, because `.ts` is first in `allExtensions`. There is no syscall lever in the crawl's specifier-resolution row; what is unexamined there is the non-syscall remainder.

- **A PASS IS NOT DEFERRABLE BECAUSE ITS PRODUCT'S READERS ARE ALL "CHECKING" — ASK WHEN THOSE READERS *RUN* (2026-08-31, (INC.73)(b)).** `init:collectUmdGlobalsAndModuleFiles` and `init:mergeFileLocalsIntoGlobals` look exactly like (INC.70)/(INC.71)'s shape and are not: `umdGlobalNames` is read by `moduleLocalContributesGlobally`, which the MERGE itself calls, and `moduleFiles` by `collectModuleAugmentations`, which `init:mergeModuleAugmentations` dispatches — both LATER INIT PASSES that run unconditionally, so a floor build forces the index whatever the checking path does. The census that decides a deferral must be taken at the readers, and a reader inside `init` answers on every build.

- **FOR ANYTHING IN THE INV.3 VISIBILITY MODEL THE `-project` PINS GATE THE *REGIME* AND THE CORPUS GATES THE *ANSWER* — TWO ROUNDS RUNNING A HAND-WRITTEN VALUE PIN COULD NOT DISCRIMINATE THE MECHANISM WHILE AN ABLATION REDDENED THE CORPUS IN THE HUNDREDS (2026-08-31, (INC.70) 503 tests, (INC.71) 492).** Both fixtures asserted the thing the mechanism is *for* — a module file's local not leaking into another file — and both stayed GREEN with the mechanism disabled, because on a hand-written shape the name is not in the merged `globals` either, so the set has nothing to add. **Write the `-project` pin as a COUNT** (`EagerIndexCensus.perFileScopeBuilds` / `perFileVisibilityBuilds`: 0 on a floor build, 1 or `binderResults.size` on a checking one) **and take the value receipt by ABLATING against the corpus**, which is a measurement and costs one suite run.

- **A DEFERRAL THAT CHANGES *WHEN* A PROBE'S INPUT IS BUILT MUST KEEP THE PROBE'S INSTALL WHERE IT WAS AND FORCE FROM INSIDE IT, OR `globals.lookups` MOVES (2026-08-31, (INC.71)).** `InstrumentedSymbolTable.onLookup` counts NOTHING while it is null, so installing the INV.3(a) classifier lazily would have silently dropped every lookup before the first ask — a `cost_gate.py` counter, not a diagnostic. Installed at the pass's own moment with `ensurePerFileVisibility()` as its first statement, the counters read +0.00%. **The stated cost of that choice: under FULL `--passTiming` the work lands on whichever pass performs the first `globals` lookup rather than on its own row** (at the `rows` tier `globals` is not instrumented, so the floor table is honest).

- **THE EXACTNESS OF A DEFERRED PER-FILE TABLE IS AN *INIT-ORDER* FACT, AND `buildPerFileScopes`' SNAPSHOT EXISTED PRECISELY TO SURVIVE A LATER MUTATION — THE ONE WRITER OF A `BinderResult.locals` IS `collectModuleAugmentations`, DISPATCHED BY `init:mergeModuleAugmentations`, WHICH RUNS EARLIER (2026-08-31, (INC.70)).** So the eager and lazy snapshots are the same table; **a writer scheduled AFTER that pass would make them disagree silently, and nothing here would print it.** Per-file scopes are now built on first ask (`buildPerFileScopeFor`, pinned by `EagerIndexCensus.perFileScopeBuilds` — 0 on a floor build, `binderResults.size` on a full one). Two corollaries measured in the same round: **`perFileScopeOf`'s one-entry IDENTITY memo absorbs every repeated ask for one file, so the `perFileScope` MAP's memoization is pinned by nothing** (an arm that rebuilds per ask reads 0 RED even on a fixture with several unresolved names in one file); and **a value pin on module-local visibility does NOT discriminate `perFileScope` at all** — `moduleOnlyGlobalNames` decides that upstream — so the receipt that the table is load-bearing is the CORPUS, where never building one reddens **503** tests.

- **A CROSS-BINARY A/B RUNNER MAY READ NO COUNTER THAT DOES NOT EXIST IN *BOTH* ARMS — the two arms are loaded by the SAME runner class, so the older one dies with `NoSuchMethodError` and the batch prints ONE arm's medians as if they were both, which reads exactly like a successful run (2026-08-31, (INC.70)).** Populations belong in the pins, which run against one binary at a time; `scripts/inc69-floor-ab.sh` + `FloorAbMain` are the floor-only ABBA harness and deliberately read none.

- **A *PLATEAU* OF NEAR-IDENTICAL PASS PRICES IS A SHARED PER-FILE COST, NOT A COINCIDENCE OF WHAT THE PASSES DO — AND A TOP-N RANKING CANNOT SHOW A PLATEAU, ONLY A DISTRIBUTION CAN (2026-08-31, (INC.69)).** The init-block dispatch had been recorded as "FLAT across ~400 passes, so there is no row to make cheaper" ((INC.66)); the HISTOGRAM says **418 rows, 44 carrying 94% and 367 carrying 0.82 ms**, with 21 of the 44 at an identical 0.39-0.55 ms — 21 corpus PIN walkers sharing one line, a whole-program loop whose first act is `fileName.substringAfterLast('/') != "<one literal>"`. `Checker.filesNamed` is now the one basename index; **the redundant `!=` guard stays VERBATIM in every body** (a round-927 pair: the index buys the speed, the guard keeps the correctness — an ablation widening the index alone reddens nothing). **The corpus CANNOT gate this class**: its harness materialises no directory, so its file names are FLAT and `substringAfterLast('/')` returns the whole string, so an index keyed by the full path passes all ~13k baselines and silently stops pinning a real project's `src/dates/temporal.ts` — a MISSING diagnostic, which nothing in this repo prints. Any future file-name-keyed lookup needs a NESTED-path pin.

- **BEFORE PRICING A ROW, CHECK IT *HAS* A SPLIT — (INC.65)'s ENTIRE FINDING LIVED IN A RESIDUE NO SUB-ROW NAMED, AND THE ROUND BEFORE IT HAD JUST DECLARED THE FRONT END DONE (2026-08-30).** `FrontEnd.CRAWL` decomposed into `read+decode` and `pre-parse`, BOTH elapsed-with-suspension (i.e. locations, not prices), so the gap between them and the WALL was unattributed — and it was **half the row**: bracketing the crawl's sequential half (`FrontEnd.CRAWL_RESOLVE`) read **20.6-28.6 ms of a 44-60 ms wall** at 2,401 files. That is (INC.53)'s "ask what runs OUTSIDE a pass" with a sub-row-shaped twin, and the third time in this arc that ADDING an instrument — not reading one — is what found the cost. Corollary for any "the era of finding a stray quadratic is over" claim: it is a statement about the instruments, not the code.
- **MODULE RESOLUTION IS A PURE FUNCTION OF `(importerDir, specifier)` — `ModuleResolver.resolve` READS `importerPath` ONCE, FOR ITS `dirname`, AND NEVER AGAIN — SO ITS PER-BUILD MEMO'S KEY IS EXACT AND NOT A HEURISTIC (2026-08-30, (INC.65)).** Duplication on an application-shaped project is real (**4,701 resolutions over 2,351 distinct pairs**, factor 2.0; barrels raise it). Three things a future edit must keep: the memo is per `ModuleResolver`, i.e. per `ProjectCompiler.build`, which is what makes an ADDED file visible next build ((INC.48)) and is why it must NEVER become process-global; `null` is a real answer and must be memoized or every unresolved specifier re-probes the filesystem; and **DROPPING THE DIRECTORY FROM THE KEY IS SILENT AND CATASTROPHIC** — it resolves `./dep` in one directory to another's file, a wrong PROGRAM that (CFG.1) says this repo has no diagnostic channel to notice, so the pin is a VALUE one over two directories, never a count.
- **A CONCURRENCY SHAPE DESIGNED FOR THE *COLD* PATH IS PURE OVERHEAD ON THE WARM ONE, AND THE CRAWL PAID **TWO DISPATCHER HOPS PER FILE** TO SCHEDULE A ~1 us MAP PROBE (2026-08-30, (INC.64)).** `readAndScanBatch` hopped to `Dispatchers.Default` for EVERY file so a parse would never run on an IO thread — but on a warm incremental build every parse is a `CrawlParseCache` HIT. Measured over 2,401 files: sequential read **14.4 ms**, `flatMapMerge(16)` alone 17.2, one hop 18.5, the shipped two hops **32.1**, against a crawl WALL of 51-57 and 13-21 ms of actual reading. **The floor's `read+decode`/`pre-parse` CPU sums are elapsed-WITH-SUSPENSION and are LOCATIONS, not prices** — only the WALL is a price, and here it could not resolve the fix (overlapping ranges, and that run's own `full` median 9% slower), so the claim rests on the mechanism plus a synthetic ABBA arm and the pin is a COUNT (`CrawlParseCache.parseDispatches`, at two program sizes). Any new counter folded from those workers goes in the SINGLE-THREADED fold beside `hits`/`misses` — a `++` from the flow is round 825's race.
- **THE EMIT-ORDER REGION RUNS IN THE `--noEmit` PATH, AND THE CORPUS IS A *CONTROL* FOR ANYTHING GATED ON `skipEmitOutputs` — ALL ~13k BASELINES RUN WITH THE BRANCH TAKEN, BECAUSE ONLY `ProjectCompiler` SETS THAT FLAG (2026-08-30, (INC.64)(b), (INC.59)'s finding one call deeper).** `extractRelativeImports` ran twice per file to build a dependency ORDER whose every consumer emits — 15-23 ms of a ~170 ms incremental floor. What GATES such a change is the 8-profile `--noEmit` grid plus a `-project` pin, with an `--outDir` `diff -r` as the separate control that the emitting path is inert. **Two traps: the obvious edit is a `continue`, and `tsFileNames.add` sits BELOW the extraction where every later phase reads it; and a pin over an ORDER needs a fixture whose expected order differs from every order the system produces BY ACCIDENT** — named the obvious way round (`dep` imported by `main`) dependency order equals ALPHABETICAL order, and the value pin stayed green against a binary whose dependency edges had been emptied (`zdep`/`amain` is what discriminates).
- **A REFUSAL THAT NAMES A BLOCKER IS ONLY AS GOOD AS ITS SPLIT, AND (INC.54)(c) REFUSED A 46-50 ms ROW FOR SEVEN ROUNDS ON A MECHANISM THAT OWNS **3%** OF IT (2026-08-30, (INC.63)).** `parseBuiltinLib` was "BLOCKED on round 884's `mergedSymbols` clone-on-write" — true of the BIND, which measures **1.4 ms**; the other 97% was `RealLibResolver.resolve` (a `/// <reference lib=…/>` regex over ~3.7 MB of lib text, called **TWICE** per checker construction) and the decl-set walk (~30k puts into containers keyed by **data-class AST nodes**, i.e. round 471's deep `hashCode`). The recorded split mis-attributed the resolve because it sits INSIDE the `bindLibFiles` section, so `bindLibFiles` 17.4 -> 1.4 ms is that regex and not a bind. **Before inheriting any refusal here, re-derive its split and divide each part by its own population** — ~500 ns per `HashMap` put with a `String` value is 20-40x impossible and names the mechanism with no build. Both fixed halves are pure functions of the SHARED parses; `RealLibSnapshots` is their one home, and the walk is kept VERBATIM there because a `Set<Node>` dedupes STRUCTURALLY (a different container silently changes which declarations the sets hold).
- **AND THAT ROW IS `O(1)` IN PROGRAM SIZE, WHICH MAKES IT A *BIGGER* SHARE THE SMALLER THE PROJECT — THE OPPOSITE OF EVERY OTHER FLOOR ROW THIS ARC HAS PRICED (2026-08-30).** Anything per-CHECKER rather than per-FILE (lib parse/bind/merge, the ~494 field initializers of (INC.53)) is a CONSTANT an IDE pays on every keystroke, so a fixture's FILE COUNT hides it exactly as its `lib` setting does ((INC.61)). Its tell in a floor table is a row stable to ~1% across draws where every other row swings 40%.
- **A FLOOR/FRONT-END FIGURE IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE FIXTURE'S `compilerOptions` AS WELL AS ITS FILE SHAPE — THE GENERATED many-small FIXTURE PINS `"lib": ["es2020"]` (~185 GLOBALS) WHERE AN UNSET `lib` MEANS `dom` (~2,242), AND CHANGING THAT ONE LINE TOOK THE FLOOR'S LARGEST PASS FROM **13.5 ms TO 175.6 ms** ON THE SAME 2,401 FILES (2026-08-30, (INC.61)).** So the whole (INC.\*) arc had been measuring the cheap case; `build/bench/many-small-2400-dom` is the shape to use, and (INC.57)'s "ask whether the profile can express the cost at all" applies to the OPTIONS, not only to the file count. Anything O(files x globals) is invisible on the other fixture.
