So a bisection over the replay set may not assume monotonicity, and every arm needs a wall-clock cap. **And derive the pass universe AT RUN TIME (`PassTiming.registeredPasses`): a source grep of `pass("…"` reads 480 names against the dispatch's 417, so a grep-derived candidate set cannot close.**
- **A GENERIC INSTANTIATION THAT ANSWERS ONE OF ITS OWN ARGUMENTS *UNCHANGED* MUST NOT REGISTER AN ALIAS NAME FOR IT — DOING SO PUT AN UNBOUND `T` IN A HOVER, PROGRAM-WIDE, ON THE ORDINARY SHIPPED BUILD (2026-08-23, (INC.11)).** A conditional that cannot decide (a free type parameter in one argument — `Extract<ClassLikeDeclaration, Pick<T, "kind">>`) answers its CHECK TYPE, i.e. the interned union it was handed; the generic site then wrote `aliasDisplayMap[thatUnion.id] = ("Extract", args)` unconditionally and every later caret on that union rendered the un-instantiated `Extract<…, Pick<T, "kind">>`. **Nothing in this repo sees it but the capture sweeps** — no diagnostic moves, so the corpus, `cost_gate.py` and the profiles are all green. `AliasDisplayIdentityTest`, and note it needs `@useRealLibs` (round 725) or `Pick` degrades to `any` and the pin is vacuous against an ablated binary.
- **…AND `aliasDisplayMap` IS AN *ID-KEYED GLOBAL*, SO IT STRUCTURALLY CANNOT EXPRESS WHAT tsc DOES — tsc PICKS AN ALIAS BY THE **REFERENCE's** DECLARATION SITE (2026-08-23).** Two synonymous non-generic aliases resolving to one interned type are decided FIRST-WINS here (302 spans in `checker.ts` alone once `init:buildFileLocalTypeMaps` is deferred), and no policy change closes that — only a change of KEY, against round 754's deliberate `Type.Reference` exclusion and a union display order pinned byte-for-byte across ~13k baselines. Read a first-wins alias divergence as a KEY problem, and treat it as a logical-parity conversation, never a refactor.
- **A PIN THAT INSTALLS THE MODE IT WANTS AND RESTORES IT LEAVES THE SHIPPED *DEFAULT* PINNED BY NOTHING — AN ABLATION THAT RESTORES THE OLD BEHAVIOUR THEN READS **0 RED** AND LOOKS LIKE A REDUNDANT CHANGE (2026-08-23, (INC.16) arm a1).** Every pin around a `LexDefer`-style mode flag set the mode explicitly, so flipping the default back broke nothing anywhere in a 15,741-test suite. Ship ONE pin that asserts the default IS the intended configuration, with no mode install in it; without it the ablation protocol credits a change with discrimination it has never been tested for, which is round 807's law one layer up.
- **DEFERRING A PER-FILE TABLE IS DECIDED BY *WHO FORCES IT*, AND THE ANSWER IS A COUNT YOU MUST MEASURE, NOT READ — hook `PassTiming.currentPass` at the first ask (2026-08-23, (INC.16)).** `BinderResult.lexicalScopes` was forced for all 78 program files by exactly ONE pass, and the 45 real-lib `.d.ts` binds by NOBODY (worth ~2 ms — the tempting lib half was never the prize). Where the forcer is a round-609 COLLECTOR that may not be gated, the move is to serve it by a PROJECTION: census what it actually consumes (here `SymbolFlags.Enum`/`TypeAlias` scope symbols) and check whether the two halves are symmetric — they were not, the alias half needing only a NAME while the enum half needs the id-keyed SYMBOL, which is what took the skip from 67 of 78 to 75.
- **"THE PASS WRITES A CHECKER FIELD" IS NOT A REFUSAL UNTIL YOU ASK WHETHER THE WRITE IS A PROPERTY OF THE *PROGRAM* OR OF THE *FILE* — 9 of the 53 passes (INC.7) refused on that verdict were installing a per-FILE AMBIENT and were gateable all along (2026-08-23, (INC.20)).** `currentFileLocals = result.locals` / `currentCheckFileName = fileName`, reset after the loop or save-and-restored per iteration, is gone before the next file is walked and leaves the same resting value whether the loop ran 78 times or none. The general move for a MIXED pass is (INC.17)'s split — a program-wide INDEX-building loop stays, the per-file EMITTING loop narrows, and only the second one moves. A per-file RETRACTOR is likewise safe (`getDiagnostics` drops out-of-partition rows anyway), but check the retracted row's own `fileName`: `checkPreEmitCountMismatchPins`' TS-1 marker carries `fileName = null` and so SURVIVED the partition filter — gating it FIXED a global marker being emitted about a file nobody asked about.
- **THE `srcScan` FAMILY MUST BE GATED ALL AT ONCE OR NOT AT ALL: round 895's `srcHas` BUILDS ITS PER-FILE n-GRAM FILTER *LAZILY*, SO THE FIRST SUCH CALLER IN PASS ORDER PAYS IT FOR ALL 78 FILES — ~19.4 ms THAT GATING ONE WALKER MERELY HANDS TO THE NEXT SCANNER (2026-08-23, measured TWICE).** (INC.7) batch 2 mis-read it as a walker that "got slower" (`checkBaseClassImprovedMismatch` 0.07 -> 17.89 ms) and (INC.20) named it (`checkReverseMappedIntersectionConstraint` 0.067 -> 19.431 ms, the only row outside its batch to move more than 0.2 ms). **19 registered passes still iterate `binderResults` and scan whole source**; until every one is gated the cost only relocates. Any "this walker got dearer" reading in this repo should first ask whether it merely became the first ASKER of something lazy.
- **A WHOLE-PROGRAM GATE *ABOVE* A PER-FILE LOOP IS A QUESTION ABOUT THE PROGRAM AND MUST STAY ON `binderResults` — SO NARROWING THAT LOOP BANKS ESSENTIALLY NOTHING, AND WHAT DOES BANK IS A **NAME PRE-GATE** (2026-08-24, (INC.21)).** Three passes with a whole-program `String.contains` above their loop moved ~0.02 ms each when gated, and `checkModulePreserve4Pin` is the control: loops narrowed, row unmoved (1.639 -> 1.699). Adding a pre-gate on the FILE NAME — sound only where it asks what the pass can already do (a `when (basename)` with no `else`, or a loop that `continue`s on every other name) — took two of them 2.509 -> 0.002 and 2.064 -> 0.002. **Read what sits ABOVE the loop before pricing a gating candidate**; and a pre-gate is NOT sound for a pass that wipes rows unconditionally once its needle is found.
- **A RATIO IS A PROPERTY OF WHAT *BOTH* ARMS WERE ASKED, AND A COST COMMON TO BOTH DILUTES IT WITHOUT TRACE — THE RE-ENTRANT REPLAY'S "DECAYING" 3.06x -> 1.91x -> 1.68x LINEAGE WAS MEASURED WITH A WHOLE-FILE CAPTURE REQUEST IN BOTH ARMS, AND THE SAME RUN READS **2.25-2.30x TOTAL / 4.2x AT THE MEDIAN QUERY** ONCE THE CAPTURE IS DROPPED (2026-08-24, (INC.40), two JVMs; with captures at HEAD it reads 1.34x).** The floor law still holds and still applies (quote a replay speed-up with the floor it was measured against — 54-61 ms here), but a mechanism can read "not worth it" purely because the differential that grades its CORRECTNESS also priced it, carrying that gate's extra channel into both arms. **Before re-reading any refusal as final, ask what the arms were asked, not only what regime they ran in.** (INC.40) wired the replay to `Project.diagnosticsOf` on this: the diagnostics channel is 0-divergent on both arms of `replay-differential.sh` where the captured-TYPE channel diverges in 43 of 75 files, so the split is a TYPE — a private one-way valve taking `Set<String>` and returning `List<Diagnostic>` — and never a comment.
- **AND THE FLOOR-vs-WARM-UP TRAP IN THAT HARNESS: A FLOOR BUILD IS ITS OWN CODE PATH, SO FLOOR DRAWS TAKEN AFTER *WHOLE-PROGRAM* WARM-UPS READ A RAMP — 129/89/96 ms BEFORE THE ARMS AGAINST 52-56 AFTER THEM, WHERE `partition-equivalence.sh` READS 54-62 (2026-08-24).** A floor that checks no file exercises nothing the full-build warm-up warmed; measure it at the END, and cross-check a floor figure against the other instrument before quoting it (the inflated one over-read the floor 1.7x and would have understated every ratio derived from it).
- **A PROGRAM-WIDE `init` PASS MAY BE THE PROGRAM'S *FIRST-TOUCH ORDER* RATHER THAN ITS OUTPUT, AND THEN NO AMOUNT OF MAKING IT CHEAPER HELPS — `init:buildFileLocalTypeMaps` HAS NOW BEEN REFUSED THREE TIMES ON THREE DIFFERENT AXES (2026-08-24, (INC.22)).** (INC.10)/(INC.11) deferred PHASES and moved 2,722 / 1,665 capture spans; (INC.22) varied WHICH FILES through the partition view — an axis that cannot move a full build by construction — and still lost **+61 member types to `any`** (`capture-channel` `moreAny` 168 -> 229) plus a DIAGNOSTIC divergence on `partition-gate`'s sensitivity arm. **The order buys TWO things and only one is cheap**: an alias NAME (keeping the `TypeAlias` phase eager costs 6.68 ms and closes 2,275 divergent spans down to +1 row) and member RESOLUTIONS (needs the whole 69 ms). Any future attempt must make member resolution ORDER-INDEPENDENT — round 778's `getTypeOfSymbol` write gate is the known mechanism — and not make the pass cheaper. **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-24 BY (INC.25) — THE PASS *IS* NOW PARTITION-SCOPED AND IS THE SHIPPED DEFAULT.** Both of this entry's obstructions dissolved rather than being solved: the "+61 member types" was **78 rows carrying ONE member name**, caused by a single TRUNCATED resolution that was a SHIPPED bug on ordinary full builds ((INC.25)); and the DIAGNOSTIC divergence belonged to the MIXED `TypeAlias`-program-wide configuration, not to partition-scoping ((INC.23)). **The transferable half that SURVIVES**: a program-wide `init` pass may be the program's first-touch ORDER rather than its output, so before pricing one, ask what the order BUYS — here a name (cheap) and a set of resolutions (which turned out to be one bug). Round 778's write gate was the prime suspect and was REFUTED by measurement (`ambient=empty persisted=true` in both arms).
- **"A FULL BUILD IS UNCHANGED BY CONSTRUCTION" IS THE CLAIM MOST LIKELY TO BE TRUE OF THE CODE AND FALSE OF THE BINARY — MAKE IT A DIGEST, NOT AN ARGUMENT (2026-08-24).** (INC.22)'s axis was sound in the source (the partition view IS `binderResults` when there is no partition, the same property carrying (INC.7)), and it was still checked: a per-arm digest over **381,666 captured types and 360,152 definitions** read identical across arms, corroborated by a COUNT (`lazyBuilds == 0` on every unpartitioned build) and `cost_gate.py` at +0.00% on `output.errors`/`spine.nodes`. A structural argument plus a digest costs one probe; a structural argument alone is round 853's frozen classpath waiting to happen.
- **`narrowRendersMoreAny` IS A SUBSTRING HEURISTIC AND IT OVER-REPORTS — **ZERO** OF THE SHIPPED BASELINE'S 168 "moreAny" ROWS ACTUALLY LOSES A MEMBER TYPE (2026-08-24, (INC.23), classified per ELEMENT).** So a NONZERO value on `capture-channel-equivalence.sh` is a LEAD, never a finding, and (INC.22)'s headline "+61 member types collapse to `any`" was the heuristic rather than the defect — classified properly it is **78 rows carrying exactly ONE member name**. A ZERO still means what it always did, which is why `capture-equivalence.sh`'s `narrowRendersMoreAny=0` remains a real gate. **Classify per element, nesting-aware** (a function-typed member otherwise fragments into its parameters and inflates every count), and re-read any moreAny figure quoted in an older round note with this in mind.
- **A MEMBER TYPE THAT DEGRADES TO `any` UNDER A PARTITION IS USUALLY A *TRUNCATED* RESOLUTION, NOT A CACHE ORDER ONE — AND ROUND 778's WRITE GATE IS NOT THE MECHANISM (2026-08-24, (INC.23), measured with a writer hook printing `(pass, ambient, persisted, depth, truncated)`).** The victim reads `ambient=empty persisted=true` in BOTH arms and differs only in `truncated`: under a partition the first ask arrives from INSIDE the member-table resolution its own `keyof` needs, `resolveStructuredTypeMembersCore` returns silently with `properties` null, and the mapped type degrades. **A narrowed compile has ONE truncated resolution of 822; a full build has 0 of 21,315.** Refusing to PERSIST the truncated answer is measured inert — the re-resolution re-enters the same guard — so the lever is the CYCLE HANDLING, never the cache.
- **A LIB INTERFACE'S BODY IS NEVER SPINE-WALKED, SO A SHAPE THAT RESOLVES CORRECTLY WHEN YOU HAND-WRITE IT CAN BE BROKEN FOR THE **LIB** TYPE OF THE SAME SHAPE — `[Symbol.unscopables]` RENDERED `any` FOR EVERY SMALL PROJECT ON THE ORDINARY SHIPPED BUILD (2026-08-24, (INC.25)).** Four hand-written forms of `interface Foo { un: {[K in keyof Foo]?: boolean} }` all resolve, because the spine walks the interface body and resolves the mapped-type node before any member table is in flight; `interface Array<T>` is never walked, so the first ask arrives from inside `resolveReferenceMembers` and `resolveStructuredTypeMembersCore`'s re-entry guard returns with `properties` null. **So a failed repro of a lib-type defect is evidence about the SPINE, not about the shape** — reproduce against the lib type (three lines: `export const a: string[] = []` plus a `number[]` sibling), and note the 78-file tsc profiles HIDE it because `init:buildFileLocalTypeMaps` happens to resolve that member first.
- **…AND THE RE-ENTRY GUARD IS CORRECT FOR HERITAGE AND *TRUNCATING* FOR ANY READER OF THE KEY SET: A `keyof` OVER AN IN-FLIGHT MEMBER TABLE MUST ANSWER FROM THE **DECLARATIONS**, NEVER BY RE-ENTERING RESOLUTION (2026-08-24).** `getKeyofType` read the null `properties` as `string`, the mapped type bailed to `any`, and round 778's write gate then FROZE it with `ambient=empty persisted=true` — so the cache is not the lever and refusing to persist a truncated answer is measured INERT (the re-resolution re-enters the same guard). The safe shape calls no resolver, reads only already-computed tables plus AST, is bounded by a visited set and a depth cap, and REFUSES rather than returning a partial key domain (round 463) — which is what keeps (INC.19)'s self-referential-alias TS2589-at-(0,0) family unreachable.
- **`getUnionType` PERFORMS NO SUBTYPE REDUCTION, SO A FLOW JOIN KEEPS A NARROWED SUBTYPE BESIDE THE DECLARATION'S OWN CONSTITUENTS — AND THAT IS A **FILTER** BUG, NOT A DISPLAY ONE: THE EXTRA MEMBER SURVIVES A LATER DISCRIMINANT TEST THAT WOULD HAVE FILTERED THE SUPERTYPE AWAY (2026-08-28, (CHK.66); four lines, no loop, no partition — `const x = zzzMk(); if (x === "a") { } x` read `string | number | "a"` where tsc 7.0.2 reads `string | number`).** `flowJoinUnion` applies tsc's `UnionReduction.Subtype` at the TWO joins in `narrowTypeFromFlowCore` and NOWHERE else — **never inside `getUnionType`**, where INV.5(a)'s member-id interning and the byte-pinned union member ORDER of ~13k baselines make it a whole-corpus change. Two conservatisms it needs: only a member the DECLARATION does not itself contain may be dropped (a filter only SUBTRACTS, so a foreign member is one a narrowing INTRODUCED — and this makes the reduction free on almost every join), and the drop needs a STRICT subtype, because **`subtypeRelation` is declared in this repo and has ZERO readers**: assignability is all there is, and it is bidirectional for pairs a subtype relation separates (`any`, optional members).
- **AN ALIAS MUST NOT RENAME A TYPE THAT ALREADY HAS ITS OWN NAME — `type FunctionBody = Block` MADE *EVERY* OCCURRENCE OF `Block` PROGRAM-WIDE RENDER AS `FunctionBody`, IN THE **DIAGNOSTICS** CHANNEL, ON ORDINARY BUILDS (2026-08-24, (INC.26); four lines reproduce it, and tsc 7.0.2 says `Block`).** `shouldRegister`'s Object arm never applied the `symbol == null` test its own Intersection arm applies per constituent, so an alias whose body is a single NAMED interface was stamped onto that interface's `Type.id`, and `typeToString` reads `aliasDisplayMap` BEFORE the structural fallback. An ANONYMOUS body must still register (`type Foo = { a: number }` -> `Foo`) — that boundary is the negative control. **A GENERIC named type is excluded**, because a bare all-defaulted generic resolves to the raw `Type.Interface` rendering `TableClass<any>` rather than a name the source spells — round 754's `typeVariableConstraintedToAliasNotAssignableToUnion` reddens the moment that exclusion is dropped, together with its pin.
- **A FULL-vs-NARROW DIFFERENTIAL SILENTLY ASSUMES THE *FULL* ARM IS THE REFERENCE, AND WHEN THE FULL ARM HOLDS THE DEFECT THE GATE REPORTS A FIX AS A REGRESSION (2026-08-24, (INC.26)).** `capture-equivalence.sh`'s 2,275 divergent spans were read for two rounds as narrowing damage; classified per ELEMENT they are overwhelmingly the FULL build attaching a wrong alias name, and in every family that survives the fix **the NARROWED arm is the more correct one**. So the target for that gate is NOT "narrow agrees with full" but "neither arm has a wrong name to disagree about" — **refuse on sight any proposal to close it by making narrow match full** — and expect the FULL digest to move whenever the fix is a full-build one (it did here, for the first time in the arc: re-record it rather than reading it as a regression).
- **AND `init:buildFileLocalTypeMaps` IS NOT DEFERRABLE, FOR A REASON ITS OWN QUEUE ITEM HAD WRONG: PART OF ITS 66 ms BUYS *RESOLUTIONS*, NOT A FIRST-TOUCH ORDER — a fully deferred arm loses **321 resolutions to `any`** (1,665 divergent capture spans in 47 files) (2026-08-23).** (INC.10) refused it on 2,722 moved DISPLAY spans and (INC.11) re-measured with a three-phase arm (`XTSC_FLTM_EAGER`, default = shipped, pinned inert): better numbers, same refusal, and now a mechanism that no display fix can reach. Do not re-open it from round 829's read-count census either — read-ness of the ENTRY is the wrong question. **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-24 BY (INC.25): it IS deferrable — partition-scoped, shipped, floor 129 -> 58 ms — once the ONE truncated resolution behind the lost resolutions is fixed.** What survives is the DEFERRAL-BY-PHASE refusal ((INC.10)/(INC.11)) and round 829's point that read-ness of the ENTRY is the wrong question; the axis that works is WHICH FILES, through the partition view.
- **A SOURCE-SCANNING ANALYZER'S FUNCTION SPANS ARE CUT BY A *MULTI-LINE PARAMETER LIST*, WHICH TRUNCATES A FUNCTION TO ITS HEADER AND HIDES ITS WHOLE BODY — SO EVERY FIELD WRITE IN IT READS AS ABSENT, i.e. THE ANALYZER CLEARS A PASS IT SHOULD REFUSE (2026-08-23, (INC.7) batch 4).** Third defect in this family after round 809's `'\''` desynchronisation and the `${…}` template, and like both it fails in the REASSURING direction; an expression-bodied `fun f(x) = expr` swallowing the next function is a fourth. **The control that caught it was the QUEUE'S OWN REFUSAL LIST used as an oracle** — the run cleared `checkSpreadNonIterableIntoFixedArity` and `populateAmbientCyclicBaseClasses`, both already refused by reading, which is what exposed the truncation. Keep a KNOWN-REFUSED set beside the stripper's positive control; a span census that cannot re-derive an answer you already know has not been validated. Working control set: raw `fun` count == stripped count with ZERO blanked, every `pass("name")` KDoc sample REFUSED, whole-file brace balance 0, spans non-overlapping and containing no inner header, plus named body probes.
- **…AND IN THIS CHECKER A `pass("…")`-REGISTERING HELPER IS NOT A CALLER: WITHOUT EXCLUDING THE 12 `initCheckPasses*` REGISTRARS FROM THE CALL GRAPH, *EVERY* WALKER READS AS "REACHED FROM ELSEWHERE" AND THE CLEAN SET IS **0** (2026-08-23).** The registrars mention every pass by name, so a naive reachability pass makes the analyzer answer "refuse everything" — which looks like conservatism and is in fact a dead instrument.
- **A `Checker` REUSED ACROSS k QUERIES ***IS*** A CHECKER WHOSE PARTITION IS THOSE k FILES, SO THE ORDER-DEPENDENCE OF A RE-ENTRANT CHECKER CAN BE CENSUSED WITH **NO** CHECKER SURGERY (2026-08-23, (INC.14): 1 divergent row in 741,864, and the shared arm is the one that is right).** `recheckOnly` is a SET and the spine walks it in program order either way, so `recheckOnly = {file}` per query versus `recheckOnly = group` once are the two arms, they must agree, and no baseline is recorded — the wall ratio of the same run is the prize (1.79x/3.19x/3.82x at k = 2/8/26). `scripts/checker-reuse-differential.sh`. Vary k to REPLICATE: it re-groups every file, so it is not one draw repeated.
- **AND A FIXTURE PINNING CROSS-FILE CAPTURE ORDER-DEPENDENCE IS HALF BLIND BY CONSTRUCTION — a capture is recorded DURING the walk, so a file walked LATER cannot influence an earlier file's captured answers in EITHER arm (2026-08-23).** Only the second-walked file's rows are sensitive; write the fixture so the file you are testing is the one the crawl sorts LAST, or the pin can only ever see half of what it claims. Three ablations were run against such a pin and all three read green, one of them demonstrably a DEAD arm (`buildFileLocalTypeMaps` resolves only Function/Class/Interface/Enum/TypeAlias/Alias symbols, and a two-file fixture of `const`s holds none).
- **AND THE FIXTURE FOR "DOES NARROWING LOSE A DIAGNOSTIC" IS VACUOUS BY DEFAULT: AN ARGUMENT-POSITION ERROR (`use({ program: 1 })` AGAINST A FOREIGN TYPE LITERAL PARAMETER) IS NOT REPORTED BY THIS COMPILER AT ALL, SO BOTH ARMS AGREE ON AN EMPTY LIST AND THE PIN PASSES HAVING MEASURED NOTHING (2026-08-22).** The shape that DOES report is a read of the literal's member (`const n: number = make().program` -> TS2322). Any equivalence pin over two compilers, two modes or two arms needs a control asserting the reference arm is NON-EMPTY — round 790's "a verifier reads 0 both when the skip is sound and when the instrument is dead", one instrument over.
- **AN IMPOSSIBLE *ORDERING* IS THE CHEAPEST TELL THAT A WARM-UP RAMP IS BEING MEASURED, AND IT IS SHARPER THAN ANY SPREAD STATISTIC (2026-08-22).** A "floor" arm measured 1,632 ms where the median partition it is a strict SUBSET of the work of measured 1,107 — impossible, and the cause was position: the floor sat at slot 3 of the process while the partitions ran at slots 4-81, and the same process read a full build at 9,421 ms against a warm 4,818. **Put timing arms after whatever warms the JVM, rotate them, and check subset-vs-superset before quoting anything**; four rotated draws then spanned 0.7%. Round 869's law with a free consistency check attached.

- **52.3% OF THE FLOW NODES THIS COMPILER MINTS ARE NEVER READ BY ANY CONSUMER, AND IT IS WORTH 0.63% — DO NOT RE-OPEN "the flow graph builds too much" (round 865, `--flowCensus`, `docs/perf/warm-flow-graph-attribution.md` § 9).** Only **22.0% of the minting WALK** sits in function-like containers nothing reads (43 ms of a 196 ms row); 0.3% is in files nothing reads, and the rest is interleaved with read nodes on antecedent chains that cannot be skipped one at a time — what is left over is one allocation each, which round 801 priced at 0 ms. **And the asymmetry is INVERTED from round 864's side table: a missing side-table entry degrades to a correct fallback, a missing FLOW NODE makes `flowAt` answer `null`, nothing narrows, and the compiler emits a FALSE POSITIVE** — so any laziness here must defer-and-build, never omit.
- **THE FLOW-NODE CLASSES MUST STAY PLAIN CLASSES — `FlowCensus` (and any future side table) keys an IDENTITY `HashSet<FlowNode>` on them, which is sound only while they are not `data` classes (round 865).** Adding `data` to `FlowBranchLabel` & co. turns every such set into round 471's deep-`hashCode()` hazard over a mutable antecedent graph, silently. `FlowNode.id` is NOT an alternative key: it restarts at 0 in every file, exactly as `nodeId` does (round 787).
- **THE INV.4 REACH MACHINERY IS CENSUSED AND CLOSED: 3.32 M EDGE EVALUATIONS AND 1.91 M CONSULTATIONS PER WARM REBUILD, AND EVERY MECHANISM IN IT PRICES BELOW 1% (round 875, `docs/perf/reach-machinery.md`).** Three traps a next agent will otherwise re-derive: (1) **the edge predicates' `when (parent) { is X -> }` chains of 41-119 arms are NOT the cost** — amplification measures **13.3 ns per evaluation and 106 arms costs the same as 49**, so the whole population is 44 ms = 0.67% and a `kindId` tableswitch conversion (the M0.2 shape, tempting because round 803 got -3.93% converting `forEachChild`) can take almost none of it; (2) **a push-based status maintained by the walk is 11.1x MORE work**, because it must compute a status for all 43 classifiers at every node while the pull scheme folds only the ancestors of the kinds each is consulted at (16 of the 43 are consulted under 1,000 times, one ONCE); (3) **a memo hit rate of 8%, with 23 classifiers at exactly 0%, is CORRECT, not a cache to fix** — a classifier is consulted at most once per node, so the memo can never answer its own query and exists only to terminate an ascent. Only the memo TRANSPOSITION (43 per-file `nodeId`-keyed arrays into one row per node) is still open, at <= ~0.8%.
- **A PER-FILE, NAME-KEYED "could this root ever be narrowed" SET IS VACUOUS — it refuses 0 of 14,117 openings and costs 150-211 ms to do it (round 855, `docs/perf/narrowed-any-opening-price.md` § 4b).** Every `VariableDeclaration`/`Parameter`/`BindingElement` mints a `FlowAssignment` whose subtree contains the declared name, so **every root DECLARED in a file is in that file's narrowable set by construction** — the set can only refuse a root with no declaration in the file (an import), and real `any` receivers are locals and parameters. Skipping a narrowing walk needs a *path-and-position* oracle, which is the walk; do not re-propose the name-keyed form. Corollary for pinning it: `diagnose()` is SINGLE-FILE, an unresolved import is not `anyType`, so the refusing population cannot be reached from a fixture at all (`openings=0`) — pin such a predicate at the SET level (`FlowGraphBuilder().build(…)`), not through a compile.

- **AND IT IS CLOSED FOR A SECOND, LARGER REASON THAT ITS OWN CENSUS CANNOT SEE: THE PASS'S REAL PRODUCT IS THE PROGRAM'S FIRST-TOUCH *ORDER*, AND DEFERRING IT MOVES THE CAPTURE CHANNEL FROM **5 DIVERGENT SPANS TO 2,722 IN 46 OF 76 FILES** (2026-08-22, (INC.10), built and reverted).** Resolving every file's declarations up front makes type DISPLAY a function of the program rather than of who walked first; 83% of the divergence is `aliasDisplayMap` (an alias name attaches to an interned type at its FIRST mint — `ModuleName` vs `ModuleExportName`) and the residual is UNDIAGNOSED and runs the other way (full `Extract<…>` vs narrow `ClassLikeDeclaration`), so it may be two different `Type` instances rather than two renderings. **Measured trade-off, and it is almost exactly one-for-one: fully deferred = 0.01 ms / 2,722 spans; `TypeAlias` symbols kept eager = 6.81 ms / 462 spans; the whole DECLARATION branch kept eager = 64.94 ms / 5 spans (baseline). The deferrable part is 1.13 ms of 66.** So round 829's "1,499 of 4,161 entries are ever READ" is the wrong question — read-ness of the ENTRY says nothing about the ORDER — and `scripts/capture-equivalence.sh` is the ONLY gate that sees it: `partition-equivalence.sh`, `cost_gate.py` and all ~13k corpus baselines are green on the fully-deferred build. (INC.2)'s "do NOT infer a capture's correctness from a green diagnostics sweep — they are different resolvers", collected.
- **A PASS WHOSE PRODUCT HAS ONE READER MAY STILL BE UNDEFERRABLE, AND A PASS THAT LOOKS LIKE A COLLECTOR MAY BE PURE EMIT-ONLY WORK — CHECK THE READER'S *CALLER*, NOT THE PASS'S NAME (2026-08-22, (INC.10), one of each in the same round).** `trackAllImportReferences` reads as a program-wide collector and is 100% emit-only: `referencedAliases` is read by `isReferencedAliasDeclaration` alone, whose ONE caller is a single line of `Transformer` reached only by `import x = require(…)` under `module: preserve` without `verbatimModuleSyntax` — and round 738's `skipEmitOutputs` gate means a `--noEmit` build never constructs a transformer, so the walk ran **78 times per checker** to fill a set nothing could read. **Two consequences for pinning such a deferral.** Prefer deferring to a static `if (options.skipEmitOutputs) return`: the corpus EMITS, so a deferred path is exercised by thousands of `.js` baselines while a statically gated one is never tested at all. And **a pin written on the mechanism's OBVIOUS shape can be vacuous** — an ESM `import { a } from "./x"` fixture answers correctly with `referencedAliases` EMPTY, so both elision assertions passed against a binary with the ask deleted, and only the ablation said so.
- **A PRODUCED-vs-CONSUMED CENSUS MUST BE KEYED ON THE *CALLS*, NEVER ON WHAT WAS STORED (round 829, a 4x under-read).** Keying the deletable population on map ENTRIES missed the largest population in the pass — **67.3% of its resolutions store nothing at all** (they answer `any`/`errorType`), so they can never be "read" and were structurally invisible; the tell is a classified population that does not sum to its own denominator.
- **A FALSIFIER CONTROL CAN BE DEAD ON THE VERY PROFILE THE PRIZE WAS MEASURED ON — RUN IT WHERE THE THING IT WATCHES ACTUALLY HAPPENS (round 793).** `--ccetPreGateBogus` (the gate refuses everything) reports 14 on harness and **0 on the compiler profile**, because nothing in tsc's own compiler sources ever reaches a call-prologue emission; a "0 skipped calls emitted" column read only there proves nothing. Check the control fires on the SAME run that reports the zero, and if it cannot, say so and move the falsification to a fixture (an in-process probe pin) or another profile.

- **A CALL WHOSE CALLEE IS A MEMBER OF AN IMPORTED NAMESPACE IS NEVER ARGUMENT-CHECKED — `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` does not run for it at all (round 766, isolated with three probes).** A same-file `namespace Debug` reaches the argument gate and a plain exported function imported from another file reaches it; an exported `namespace Debug` imported from another file does NOT, in both the direct-import and `export * from` barrel forms. That is **1,108 `Debug.<member>(…)` calls on the compiler profile and 1,518 on services** whose arguments no argument-position check ever sees. **So a call-argument fix that measures ZERO firings on the profiles is probably not inert — it is probably invisible**: price it against a probe that uses a plain imported function before concluding anything (rounds 764-766's arms all read 0 for this reason). It is NOT a general resolution failure — the flow-walk assert path resolves a namespace member fine.
- **A PER-KIND SPINE DISPATCH TABLE IS CLOSED *COLD* AND IS A ~1% LEVER WARM — PRICED ROUND 867, DO NOT RE-DERIVE IT.** `s_p`, the production cost of ONE consultation that is entered and immediately declines, is **2.286 ns [2.148-2.512]**, so the whole prize is **`R` = 32.0 M x `s_p` = 73.2 ms = 1.06% of a warm rebuild** (`docs/perf/dispatch-table.md` § 9; round 866's `[0, 352] ms` / 187 ms estimate is SUPERSEDED, 2.6x too high). Three consequences: (i) **do NOT re-run `--dispatchGated` to decide it** — that arm measures `G - R`, one equation in two unknowns, and the machinery alone is **+715 ms = +14.4% of the warm `checkSpine` row**, enough to hide the whole prize; (ii) the prize is real but **one band below what `ab-warm.sh` (±1.0%) can confirm**, so a landed table must be defended on `cost_gate.py` counters plus § 9's decomposition; (iii) the implementation shape is decided — a per-kind `Long` **BITMASK** (`SpineDispatch.enterSkipMask`/`leaveSkipMask`, complement-exact and pinned, measured at 13.16 ns/node = 15% of the prize), never a dense `when (kindId)` of 138 arms, which is far over the 8,000-bytecode cliff (round 845's -33.6% warm). **The spine's cost is SIX handlers holding ~61-63% of it** (warm, round 847): `ccetSpineLeave` 876 ms, `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` 853, `cpaSpineLeave` 617, `ctaSpineEnter` 359, `spineIanyEnterNode` 171, `spineArithEnterNode` 153 — cta/cpa/ccet frame bookkeeping, i.e. a per-handler problem ((SPINE.1)), and round 732's older ordering is STALE. Two handlers LOOK own-kind-closed and are not (`spineArithLeaveNode`, `spineNpLeaveNode`) — never read only a handler's first three lines. The full derivation, the consultation census and round 732's cold figures are in the archive.
- **BYTES IS A 10x-NOISY PROXY FOR CHECKING COST — CENSUS PER *NODE*, OR A SIZE LADDER WILL MANUFACTURE A SUPER-LINEARITY THAT DOES NOT EXIST (2026-08-24, (INC.37)).** Over tsc's own files >100 KB the per-byte price is **76-739 µs/KB** (data literals vs expressions) and two 1.3-1.5 KB files differ **3.5x**, so a fitted intercept is whichever small file you drew; per NODE the population is flat (p10 6.08 / median 9.71 / p90 19.63 µs) and `checker.ts` is at the **p10**. Every byte-based regression in that round predicted it low by 1.2-4.3x. `docs/perf/file-check-decomposition.md` § 3.
- **A PER-HANDLER SPINE RANKING IS A PROPERTY OF THE *CODEBASE*, NOT OF THE COMPILER — ROUND 847's ORDER IS REFUTED UNDER A SINGLE-FILE PARTITION AND THE TOP-THREE PERMUTATION DIFFERS ON EVERY FILE (2026-08-24, (INC.37); every arm warm, so warm-up rate is not the cause).** The six-handler SET survives (65.7% vs 63.0%); `cpaSpineLeave` goes third -> **first (22.9%)** and `ccetSpineLeave` first -> third. **Quote the file with the ranking**, and note the `dispatch` tier bypasses `spineEnterMask`, so its enter-side shares price the pre-888 regime.
- **A SECTION-PROBE'S OWN BOUNDARY COST MUST BE PRICED DIFFERENTIALLY — an in-situ EMPTY-SPAN calibration over-reads by 3.5-4.4x and has now misled three consecutive rounds (733/734/735).** Back-to-back empty `at()` spans read 306 ns (unrolled) and 391 ns while the true cost is ~86-92 ns: a `repeat` loop puts a back-edge SAFEPOINT POLL inside every empty span, an unrolled sequence still attributes stop-the-world pauses, and a span across a non-inlinable call reads ~1 us (a cold transition, not a timestamp pair). **The only trustworthy figure is a DIFFERENTIAL: run the SAME code at N boundaries and at 1, divide by the extra boundary count.** `ArgSections` implements that as a MODE (`--argSectionsCoarse` keeps only the partition anchors, so a non-anchor boundary costs a static read and a not-taken branch); copy that shape rather than the empty-span loop. Per-section nanos are sound for RELATIVE attribution only, never as a production cost model.
- **When the thing being priced is SMALLER than a timestamp pair, AMPLIFY THE SIGNAL instead of shrinking the instrument (round 759, `docs/perf/claim-audit-round758.md` § 11).** A ~89 ns pair around a ~37 ns `globals[name]` probe would BE the measurement, and counters (round 736's escape) cannot price a `HashMap` get. `--globalsAmp r` brackets **r** reads of the same key under ONE pair, so `p(r) = cold + (r-1)*warm + boundary` and **two values of `r` cancel the boundary algebraically** — no calibration needed for the slope. Falsify it by ARITHMETIC, not timing: the sink must be an exact multiple of the hit count at every `r` (that is what rules out JIT elision), and the slopes from three independent pairs must agree. The in-situ empty bracket (`r < 0`) is used ONLY to split `cold` from the boundary, and its answer carries the 3.5-4.4x over-read caveat above.
- **…AND AN AMPLIFICATION LADDER READ OFF THE *WHOLE-REBUILD WALL* MUST BE ABBA-ROTATED INSIDE EACH PROCESS, BECAUSE THE FIRST INSTRUMENTED REBUILD IS THE SLOWEST DRAW AND IS WORTH UP TO 15% (round 869).** An ascending `r0,r8,r16` ladder puts that bias entirely on the `r = 0` arm and FLATTENS the slope; the same `r = 0` arm read 8,082 ms in one cycle and 6,872 ms in the next of one process. Run `r16,r8,r0,r0,r8,r16` and average per arm, and put >=8 rebuilds before the first tier. This is a 5-for-5 law in this arc; it is only invisible when every arm is measured once. **AND ROTATION INSIDE ONE PROCESS IS NOT ENOUGH AT TWO DRAWS PER ARM — RUN THE MIRROR ROTATION AS A SECOND BATCH (round 891).** `r16,…,r16` and `r0,…,r0` over the SAME binary and the same family read **53.6 and 14.1 ms/rep**, a 4x disagreement, because the leading draw's 15% lands wholly on whichever arm ran first and a 6-point fit cannot absorb it; pooling the two rotations (or discarding the leading draw from each) gives 33.9/32.6 ms with the two sub-intervals agreeing to 2.5%. A single 6-draw ladder is a batch, not a result.
- **THE REVERSE-REPLAY SCOPE MECHANISM HAS ONE HOME — `MapScopeStack<V>` / `SetScopeStack` (`ScopeStack.kt`) — AND A THIRD COPY FAMILY MUST INSTANTIATE IT, NEVER HAND-ROLL A FOURTH (round 892 retired round 891's `VarScopeStack` onto it for exactly this reason).** Two facts a new instantiation needs: the scope is a `MutableMap`/`MutableSet` **FACADE** because these maps are handed OUT to legacy helpers that write through them, and `clear()` THROWS (it cannot be recorded in O(1), and a silent one drops the ENCLOSING scopes' entries). A frame that SHARES its parent's map is "open no scope" — a flag the pop mirrors, so push and pop are paired by construction.
- **A REFUSAL'S REASONS MUST EACH NAME A COST, NOT A FIELD — round 891 refused the cta local family partly because its sibling `ambiguousNames` RESETS rather than shadows (O(size) for an undo log), and that sibling was never COPIED at all: a fn frame gives it a fresh EMPTY set, which is O(1) (round 892).** A field that lives beside the expensive ones, is installed by the same sandwich and is named in the same census row can still be outside the family; check whether it appears in the COPY accounting before letting it block a conversion. The same round's other two reasons were an un-instrumented zero and a real one — so one of three refusal grounds was a category error that cost a round.
- **A PER-SCOPE WHOLE-MAP COPY IS REPLACEABLE BY ONE LIVE MAP + AN UNDO LOG *EXACTLY* WHEN THE STACK IS STRICTLY LIFO, EVERY REMOVAL IS RECORDED, AND NO READER MUTATES OR RETAINS THE MAP — AND WHAT DECIDES WHETHER IT IS WORTH IT IS `writes/entries`, NEVER THE COPY COUNT (rounds 869/892, `docs/perf/warm-leaf-profile.md` §§ 9-13).** The pop replays its frame's slice in REVERSE, which is what makes a repeated write to one key correct with no per-frame "already shadowed" set. Two audit refinements that decided the hardest case: **a local `val saved = <the map>` pointer swap is NOT a retention** (identity is stable, so the restore puts the same object back — only a FIELD assignment is), and **an ad-hoc `EpochMap(currentLocalTypes)` install is NOT a disqualifier** (a DETACHED snapshot that never merges back cannot observe the difference). **The two annotation families (`spineOs`/`spinePd`) satisfy all three; `currentLocalTypes`, `CtaFrame.localTypes` and `CtaFrame.varTypes` DO NOT — do not widen `AnnScopeStack` to them without new pins**: a wrong scope there does not crash, it silently resolves a name to an outer binding (the `applyBodyLocalShadowing` FP class), and `varTypes` is deliberately SHARED at some frames and copied at others, so "share" becomes a replay FLAG rather than a structural fact. `AnnScopeStack.view` is the LIVE map, not a snapshot — retaining it past its frame is the one way to break it silently.
- **…AND WHEN THE SCOPE'S MAP IS HANDED *OUT* TO HELPERS THAT WRITE INTO IT, THE SCOPE MUST BE A `MutableMap` **FACADE** (`put`/`putAll`/`remove` routed through the log), NOT A PRIVATE MAP WITH A `put` METHOD — AND THE FACADE IS THEN THE ONLY PLACE THE FAMILY'S WRITES CAN BE COUNTED (round 891, `CtaFrame.varTypes`: 1,145,523 entries -> 16,182 undo records, ~33.9 ms = 0.59% warm).** That is not bookkeeping: the pre-891 census hooked **2 of the 3** write paths and its recorded ratio was 6.3x optimistic with nothing to say so. **`clear()` on such a facade must THROW** — it cannot be recorded in O(1), and a silent one drops the enclosing scopes' entries. The remaining three families are REFUSED with reasons, not unstarted (`docs/perf/cta-frame-copy-families.md` § 3).
- **A SKIP'S POSITIVE CONTROL IS ITS COMPLEMENT POPULATION, WHICH COSTS NOTHING TO SHIP (round 790).** A verifier that keeps the OLD behaviour and counts violations over the skipped population (round 788's protocol) reads 0 both when the skip is sound and when the instrument is dead. Running the SAME comparison over the population the gate never skips answers that for free — there it must diverge — so no deliberately bogus baseline has to be injected. `--verifyLoopRetry` / `--verifyLoopRetryAll` are the reference pair.
- **THE FLOW-NARROWING TAIL IS EXTREME AND ITS THREE OBVIOUS CAUSES ARE ALL MEASURED FALSE (round 735, compiler profile): 394 of 70,037 walks (0.56%) cost 1,485 ms = 47% of all narrowing and 4.9% of the compile.** Before proposing anything there, know that the tail does NOT trip (`narrowWalk tripped: 0` — so the "tripped walks are never memoized, hence re-run in full" theory is dead), does NOT exhaust the 1,000,000-visit budget (630,641 arrivals across all 394; 1,601 each, max 19,515), and is NOT memoizable (`walkMiss cold=69,968` of 70,037 — essentially every launched walk is a first sighting, on top of the 40,709 the LIVE memo already serves). The residue is arithmetic: the tail runs at 2,354 ns per flow-node arrival against a 372 ns all-walk mean, i.e. 13x more arrivals AND 6.3x costlier arrivals. Diagnose with `--passTiming` (grep `narrowWalk`); full derivation in `docs/perf/argument-check-attribution.md`.
- **tsc's UNION-ALIAS DISPLAY IS *IDENTITY PRESERVATION*, NOT STRUCTURAL MATCHING, AND INV.5(a) MAKES IT UNREACHABLE HERE BY CONSTRUCTION (2026-08-24, (INC.27), proved rather than argued).** tsc keys its union cache by `getTypeListId(types) + getAliasId(aliasSymbol, …)`, so ONE member set has several instances and it answers `ModuleName`, `ModuleExportName` **and** the bare `Ident | Str` where no alias was written; a join-built `A | B` renders structurally while a no-op narrow of `x: MyType` renders `MyType` (both in `narrowByClauseExpressionInSwitchTrue6`'s pristine baseline) — i.e. the rule is "an operation that did not change the type does not change its name". **Round 545's INV.5(a) interns our unions by member-id list ALONE**, so all of those collapse to one `Type`: **no id-keyed or member-set-keyed table can give three answers from one key, and any mechanism that can name the flow-RECONSTRUCTED union necessarily also names a union nobody wrote.** So a union-display divergence is an INTERNING-KEY question — do not attempt to fix it in `unionAliasStructural` (B416) or `aliasDisplayMap`.
- **…AND A "POISON THE AMBIGUOUS CASE" RULE IS SELF-DEFEATING WHENEVER ITS OWN TRIGGER IS COVERAGE-DEPENDENT — MEASURED, IT MADE THE GATE WORSE (1,128 -> 1,351 spans, 43 -> 46 files) WHILE DOING EXACTLY WHAT IT CLAIMED (2026-08-24).** Refusing to name a member set that two differently-named aliases claim collapsed the `full=name/narrow=name` bucket **416 -> 2** and created a `full=structural/narrow=name` bucket of **657**, because whether the trigger fires depends on which aliases happened to be RESOLVED — so it converts a small coverage difference into a presence-or-absence difference and amplifies it. Nor is ambiguity decidable syntactically: of 407 collisions per compile the largest are aliases whose body is ANOTHER alias (`type FunctionLike = SignatureDeclaration`), spelling no members at all. **Before adding a guard, ask whether its PRECONDITION is a function of the very coverage you are correcting for.**
- **A GENERIC ALIAS'S OWN TYPE PARAMETERS MUST BE IN SCOPE FOR ITS BODY, AND THEY WERE NOT — `type Box<T> = { v: T }` RENDERED `{ v: any; }` ON ORDINARY BUILDS (2026-08-24, (INC.28); four lines, no partition, not order-dependent).** `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker`'s type-alias arm resolved `decl.type` with NO type-parameter scope, so the alias's own `T` answered `errorType` — and **`any` ABSORBS A UNION**, so a union body collapsed entirely rather than partially, which is why the symptom is a whole type vanishing rather than one member degrading. **The fix is a SPLIT and the split is forced**: `getTypeOfSymbolWorker`'s alias arm answers the parametric form, while `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol` — what a REFERENCE resolves to — must NOT, because handing references the parametric form costs two measured corpus false positives (`typeArgumentDefaultUsesConstraintOnCircularDefault`, `excessPropertyCheckIntersectionWithRecursiveType`). And `declaredTypes` has **no write gate at all**, so whoever touches an alias first freezes it — which is how a partition made this visible ((INC.19)'s shape with the roles reversed).
- **…AND A FULL-vs-NARROW COMPARISON IS NOT A PIN WHEN BOTH ARMS AGREE ON THE *WRONG* ANSWER — (INC.28)'s two-arms-agree test and its negative control stayed GREEN against the unfixed binary while two real pins went RED (2026-08-24).** The capture sweeps are differentials, so a defect present in BOTH arms is invisible to them by construction; that is how a generic alias rendering `{ v: any; }` survived every gate in this repo. **A pin for a display defect must assert the VALUE** (`[{ v: any; }, { v: any; }]` is what the unfixed binary reads), never that two arms agree — and the ground truth is obtainable rather than guessable: `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc --lsp -stdio` answers hover/definition/completion directly (round 924).
- **…AND THE SEQUEL IS THE OPPOSITE READING OF THE SAME PARAMETER: A BARE `Type.TypeParam` ARGUMENT JUDGED BY `checkTypeRelatedTo` READS AS A CONSTRAINT **FAILURE** WHERE THE HONEST ANSWER IS **UNDECIDED**, SO B57.1b SKIPPED THE SUBSTITUTION, THE REFERENCE ANSWERED `errorType`, AND THE WHOLE ALIAS BODY RENDERED `any` ON ORDINARY BUILDS (2026-08-24, (INC.42)).** The relation has no "TypeParam source via its constraint" rule ((INC.30)), so **an inner parameter carrying ANY constraint fails — including one constrained IDENTICALLY to the argument**; only an unconstrained one was ever correct, and `any` ABSORBS A UNION, so the body vanishes rather than degrading (`type A1<X extends Nd> = (n: number) => R1<X>` -> `(n: number) => any`). Judge such an argument LOCALLY against its own constraint and confine it to the DISPLAY path (`aliasBodyDisplayDepth`) — on the checking path it reads `output.errors` **46 -> 48** on the compiler profile.
- **A RECURSION BRAKE LIVES IN THE *ENCLOSING* DECLARATION, NOT THE REFERENCED ONE — A GUARD WRITTEN ABOUT THE REFERENCED ALIAS LEFT THE CORPUS RED, AND ONLY A **FLIP CENSUS** (WHICH DECISIONS DOES THE RELAXATION ACTUALLY CHANGE?) NAMED THE MECHANISM (2026-08-24, (INC.42)).** Relaxing B57.1b's constraint guard flips exactly four decisions in `excessPropertyCheckIntersectionWithRecursiveType`, and every one is a **NON-recursive** alias (`Length<I>`, `Prepend<any, I>`) referenced from inside the self-referential `BuildTree` — reading the guard cannot show that. Census what a change FLIPS before deciding what a guard is for.
- **CACHING IN FRONT OF A RESOLUTION IS A CLOSED DIRECTION (round 716, the THIRD independent confirmation — see docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md § 0). The law: in xtsc the cacheable population is the CHEAP TAIL, so a keyed probe costs more than the work it avoids.** Priced three ways in one session: the whole context-bypassed `getTypeFromTypeNode` population is **68 ms** (31,571 outermost calls @ 2.2 µs = 0.35% of the compile), so INV.5(c) exists for a third of one percent; WIDENING its round-548 checking-file gate lifts hits 23% → 46% and measures **+28% wall** (6 interleaved pairs), and memoizing the fingerprint (builds 53,765 → 13,293) still measures **+11.9%**; pure IDENTITY keying (tsc's mapper-object shape) gets **4.1%** hits because the context maps are re-allocated per install, not reused per region. Prior confirmations: round 665 (`getTypeOfExpression` memo servable population = 30 ms, a 35× over-estimate) and round 659 (75% of a migrated pass's cost reappears in checkSpine). **tsc is not fast because it caches** — `NodeLinks.resolvedType` is a field read on the node, with no key to build. **Before proposing any new cache, measure the PRIZE first** (time the outermost calls of the population you intend to serve); every over-estimate in this codebase came from hits × mean-call-cost.
- **The INV.5(c) context fingerprint is INCOMPLETE, and the round-548 checking-file gate is what hides it (round 716).** Widening the gate produced 1,269 shape-different serves — every one a lib generic signature (`(value: T, …)` served where `(value: Declaration, …)` was correct) under an IDENTICAL fingerprint — so the substitution input is ambient state captured by none of `inferenceNamespaceStack`/`currentTypeParamScope`/`currentTypeAliasArgs`. Any future widening must close that first; the prize for doing so is the 68 ms above, so it is almost certainly not worth it. Diagnose with `--verifyMappedCache` (recomputes every served hit and splits shape-different from id-only).
- **CUTTING THE `getTypeOfExpression` RECOMPUTE FACTOR IS CLOSED (round 737, `docs/perf/type-of-expression-attribution.md`) — the ×2.76 is REAL and its mechanism is exactly the one ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK § 0.1 stage 3 named, but the whole prize is 823 ms (2.9%) and the largest actionable handler-pair merge is 166 ms (0.58%).** The factor decomposes as **2.05× cross-handler × 1.34× recursion**: 177 sites initiate typing and 45.2% of typed nodes carry >1 origin, yet per-caller factors are 1.00–1.11, so no handler re-types alone and there is nothing for one handler to fix. § 0's law in a cache-free shape: the four biggest co-occurrence pairs by COUNT are 141,388 repeats worth 71 ms (0.5 µs each) while the biggest by TIME is 2,603 repeats worth 166 ms (64 µs) — **what is redundantly recomputed is what was already cheap.** Two numbers to stop mis-quoting: `typeOfExprNanos` ("total incl. nested") DOUBLE-COUNTS a subtree once per nesting level — the true cost of all expression typing is **2,439 ms = 8.5% of checker-init** — and **74.4% of the calls are OUTERMOST**, so "the factor is just recursion" is false. Attribute with `--typeOfExprCallers` before proposing anything here.
- **SKIPPING A *CACHED* RESOLUTION MOVES THE WORK, IT DOES NOT DELETE IT — so a SKIPPABLE population is not a RECOVERABLE one (round 788, measured on the `cpaComputeArgCtxTypes` pre-gate).** 96% of its calls produced a value nothing could read, priced at 265 ms; gating them out skipped 94.3% of the calls and dropped four cost counters 2.75-5.09%, yet the timed row fell only **109 ms** and the downstream engine row rose by about as much — because the callee type it resolved is memoized, so the first later asker pays instead. **Before pricing any skip, ask which of the skipped callees CACHE their result: what is recoverable is only the part with no other consumer.** This is the dual of the closed caching direction below, and the same law: what looks free to remove is usually what was already going to be paid.
- **A WARM A/B IS MORE SENSITIVE IN ms AND *LESS* SENSITIVE IN PERCENT (round 788).** The warm rebuild is ~11.9 s against a ~27 s cold compile, so a saving whose code C2 has already compiled is a SMALLER fraction of the warm run — a change worth ~1.4% cold can read as ~0.5% warm and land inside the +-1.0% band. Quote the driver's own verdict line (round 788 got `NOISE-DOMINATED`: per-pair spread 270 ms against an 80 ms median delta, with both arm sds under 1%), never a median alone, and fall back to `cost_gate.py` counters — which is what the driver itself tells you to do.
- **POPULATION vs FREQUENCY — the arc's most expensive recurring error, now with three measured instances (round-758 audit of 57 claims, `docs/perf/claim-audit-round758.md`).** "N% of the nodes / N of the calls / N sightings" answers *how often a path is entered*, NEVER *how much work is behind it*, and here the two differ by 5–146×: `IDENTIFIER` is 44.5% of the nodes and **8.4%** of the spine's time (5.3×); `getCalleeType`'s "half its results are thrown away" is 50.6% of the calls and **8–10%** of the time (6.2×); 874 expression-bodied arrows held **six** block bodies (146×). So: **price the population before designing the fix; never multiply a count by a mean cost.** Corollary: a RESIDUAL (a time obtained by SUBTRACTION) is not a measurement of whatever you then name it — § 0's "dispatch + handler machinery, 42%" is the migrated passes' own checking work, and real dispatch is 100–300 ms.
- **BUT THE DIRECTION IS NOT ALWAYS "SMALLER", AND ASSUMING IT COST TWO ROUNDS THEIR PREDICTIONS (round 759, `docs/perf/claim-audit-round758.md` § 10).** "What you could skip cheaply is what was already cheap" **holds only when the exit predicate and the cost share a CAUSE** — a `getCalleeType` resolution is discarded *because* it failed fast. Inside `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` it is FALSE: the 72% of arguments that never reach the assignability check carry **89%** of the argument-typing time (**22,604 ns vs 7,134 ns**), because the predicate reads the PARAMETER (`isSimpleCheckableType`, foreign TPs) while the cost belongs to the ARGUMENT, and complex parameters attract complex arguments. Rounds 758 and 759 both predicted "< 40%" from the prior and were out by ~2.5×. **Ask what SELECTS the population and what DRIVES its cost before invoking the law.** The CACHE form (line above, "stop proposing caches") is unaffected. Also from that round: *paying for a value is not wasting it* — the 89% is consumed by eleven downstream blocks, so a big share is not automatically a prize.
- **The flow-walk memo (round 664) is LIVE and DEPENDENCY-KEYED: key = (reference nodeId, fileHash, walkKind, inputId), value = the FlowGraph instance + the Type instances bound to the reference's ROOT NAME in currentLocalTypes / narrowedDeclaredTypes; it serves only while all three match.** Three invariants a change must preserve: a walk that TRIPPED (`flowDepthTripped`) is NEVER stored (its result is truncated and its TS2563 side effect must keep firing; a served hit runs no walk, so it consumes no visit budget and trips can only get rarer); the value is `Any?` + an unchecked cast, sound ONLY because `walkKind` is in the key (Boolean-returning `isAssignedAtFlow` must never share a key with the Type-returning walks); and a NEW `flowWalkWithTripCheck` call site must pass a `kind`, an `inputId` folding its starting type id with its path hash, and the path's ROOT NAME — omitting any of them silently mixes distinct walks into one entry (round 662 measured that collision as 165 wrong serves).
- **There are TWO narrowing memos at DIFFERENT scopes and they fail differently — the entry above is the INTER-walk one; `NarrowFlowMemo` is the INTRA-walk one, and round 736 found its serve condition was costing 631,585 recomputed flow-node arrivals per compiler-profile compile.** Its `served(id, depth)` guards ONE thing: a deeper entry has less budget before `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH` and might truncate where the stored computation did not. That is decidable, not approximable — `depth` reaches the result through no other channel, and only NON-truncated subtrees are ever stored — so an entry carries `hi` (the max depth its own subtree reached) and also serves when `depth + (hi - storedDepth) < maxDepth`. **THE NON-OBVIOUS INVARIANT: a served hit MUST fold `depth + memo.lastHitHeight` into the caller's `narrowWalkHiDepth`** — otherwise an ancestor records the memo shortcut's height instead of a fresh recomputation's and the disjunct goes unsound under nesting. Both walker mirrors carry it; `NarrowMemoDepthTest` + `IntKeyMapTest` pin it. Corollary for future rounds: when a cache measures as "cannot reach this population", check whether a DIFFERENT cache at a different scope is the one failing.
- **The expression-memo epoch (`spineExprEpoch`) is PROBE-ONLY — it is read only by the two `--passTiming` shadow memos, so a fence change cannot alter compiler output (round 660).** Its churn is attributed: of the narrowing walks' 80 k memo misses, 57% are COLD (first sighting — unrecoverable by any fence), and of the 43% epoch-invalidated repeats 99.6% recompute IDENTICALLY, but `meanEpochDelta` is ~154–218 with 96% of blame on genuine `currentLocalTypes`/`currentFlowGraph` swaps — so **a finer per-map fence does NOT work; memo validity must be DEPENDENCY-KEYED (per reference-root-name generation)**. Fenced setters skip the bump when the value is unchanged (`if (field !== v)`) — 28% of bumps were no-op save/restore round-trips; keep that guard on any new fenced field.
- **THE SEQUENTIAL SELF-COMPILE ANCHOR IS NOT STABLE ACROSS ROUNDS AT THE ~10% LEVEL, WITH IDENTICAL CODE AND AN IDENTICAL PROFILE — 23,183 (r826) / 25,299 (r823) / 26,145 (r824) / 26,518 ms (bench row, 2026-07-28) = a 12.8% spread, all four the same sequential path on the same 78-file profile.** Round 824 reconciled its seq arm with round 823's as "3.3% apart, well inside either arm's band"; the third point shows that was luck. **Only WITHIN-round paired deltas are quotable** — never compare an absolute ms figure to another round's.
- **A "SINGLE-THREADED" xtsc RUN CONSUMES ~4.17 OF THIS BOX'S 8 CORES (round 824; it was ~3.15 of 4 on the old box, round 740) — AND THAT TAX IS NOT A CONSTANT: IT SCALES WITH `nproc`, BECAUSE `CICompilerCountPerCPU` IS TRUE BY DEFAULT.** Doubling the cores took `CICompilerCount` 3 → 4 and `ParallelGCThreads` 4 → 8, and the JIT's CPU from ~21.7 s to ~43.8 s (`-XX:CICompilerCount=2` drops the run 4.20 → 2.55 cores). **So never express a core budget as "N cores NET of a fixed ~3.2"** — round 740's ">= 12 cores" unpark condition did exactly that and was miscalibrated in the conservative direction (the answer arrived at 8; free cores went 0.85 → 3.83, not → 4.85). Two consequences that did NOT change: **never run two compiles/benchmarks concurrently** (one copy already occupies half the box), and **this is not a single-thread lever** — self time is flat across baseline / JIT-starved / GC-starved (round 618's "JVM flag hunting is DEAD" stands; the JIT threads occupy cores a WORKER would want, they do not steal from the compile thread).
- **THE `--passTiming` PROBE LANDS ~100% INSIDE `checkSpine` AND NOWHERE ELSE, SO A `full`-TIER TABLE OVERSTATES THAT ONE ROW AND NOTHING ELSE — USE `--passTimingRows`, WHICH KEEPS THE WHOLE PER-PASS TABLE FOR +0.25% COLD AND 0.0% WARM (round 846, `docs/perf/warm-jvm-attribution.md` § 10).** Measured both regimes: of the probe's ~2.6 s, **99.5-99.8% is inside checker-init and 101-109% inside `checkSpine`**, while the other 416 pass rows and the front end are probe-FREE (top-20 `full`/`rows` ratios 0.63-1.35, no sign). Two consequences a reader of any older table must carry: **a warm-up RATIO taken from a `full` table is compressed for the row the probe lands on** — `checkSpine` really warms **3.46x** and the tail passes **2.59x**, where § 4 reads 2.24x vs 2.38x — and **the probe's OWN cost warms up** (first instrumented rebuild in a process 3,457 ms, second 1,856 ms, sign-consistent in two processes), so pricing it needs >=2 instrumented rebuilds per process, never one. `BenchMain <proj> <warmup> <iters> rows,spine,full,rows,spine,full` is the differential; the tier flags default to the pre-846 behaviour, so `cost_gate.py` and every counter pin are unchanged, and a reduced tier's zeros come with a header saying they are ABSENT measurements.
- **A JVM THAT SHELLS OUT TO `jcmd` WITH *ITS OWN* PID IS ATTACHING TO ITSELF, AND IT HANGS — MEASURED 30 MINUTES, AFTER PRINTING A PLAUSIBLE *PARTIAL* TABLE AND THEN NOTHING (2026-08-25, the (INC.36) heap ladder).** It fails in the reassuring direction: a dead run reads exactly like a slow measurement. Take `GC.class_histogram`/`GC.heap_info` from an EXTERNAL helper process, asked through a file and with a HARD DEADLINE after which the measurement continues without it — `scripts/inc36-retention.sh` + `Inc36RetentionMain.histogram` are the reference pair, and a histogram is corroboration there, never the finding.
- **`jfr print` SILENTLY TRUNCATES EVERY STACK TO ITS TOP 5 FRAMES, AND `scripts/aggregate_jfr.py` DOES NOT PASS `--stack-depth` — so its "INCLUSIVE time by method" table is, by default, the inclusive time *within five frames of the leaf* (round 868).** The tell is arithmetic that cannot be reconciled with this arc's own attribution (`checkSpine` read **15.6%** inclusive against the known ~74%); the printed stack merely ends in `...`. Pass `--stack-depth 512` and assert `max(len(stack))` is below the cap before believing any inclusive number or caller attribution. LEAF (frame-0) numbers are unaffected — but they are **not stable across processes**: `HashMap.getNode` reads 9.66% in one JVM and 3.70% in another on the SAME binary because C2 inlined it in the second, so attribute stdlib work to its nearest non-stdlib CALLER (that replicates to a few tenths of a percent) and filter samples to the compile thread (`xtsc-deep-stack`), since ~3% belong to the JFR recorder and the crawl's coroutine workers.
- A JFR leaf-frame self-% is NOT a wall-clock price (round 623): `computeLineStarts` showed 5.3% of samples; eliminating it (lazy) measured −0.3% = neutral — counted-loop safepoint bias inflates tight-loop self samples, and parallel-crawl worker savings don't move the serial-dominated wall. Price any JFR self entry by interleaved A/B before acting on it (the round-618 JFR shares this bias class).
- JVM GC/heap flag hunting on the self-compile: DEAD (round 618) — G1 vs ParallelGC vs +Xms4g vs +UseCompactObjectHeaders (JDK 25, 3 interleaved rotations) all land within the drift band (medians 30.2–30.8 s); the workload is not GC-bound at -Xmx4g. Perf levers are structural (docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md), not flags.
- Deleting the legacy pass tail for wall time: DEAD as a lever (rounds 619/620) — the corpus emission census (PassLab censusMode) measured 417/440 tail passes emitting, and the round-620 HONEST disable experiment showed 20 of the 23 census-silent passes are ALSO corpus-pinned (26 test failures) — only 3 pure adders were deletable. Tail wall recovery is spine MIGRATION (M0.4). **Census-silence is deletion evidence ONLY for pure `diagnostics.add` passes: `censusByPass` records net-POSITIVE deltas (`c1 > c0`), so wipe-and-pin walkers (`removeAll` + `pinDiag`, net 0), rewriters (`diagnostics[i] = copy`), retractors (net < 0), and collectors (side-set producers) are census-silent while fully load-bearing.** Round 619's "23 deletion-ready" verdict was a FALSE GREEN manufactured by Inv0PassTimingTest's cleanup assigning `PassTiming.disabledPasses = emptySet()` — a test touching fork-global PassLab state (`disabledPasses`/`censusMode`) must SAVE-AND-RESTORE, never assign the default back (the wipe re-enables the lab's disables for every alphabetically-later test class = the whole generated corpus). Also: `build/pass-lab.txt` is NOT a Gradle task input — a lab-file change alone does not invalidate an up-to-date `jvmTest`; force `--rerun` for any lab experiment.
- Deleting the `checkGeneric*` pin-walker family as "superseded by the open generic gate": DEAD — 15/16 are still load-bearing (round 600 sweep: 21 corpus failures; their shapes are inference/constraint semantics the structural relation cannot decide). Only checkGenericFnTypeBipartition was deletable (its noStrictGenericChecks pin family is tsgo-skipped). Re-attempt a per-walker deletion only after generic INFERENCE (M3.1) lands, one walker at a time, suite-gated.

- A LIVE narrowing-walk fold at the two Type-returning entries (epoch-fenced + declaredType-identity + confirm-once): DEAD — gate-green but NOISE in the interleaved A/B (round 599; the confirm-once tax + 80k/111k fresh-epoch misses cap the served fraction); savableNanos measured 1.4s but unrealizable under this policy. Revive with INV.5 canonical types.
- A LIVE per-node getTypeOfExpression memo (whitelisted instance-stable results + confirm-once + the round-595 epoch): DEAD — gate-green but 1-3% SLOWER measured interleaved (round 596); **MECHANISM MEASURED round 665: the whole servable population is worth 30 ms** (71,310 outermost served calls at ~0.42 µs each) because the servable calls are the CHEAP TAIL — trivial identifiers/literals whose resolution is already cached — while the expensive calls (fresh minting, narrowing, relation work) are precisely the non-instance-stable ones the whitelist excludes. Any future estimate of a memo's value must price the SERVABLE population, never hits × a mean call cost (that error inflated this by 35×); the servable hits are the cheap calls, the expensive recompute is the non-memoizable fresh-minting/narrowing kinds, and the per-mutation epoch bookkeeping exceeds the savings. The epoch infrastructure + the --passTiming shadow memo REMAIN for future policy evaluation.
- Global variance analysis wired into the relation engine: DEAD — ~263 regressions (round 336); strictFunctionTypes variance lives in dedicated walkers. Revisit only as part of a full M3.1 relation redesign.
- A general PropertyAccess-receiver TS2339 fallback via resolved interface members (B153): NOT viable — merged/cross-file interface member resolution is incomplete; only the class-AST-chain path + two tightly-gated exceptions (B527/B330) work.
- **`getTypeOfSymbol` PERSISTS INTO `symbolTypes` ONLY WHEN THE *CALLER'S* INSTANTIATION CONTEXT IS EMPTY (`symbolTypeContextIsEmpty`, round 778) — the same three dimensions `getTypeFromTypeNodeCore`'s `cacheable` gate has always used.** Without it a member typed by a type parameter froze globally as `T` or as `any` depending on which file touched it first, i.e. on program order — invisible in every output diff until the crawl order changes. A context the worker installs FOR ITSELF (namespace push, own type params) is a function of the symbol and stays cacheable, so the gate must be read at ENTRY. **Any NEW global, id-keyed checker cache written during a resolution that consults ambient state needs the same gate**, or it is a program-order dependency with no diff to find it by.
- **AN UNCONSTRAINED TYPE PARAMETER AND `any` ARE INDISTINGUISHABLE TO A READ PROBE — use a WRITE or an ARGUMENT probe (round 761).** `const s: string = x.v` is silent for both, which is how a whole queue item came to say "resolves to `any`" about a value that was really the unsubstituted `T`. `x.v = "s"` prints the actual target type; renaming each declaration's parameter (`TBOX`/`TFWD`) then names WHICH one survived, i.e. which hop failed.

- **AN "ACCEPTANCE-ONLY" RELATION LEG *CAN* ADD A DIAGNOSTIC, BECAUSE THE RELATION FEEDS NARROWING — A TYPE THAT NOW RELATES GETS *SUBTRACTED* BY `typeGuardMemberDisjoint` AND THE REFERENCE COLLAPSES TO `never` (2026-08-27, (CHK.61c), built and reverted).** A merged-INTERSECTION-source acceptance leg consulted only after "some constituent relates" answered false closed its intended row and added `'parent' does not exist on type 'never'` on two profiles. Round 784's "a second chance can only turn a rejection into an acceptance, so nothing that already passed can start failing" is true of the RELATION and false of the COMPILER — price such a leg on the 8-profile grid, never on the argument.
- **A TARGET TYPE'S MEMBER TABLE IS LAZY, SO ANY NEW READER OF `collectTargetPropertyNames` / `type.members` MUST `resolveStructuredTypeMembers(target)` FIRST — OTHERWISE ITS VERDICT DEPENDS ON WHETHER AN EARLIER LINE IN THE FILE HAPPENED TO RESOLVE THAT TYPE (round 833).** The helper answers "bail" (null `members`) rather than failing, so the excess-property check silently did nothing at the first `satisfies I1` in a file and worked at the second. **A corpus baseline can MASK this exactly** — `typeSatisfaction.ts`'s excess literal is preceded by another `satisfies I1` that resolves the type on the way through — so only a hand-written pin whose fixture has ONE use of the target can see it.
- **`getPropertyOfType`'s UNION arm ANSWERS AN ASSIGNABILITY QUESTION ("the property exists only if EVERY constituent has it") AND THEN RETURNS ONE CONSTITUENT'S SYMBOL — SO IT IS THE WRONG HELPER FOR EVERY "WHERE/WHAT IS THIS MEMBER" QUESTION, AND IT FAILS SILENTLY IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (round 916).** A member present on one constituent only answers **null** (a real declaration, reported as absent), and one present on all N answers **the first** (N−1 declarations dropped). A consumer that needs the member ITSELF — go-to-definition, completions, any per-constituent display — must collect across `Type.Union`/`Type.Intersection` itself, as `typeCaptureCollectMembers` does; `getPropertyAcrossType` is intersection-only and has the same first-wins tail. Reaching a PRIMITIVE's or a bare type parameter's members is `getApparentType` (the wrapper interface / the constraint), and a NAMESPACE's, MODULE's or **ENUM's** members are on no type at all — they live in `Symbol.exports`, so a type-only member lookup is silent for all three.

- **tsgo 7.0.2 PRINTS `The last overload gave the following error.` FOR **EVERY** FAILING OVERLOAD SET (measured at 2, 3 AND 4 candidates) WHERE PRISTINE tsc PRINTS `Overload N of M, '<sig>', gave the following error.` PER CANDIDATE — 42 `typescript-repo` baselines carry `Overload 1 of 2,` against **4** carrying the last-overload form (2026-08-27, (CHK.56)).** So a TS2769 CHAIN measured only against tsgo reads as a one-line elaboration and would replace our (correct, pristine-shaped) per-candidate chain wholesale; round 938's law, on the diagnostic that has the most chain machinery in this repo. `tsxStatelessFunctionComponentOverload4.errors.txt` is the pristine oracle for a *no properties in common* subline INSIDE that per-candidate chain.
- **A WEAK-TYPE REFUSAL IS NEVER tsc's MESSAGE FOR AN **OBJECT-LITERAL** ARGUMENT: THE FRESHNESS/EXCESS CHECK RUNS ABOVE THE WEAK CHECK IN `isRelatedTo`, AND A FRESH LITERAL SHARING NO PROPERTY NAME WITH THE TARGET HAS **EVERY** PROPERTY EXCESS BY CONSTRUCTION (2026-08-27).** So the two rules can never both apply, and emitting the weak wording there puts the squiggle on the whole literal where tsc puts it on the offending property, one column right. Measured: `f({ zzzZ: 1 })` against two weak overloads is `Object literal may only specify known properties, and 'zzzZ' does not exist in type '…'` at the property. A NON-fresh source of the same type (`const o = { zzzZ: 1 }; f(o)`) is the weak wording — freshness, not shape, is the axis.
- **A UNION TARGET NAMES A CONSTITUENT IN THE WEAK MESSAGE **ONLY** WHEN EXACTLY ONE CONSTITUENT SURVIVES DROPPING `null`/`undefined`; TWO OR MORE AND tsc FALLS BACK TO THE ORDINARY ASSIGNABILITY WORDING NAMING THE WHOLE UNION (2026-08-27).** `{ zzzA?: null } | null` renders `'{ zzzA?: null | undefined; }'`, `{ zzzA?: null } | string` renders `Argument of type 'number' is not assignable to parameter of type 'string | { zzzA?: null | undefined; }'`. The VERDICT is a refusal either way — only the sentence differs — so a helper that answers the verdict says nothing about the wording and needs a second, separate question.

### Checker walker gotchas

- **THERE ARE *FOUR* WAYS A LOCAL CAN SHADOW A SAME-NAMED OUTER BINDING AND ONLY THREE WERE COVERED — A **BLOCK-SCOPED** DECLARATION INSIDE A **NESTED FUNCTION** SHADOWING AN **ENCLOSING FUNCTION'S** LOCAL WAS NONE OF THEM, AND `currentLocalTypes` KEPT THE ENCLOSING BINDING (2026-08-28, (CHK.71)(b); a shipped ours-only TS2322 at every assignment to the inner name, judged against the WRONG declaration's type).** The other three are round 351 `applyBodyLocalShadowing` (top-level decls), round 460 `applyAmbiguousBlockScopedLocals` (two decls in ONE body) and round 455 `applyNestedGlobalShadow` (a GLOBAL/file-level collision, whose condition `outerBound && !currentLocalTypes.containsKey(nm)` is the inherited case inverted). **A shadow reduction must separate all four** — three of them are silent and only the fourth fires.
- **…AND THE SHADOW PASSES RUN *BEFORE* THE BODY WALK ON A FRESHLY COPIED SCOPE, WHICH IS WHY "IS THIS NAME INHERITED?" NEEDS NO BOOKKEEPING: every entry standing in `currentLocalTypes` at that moment belongs to an ENCLOSING function (2026-08-28).** Record an ANNOTATED shadowing declaration's own annotation and an un-annotated one as `anyType` — a block-scoped INFERRED type must not be claimed for reads outside the block, and recording `anyType` for both is blanket suppression that a wrong-assignment pin catches.

- **`resolveFlowCalleeDecl` DOES NO OVERLOAD SELECTION — IT ANSWERS `valueDeclaration ?: declarations.firstOrNull()` — SO EVERY CONSUMER READING A *RETURN ANNOTATION* OFF IT ANSWERS ABOUT THE **FIRST** SIGNATURE, WHICHEVER ONE THE CALL SELECTS (2026-08-28, (CHK.72)(a); universal — an implementation-bearing overload set, an interface method pair and a `declare namespace` member all read first-wins, and ARITY alone does not discriminate).** The whole call path (`getReturnTypeOfCallExpression`) resolved the overload correctly the entire time, so a DIRECT read of the call is right while a local whose type is INFERRED from it is wrong — which makes a direct-call fixture vacuous for this class. Two shipped failure modes: the post-overwrite reset installs a wrong TYPE, and the structural non-nullish claim STRIPS a `| undefined` the selected overload genuinely has.
- **…AND REFUSING AN UNJUSTIFIED CLAIM IS NOT FREE WHEN THE CLAIM IS WHAT *SUPPRESSES* A DIAGNOSTIC — merely declining `callRhsHasNonNullishReturnAnnotation` for an overload set cost one ours-only TS2322 on EVERY profile, because `factory.getGeneratedNameForNode` is two overloads that BOTH return `Identifier` (2026-08-28).** The suite was green and only the 8-profile grid saw it. Answer from the SELECTED overload rather than declining; "conservative" is a direction, not a safety proof.

- **UNIONING A LOOP LABEL'S BACK EDGES MAKES EVERY SUBTREE ENTRY-CONTEXT-DEPENDENT AND THEREFORE UNMEMOIZABLE, AND *THAT* — NOT THE EXTRA TRAVERSAL — IS ~90% OF ITS COST: `globals.lookups` 771,681 -> **15,128,215**, of which restoring the memo returns 89.2% (2026-08-28, (CHK.69), REFUSED).** A SOUND cut-keyed memo (a rolling hash of the in-progress label set as an extra equality field on every `NarrowFlowMemo` entry) recovers **0.003%**, because **the cycle almost never closes ON the loop label — it closes on the walk's OWN PREFIX** whenever the query sits inside or after the loop, and that answer is a function of the path. **The affordable shape is the fixpoint argument used forward**: `L = E union (union of narrow_i(L))`, so when no back edge ASSIGNS the reference every back edge is a pure narrowing and **the fixpoint IS the entry state** — answer the label by following `antecedents[0]`, decided by a pure graph-reachability scan (`loopBodyMayAffectName`) that resolves no type. Do not re-open the back-edge union without a new mechanism for the prefix-dependence.
- **A `for-in` / `for-of` MUST LEAVE THROUGH ITS *LOOP LABEL*, NOT AROUND IT — `currentFlow = loopLabel` COMES BEFORE `joinAntecedent(postLoop, currentFlow)` (tsc's `bindForInOrForOfStatement`), AND THE OTHER ORDER MAKES THE LOOP BODY UNREACHABLE **BACKWARD** FROM EVERY READ AFTER THE LOOP (2026-08-28, (CHK.69), a shipped false negative).** `for (const n of xs) { h.req = 1 }` then did not invalidate a narrow established before it; `while` / `do` / `for(;;)` never had it, because they exit through their condition, which carries the label. It is invisible to the 8 profiles and to the corpus and shows only against tsc on a hand-written shape.

- **`CtaFrame.narrowedDeclared` IS *SHARED* DOWN THE FRAME CHAIN AND HAS NO UNDO LOG, SO A WRITE INTO IT ESCAPES THE BLOCK, THE FUNCTION **AND THE FILE** — AND THE SYMPTOM IS AN ASSIGNMENT WHOSE *TARGET* IS READ AS SOME OTHER FUNCTION'S SAME-NAMED BINDING (2026-08-28, (CHK.64): 21 ours-only rows PER PROFILE, `type` read as `Type`, `source` as a `Map<…>`).** `ctaFnBodyFrame` passes `narrowedDeclared = base.narrowedDeclared`, so it is not even per-function; only a NARROWING frame takes a copy. A new writer must replace it with a copy of its own first (`CtaFrame.narrowedDeclaredOwned`), and must write FIRST-WINS — an unconditional write under NESTED narrows records the OUTER narrow's result as if it were the declaration, which was a shipped false TS2322. `localTypes` by contrast IS undo-logged, but only for a frame whose `localScoped` is true: a statement-position `Block` SHARES its parent's map, which is why an install there is refused rather than scoped.
- **A NARROWING PIN WHOSE SOURCE IS A *NULLISH* UNION AND WHOSE TARGET IS A *PRIMITIVE* IS VACUOUS IN BOTH ARMS — `canUseTypeEngine` refuses that pair wholesale ((CHK.63)), so the un-narrowed read is silent too (2026-08-28).** The round's own inherited repro (`build/chk61b/n1`) was silent on our compiler AND on tsc for exactly this reason; a NON-NULLISH union (`number | string` narrowed by `typeof`) makes the same gaps appear on the shipped binary with no patch. Corollary: truthiness narrowing only ever removes `null`/`undefined`, so the `!`-guard family is *structurally* unobservable at those readers until that gate opens.
- **`typeofTypeGuardToType` ANSWERS `anyType` FOR `"object"` AND `"function"`, SO A `typeof x === "object"` "NARROWING" IS A ***WIDENING*** — AND `any` IS ASSIGNABLE TO EVERYTHING, SO INSTALLING IT DELETES TRUE POSITIVES SILENTLY (2026-08-28).** Any new consumer of [Checker.extractNullNarrowing] must drop an `anyType` answer; the SINGLE-condition path still installs it and that is a known shipped false negative (`build/chk64/c4`), not a licence to copy.

- **A PREDICATE CONSULTED PER ***FLOW CALL*** MAY NOT RESOLVE ITS CALLEE THROUGH `resolvePropertyMethodDecl` — THAT **TYPES THE RECEIVER**, AND IT MEASURED `typeOfExpr.calls` **+9.61%** / `globals.lookups` **+4.43%** (2026-08-27, (CHK.62); the round-385 hazard `flowCalleeMayHaveAssertEffects` was written to avoid, one predicate over).** The symbol-table-only `resolveNamespaceMemberFnDecl` covers the shape that matters (`Debug.assertNever`) at `typeOfExpr.calls` **+0.00%**, and pre-gating its RECEIVER on `currentFileLocals` — a namespace that can name such a function is an import or a file-level declaration, a parameter never is — is what takes `globals.lookups` back under the gate. **A per-node memo there measured EXACTLY ZERO**: such a call is asked about once per compile, so the cost was never repetition.
- **A HANDED-DOWN DIAGNOSIS NAMES AN AXIS THE REPRO HAPPENED TO HAVE — **2 OF 4 WERE WRONG IN ONE ROUND**, both toward a plausible-but-innocent feature (2026-08-27, (CHK.62)).** "A function-type PROPERTY's parameters are compared covariantly" was really OPTIONALITY (our contravariance is correct, and the METHOD form named as the control fails identically); "a SHORTHAND object-literal property does not flow-narrow" was really a diverging call (the explicit `p: p` form fails identically). Both were overturned by two 20-second CLI runs. **Re-bisect before designing the fix**, and note the corollary that cost this round a build: a queue entry can be right that a mechanism is broken, right that a row exists, and wrong that they are the same thing — only re-pricing the unmasking patch says so.

- **AN ENUM-FLAVOURED TYPE IS A MEMBER-*LESS* `Type.Object`, SO `propertiesRelatedTo`'s `source.members == null` ARM ANSWERS `targetProps.isEmpty()` — WHICH REJECTS AN ALL-OPTIONAL (WEAK) TARGET AS FIRMLY AS A REQUIRED ONE AND ACCEPTS AN EMPTY / INDEX-SIGNATURE ONE VACUOUSLY (2026-08-27, (CHK.60)).** So an enum source's verdict is decided by whether the TARGET declares anything, never by what the enum is — 13 measured false positives one way, four false negatives the other. In tsc an enum literal type carries `StringLiteral`/`NumberLiteral` beside `EnumLiteral` and its apparent type is the `String`/`Number` wrapper; ours carries neither bit, so the flavour must be recovered from the member's COMPUTED VALUE (`enumLiteralApparentPrimitive`), and the WHOLE enum type has no such value and stays vacuous.
- **AN ALL-OPTIONAL TARGET CANNOT DISCRIMINATE A SOURCE'S *FLAVOUR*, BECAUSE A SOURCE LACKING AN OPTIONAL TARGET MEMBER IS LEGAL — SO A FLAVOUR RULE PINNED ONLY ON WEAK TARGETS IS A BLIND PIN SET (2026-08-27, (CHK.60), arm a4).** Collapsing string-vs-number to one answer reddened exactly ONE pin of eleven, and it was a WRAPPER-interface control (`Number` refuses a string member), not any of the weak-target positives. Pin a flavour on a target the wrong flavour genuinely fails.
