- **tsc's WEAK-TYPE CHECK LIVES INSIDE `isRelatedTo` AND OURS LIVES IN THE B482 WALKERS, SO ANY DECISION PORTED FROM tsc THAT ASKS "IS X RELATED TO Y" MUST ADD `weakParamRefusesArg` BY HAND — `checkTypeRelatedTo` ALONE ANSWERS "ASSIGNABLE" FOR *EVERY* NON-NULLISH SOURCE AGAINST AN ALL-OPTIONAL TARGET (2026-08-27, (CHK.58)).** That vacuous yes is why TS2560 *Did you mean to call it?* was emitted for every callable source: tsc offers it only when CALLING the source yields something RELATED to the target, and `() => number` against a weak object is TS**2559** there and was 2560 here. Same trap for any future port of `reportErrorResults`, `elaborateError` or an overload-ranking rule.
- **A BASELINE CAN AGREE WITH A WRONG IMPLEMENTATION BECAUSE OF A PROPERTY OF ITS *FIXTURE* — pristine's `nestedExcessPropertyChecking` says `Type 'E'` for an `E.A` source and its enum has exactly ONE member, where the enum type and the member's literal type ARE the same type (2026-08-27, (CHK.58)).** Every multi-member enum rendered `E` where tsc renders `E.A`, invisibly. Before "fixing" a display the corpus gates, vary the fixture along the axis the baseline happens to pin flat.
- **A DIAGNOSTIC'S SPAN IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THE POSITION — tsc's `elaborateError` RUNS *BEFORE* THE ORDINARY REPORT AND REPLACES THE ERROR NODE WITH THE **EXPRESSION**, so the anchor is decided by WHICH ELABORATION FIRED (2026-08-27, (CHK.59); (CHK.58)'s "a CALLABLE source anchors at the expression" is the special case and is CORRECTED here).** For the weak rule the elaboration is `elaborateDidYouMeanToCallOrConstruct`, whose predicate is exactly the TS2560-vs-TS2559 one — so **TS2560 => the expression, TS2559 => the var NAME / the `return` keyword (6 chars) / the LHS reference**, and a callable source whose result is DISJOINT keeps the ordinary anchor. Anything ported from `checkTypeRelatedToAndOptionallyElaborate` inherits this; `getErrorSpanForNode` is the other half (it maps a `FunctionExpression` to its own NAME, an `ArrowFunction` to its first token).
- **THE SAME FRESH LITERAL RENDERS DIFFERENTLY BY *DEPTH*: `"utf8"` AT A TOP-LEVEL VAR DECL AND `string` ONE LEVEL INTO AN OBJECT LITERAL — AND A BOOLEAN LITERAL WIDENS AT NEITHER (2026-08-27, (CHK.59), measured over tsc 7.0.2 and gated by pristine's `nestedExcessPropertyChecking` lines 18 vs 30/40).** A leaf is reported through the LITERAL'S OWN property type, which a mutable location has already widened for the string/number/bigint cases; the top level hands the fresh literal straight to the relation. So a display rule read off one depth must never be applied at the other, and the boolean case is the control that separates "literals widen" from the real rule.

- **A "SECOND CHANCE ON THE REJECTING PATH" IS UNREACHABLE FOR A UNION TARGET WITH A *WEAK* CONSTITUENT, BECAUSE AN ALL-OPTIONAL OBJECT ACCEPTS ANY NON-NULLISH VALUE STRUCTURALLY — THE RELATION *SUCCEEDS* AND THE FAILURE PATH NEVER RUNS (2026-08-27, (CHK.55)).** Round 728's `objLitLiteralPropsSatisfyParam` sits there for cost, so (CHK.54)'s `weakParamRefusesArg` refused signatures the rescue would have saved. **A rule that REFUSES AFTER SUCCESS must be guarded by every rule that RESCUES AFTER FAILURE at the same site** — found only by building a falsifier for an ablation arm that read 0 RED.
- **OVERLOAD SELECTION (`signatureAcceptsArgs`) AND THE TS2769 MATCHER (`allArgumentsMatch`) MUST ASK THE SAME QUESTION, AND WHEN THEY DIVERGE THE TWO SYMPTOMS LOOK LIKE UNRELATED DEFECTS (2026-08-27, (CHK.55)).** A rescue only the MATCHER has gives a **wrong type** through `resolveCallOverload`'s `arityMatches[0]` fallback (no diagnostic anywhere); a refusal only SELECTION has gives a **false TS2769**. One fixture carrying both is what identifies them as one mechanism. Same law as the archive's `restAwareParamType`/`overloadNarrowedArgType` entry — add any new argument-acceptance rule to BOTH.

- **`resolveCallOverload` FALLS BACK TO `arityMatches[0]` WHEN NOTHING ACCEPTS, SO A PIN ABOUT A *REFUSAL* IS VACUOUS WHENEVER THE REFUSED OVERLOAD IS DECLARED **FIRST** — THE FALLBACK RESTORES EXACTLY THE ANSWER THE REFUSAL WAS SUPPOSED TO REMOVE (2026-08-26, (CHK.54): three refusal pins read 0 RED against three arms that demonstrably changed the selection).** Declare the overload under test SECOND, so a wrong refusal picks the OTHER one and a write probe moves. Same shape as round 902's dead-arm law, one layer up: here the arm is live and the pin cannot see it.
- **THE WEAK-TYPE RULE LIVES IN THE B482 *WALKERS*, NOT IN `checkTypeRelatedTo`, SO OUR RELATION ANSWERS "ASSIGNABLE" FOR A SOURCE SHARING NO PROPERTY WITH AN ALL-OPTIONAL TARGET AND EVERY OTHER CONSUMER INHERITS THAT (2026-08-26, (CHK.54); `{ e?: null } = "utf8"` is TS2559 both sides, `{ e?: null } | null = "utf8"` is TS2559 in tsc and SILENT here).** The pure verdict is `weakParamRefusesArg` (per union constituent, as tsc's `typeRelatedToSomeType`); `signatureAcceptsArgs` asks it, `allArgumentsMatch` still does not ((CHK.55)). A new relation consumer that must reject a weak-disjoint source has to ask it too — the relation will not.
- **tsc's "SPECIALIZED SIGNATURE" TEST (`reorderCandidates`, GH#1133) IS **SYNTACTIC** — `param.type.kind === SyntaxKind.LiteralType` — SO A LITERAL *UNION* PARAMETER DOES NOT SPECIALIZE AND NEITHER DOES AN ALIAS TO ONE (2026-08-26, (CHK.54b), measured against tsc 7.0.2 in both directions).** `specializedFirst` reproduces it from the AST; deriving it from the RESOLVED type hoists both and diverges the other way. **Nothing in this repo except `OverloadSpecializedOrderTest` can see the rule** — corpus, all 20 cost counters, the 8-profile grid, both equivalence sweeps and both library arms are byte-identical across it.
- **A `declare global { … }` BLOCK IS A ModuleDeclaration NAMED `global`, SO THE *CARRIER* MERGES INTO `globals` AND ITS `exports` DO NOT UNLESS SOMETHING MERGES THEM — AND `globalAugmentationNames` MAKES THE BROKEN FORMS LOOK HEALTHY, BECAUSE IT IS A **SUPPRESSION** LIST (2026-08-26, (CHK.50)).** A form on that list emits no TS2304 and is typed by NOTHING (`any`); a form not on it is TS2304. So `class`/`function`/`namespace` read as fine and were the worse half — only a WRITE probe (`const s: string = <it>`) separates the three. `init:mergeGlobalAugmentations` is the merge; a new consumer of global augmentations must mirror `spineCheckGlobalAugmentation`'s TS2669 legality predicate (descend only through ambient-module bodies), because a top-level block in a global SCRIPT augments nothing in tsc either.
- **PUBLISHING A NAME INTO `globals` IS HALF A FIX: THE UNRESOLVED-NAME FAMILY'S FILE ROOT IS `perFileScope`, WHICH IS SEEDED FROM `libGlobals` + SCRIPT LOCALS + OWN LOCALS AND **NOT** FROM `globals` — so a name can resolve as a TYPE and still be TS2304 in the same compile (2026-08-26, (CHK.50)).** Two resolvers, one question, neither wrong on its own inputs. Anything that adds a program-wide name after `init:mergeFileLocalsIntoGlobals` must seed `buildPerFileScopes` too, or the type is right and the name "does not exist".
- **`namespace globalThis { … }` IS NOT A NAMESPACE DECLARATION — it is tsc's syntax for augmenting the GLOBAL SCOPE ITSELF, so its members become bare globals and `globalThis` never becomes an ordinary symbol (2026-08-26, (CHK.50), corpus `extendGlobalThis`).** Publishing it as one makes `globalThis.<anything undeclared>` a TS2339 pristine tsc does not report; no profile and no library carries the shape, so the corpus is the only instrument.
- **MAKING A TYPE *REAL* SURFACES EVERY DEFECT `any` WAS HIDING, SO A RISING ROW COUNT ON A LIBRARY ARM IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY A REGRESSION — DECOMPOSE PER ROW AND ATTRIBUTE EACH TO A BINARY (2026-08-26, (CHK.50): knip 49 -> 54 is ONE fix and SIX pre-existing overload rows).** The instrument is a repro with the round's own mechanism REMOVED: the six rows reproduce with the interface declared as a plain local, identically on the parent and the landed binary. Round 789's law at library scale.

- **A STRUCTURAL RELATION AGAINST A *MEMBER-LESS* `Type.Object` PASSES VACUOUSLY, SO ANY NEW LEG THAT *ACCEPTS* AGAINST AN OBJECT TARGET MUST DEMAND THE TARGET DECLARE SOMETHING — (REL.1)(b)'s enum rule is the special case, not the rule (2026-08-26, (CHK.32) paid 13 tests for it).** Every enum-flavoured type is such an object, so a `Number`-wrapper-to-enum comparison "succeeds"; the general guard is a members/signatures/index-infos test taken BELOW `resolveStructuredTypeMembers` (round 833 — both tables are lazy). An `Enum`/`EnumLiteral` flag test beside it is a round-927 PAIR with that guard, not a substitute: dropping either alone reddens nothing.
- **AND AN *INDEX-SIGNATURE* TARGET IS DECIDED BY WHETHER THE SOURCE SUPPLIES A MATCHING INDEXER (B418), NOT STRUCTURALLY — a structural comparison against `{ [k: string]: any }` accepts almost anything, because every property conforms to an `any`-typed indexer (2026-08-26).** So a new acceptance leg must leave an index-signature target alone; `assignmentCompat1` is the baseline that notices, and it is the only one.

- **A RECEIVER THAT REPORTS NOTHING IS USUALLY TYPED CORRECTLY — THE PROPERTY-ACCESS FAMILY HAS ITS OWN CONSUMERS AND THEY BAIL ON `anyType` PER RECEIVER SHAPE, SO A DESTRUCTURED NAME, A NESTED ACCESS AND AN UN-ANNOTATED BODY-LOCAL WERE EACH SILENT WHILE A WRITE PROBE (`const p: number = x`) ANSWERED THE RIGHT TYPE ON THE SAME BINARY (2026-08-26, (CHK.46)).** Probe the TYPE before reading a missing diagnostic as a typing gap: `getTypeOfSymbol` has NO `BindingElement` arm, `getTypeOfIdentifier` reads `currentLocalTypes` which that pass does not populate for a body-local, and `cmamGeneralReceiverType`'s `objectExpr !is Identifier` branch has no single-Object emission at all.
- **`narrowByInOperator`'s NON-UNION ARM DELIBERATELY ANSWERS THE **UNCHANGED** TYPE, SO NO `narrowed !== raw` TEST CAN SEE AN `in` GUARD — AND `if ('p' in o) { o.p }` IS LEGAL (tsc narrows to `T & Record<'p', unknown>`) (2026-08-26).** Any new member-existence emission for a non-union receiver must walk the flow antecedents for an `in` condition naming its own property on its own path (`cmamInGuardMayAddProperty`), or it is a false positive on idiomatic duck-typing that no corpus baseline contains.
- **TO FIND WHICH PASS EMITS A DIAGNOSTIC, RUN `--passTiming` OVER A MINIMAL SCRATCH PROJECT AND READ ITS `emissions by pass` TABLE (2026-08-26).** A DUPLICATE row names two passes, and a dedupe written in the one that runs FIRST cannot see the other — the identity test has to live in whichever pass runs SECOND, which the table is the only thing that tells you.

- **A UNION TS2339 HAS TWO VERDICTS WITH DIFFERENT SOUNDNESS REQUIREMENTS, AND ONLY ONE NEEDS A TRUST GATE: *PARTIAL* COVERAGE CARRIES ITS OWN WITNESS (some member answered "the property IS there", so its table resolved), *ALL-MISSING* HAS NONE — every member said "no", and a "no" from an incomplete member table is B153 (2026-08-26, (CHK.45)).** So all-missing is gated per member by `cmamAllMissingTrustedMember`; **removing that gate entirely is `added=0 removed=0` on all eight profiles AND moves ZERO of the ~13k corpus baselines, and still costs 2 false positives on knip** — a cross-file interface WITH a heritage clause. Read a green grid+corpus on any member-table-trusting change as a control, never as coverage.
- **BEFORE ATTRIBUTING A RECEIVER-SHAPED GAP TO *WHERE THE RECEIVER IS DECLARED*, RUN THE IDENTICAL SOURCE AT ALL THREE SITES — parameter, file-level `const`, body-local — BECAUSE THREE CONSECUTIVE ROUNDS DID NOT AND ALL THREE MIS-ATTRIBUTED IT (2026-08-26).** (CHK.41) read "only a parameter is checked" off a probe whose receiver was named `top` (a DOM global); (CHK.44) then read "a member on NO constituent is a different emitter" off a body-local; (CHK.45) measured all three sites and found the SAME emitter and a member-kind whitelist. Destructured bindings and nested single-object receivers are silent at all three sites too — i.e. equally not scoping gaps.
- **A TUPLE IS AN ANONYMOUS OBJECT CARRYING A *NUMBER* INDEX SIGNATURE, SO "any index signature provides the property" LOSES EVERY TUPLE (2026-08-26).** Round 479's rule is the right one and now has one home, `cmamIndexSignatureProvides`: a STRING index signature supplies every name, a NUMBER one only a numeric-literal name.

- **`currentLocalTypes` IS A FLAT *COPY* OF THE ENCLOSING SCOPE, SO A PARAMETER THAT NOTHING REGISTERS DOES NOT READ AS `any` — IT READS AS THE ENCLOSING SCOPE'S SAME-NAMED BINDING, AND A DELIBERATELY-SHADOWING CALLBACK PARAMETER THEN RESOLVES TO THE THING IT SHADOWS (2026-08-26, (CHK.42); `flatMap(exportInfo, (exportInfo, i) => …)` in tsc's own importFixes.ts, reported as a TS2322 tsc does not have).** Every "we cannot type this parameter, skip it" decision therefore needs a `currentLocalTypes[name] = anyType` shadow — round 475's rule for binding patterns, now a pre-pass in `ctaTypeParamsIntoLocals` — and it must run BEFORE the contextual write, never after (a post-pass clobbers every contextually-typed parameter in the program: 22 pins).
- **AN ASSERTION'S VALUE IS THE *ASSERTED* TYPE AND FALLING BACK TO THE OPERAND'S IS UNCONDITIONALLY WRONG — for `x as unknown as T` the operand's type is `unknown`, i.e. the type the outer assertion exists to assert away (2026-08-26, (CHK.43)).** `inferSimpleExprType` now answers null where `resolveSimpleTypeName` cannot render the asserted type, which makes the caller skip its string check; the ENGINE above still decides every assertion whose source genuinely does not relate. Read a narrow-looking trigger there as a property of the string fallback's REACHABILITY (it runs only after the engine declines, i.e. only when the source really does relate), not of the shape.

- **AN `async` FUNCTION-LIKE'S *INFERRED* RETURN TYPE IS `Promise<T>` AND EIGHT SEPARATE SITES COMPUTE IT — A NINTH WRITTEN WITHOUT `asyncWrapInferredReturn` SILENTLY ANSWERS THE BODY'S TYPE, WHICH IS A FALSE POSITIVE AT EVERY `Promise` TARGET **AND** A FALSE NEGATIVE AT EVERY UNWRAPPED ONE (2026-08-26, (CHK.40); the eight are the declaration, arrow, fn-expr, class method, objlit method, `buildMethodType` + its two declaration-carrying callers, and `getReturnTypeOfCallable`).** Nothing here notices: it moved no corpus baseline, no `cost_gate.py` counter and no dashboard row. `globalPromiseType` is wired in `init:wireGlobalArrayTypes` rather than on first ask because a lib interface's member table is lazy ((INC.25)); a lib without `Promise` leaves the type un-wrapped, which is the pre-fix behaviour and not an error.
- **A MEMBER NAME EXTRACTED AS `decl.name as? Identifier` IS THE REPEAT OFFENDER OF THIS CHECKER, AND ITS *QUIETEST* COSTUME IS `anyType` RATHER THAN A MISSING MEMBER — `getTypeOfSymbolWorker`'s MethodDeclaration arm typed every STRING-named method `any` while `resolveInterfaceMembersCore` had registered it correctly, so `interface I { "m-x"(n: N): void }` had the member PRESENT and useless (2026-08-26, (CHK.40)(c); round 937 found the COMPUTED half of the same line and left this one).** `declaredMemberName` is the single source of truth — use it, so the extraction site and the REGISTRATION site cannot disagree about which member this is; the property form of the same member is byte-correct, which is what makes the divergence invisible to any fixture that does not spell the method form.

- **A `getTypeOfSymbol` CALL MADE IN ORDER TO *WARM* A CACHE IS A NO-OP WHEREVER THE AMBIENT INSTANTIATION CONTEXT IS NON-EMPTY — ROUND 778's WRITE GATE REFUSES IT — SO EVERY "force it, then let a RAW `symbolTypes[id]` reader read it back" PATTERN SILENTLY DOES NOTHING INSIDE A `namespace` BODY, A TYPE-PARAM SCOPE OR AN ALIAS-ARG INSTALL (2026-08-22, (INC.6); it cost (INC.5) four capture divergences and its own pin was green because that fixture sits at FILE level).** The resolution SUCCEEDS and is thrown away, and `typeToString`'s anonymous-object branch then prints `any` — no diagnostic, no counter, nothing but a hover moves. **A symbol a materializer MINTS must therefore have its type written AT MINT TIME, ungated** (`resolveReferenceMembers`' idiom, now also `materializeModifierUtility`'s): that is sound where the gated write is not, because the id is reachable only from the one type just built, so the frozen value can never be read as another symbol's.

- **A STATEMENT NESTED IN A FUNCTION BODY IS EMISSION-OWNED BY THE *LEGACY* WALK — THE SPINE'S OWN ANCHOR RUNS `recordOnly` FOR IT AND TRUNCATES EVERY DIAGNOSTIC — SO A TYPE WRITTEN ONLY INTO THE SPINE FRAME (`ctaFnBodyFrame`) IS CORRECT AND *COMPLETELY INVISIBLE* (2026-08-25, (CHK.39)).** A fix that types a name for a function body needs BOTH `checkFunctionBody` (which emits) and the frame (which a language-service capture reads); the ablation partitions them exactly and neither is redundant. The instrument that shows it is a print of `(walker, currentLocalTypes[name], initType)` at `checkVarDeclAssignabilityCore` — the same declaration is visited TWICE, `walkFunctionBodiesInExpr` with the name ABSENT and `anchor(recordOnly=true)` with it typed. **And a contextual type must be resolved OUTSIDE the function's own TP scope** in both places: it is written outside the function, and INV.5(c) bypasses its cache under a non-empty instantiation context.
- **THE PROPERTY-ACCESS FAMILY READS ITS CONTEXTUAL TYPE FROM A DIFFERENT SOURCE THAN THE ASSIGNABILITY FAMILY, AND GIVING IT MORE OF ONE MANUFACTURES TS2339 BECAUSE IT HAS NO ASSIGNMENT/`typeof` NARROWING FOR A PARAMETER (2026-08-25, (CHK.39c), REFUSED at +15 knip FPs).** A parameter whose contextual type is a UNION that the body then narrows by assignment (`if (typeof x === 'function') x = x()`) reports on the un-narrowed union; **all 8 dashboard profiles stay `added=0 removed=0` on that change**, so the grid is structurally blind to the whole family — only a real library sees it. Fix the narrowing before re-applying the two sources ((CHK.41)). A `VariableDeclaration` arm added to `cpaCtxAt` is a DEAD leg: it computes the right type and reaches the frame and changes nothing, because that walker's emitter runs under the ANCHOR's ambient through the legacy `cpaExprArrowFunction`.
- **JAVASCRIPT FUNCTION ASSIGNABILITY ACCEPTS A *LOWER-ARITY* FUNCTION, SO A KIR CALL OF A FUNCTION VALUE MAY NEVER BE A DIRECT `FunctionN.invoke` — IT MUST GO THROUGH `jsCallN` (2026-08-25).** A value declared `(a, b) => void` is perfectly likely to be a `Function1` at run time (mitt's driver is the shipped instance), so a `coerce(…, types.function(arity))` is a hard cast that throws `ClassCastException` with nothing in the type system being wrong. It stayed invisible while such a callee was `any`; **any checker change that gives a callee a real function type can wake this class of defect in the backend**, and the corpus tests are the only thing that sees it.
- **NARROWABILITY IS DECIDED PER CONSUMER, NOT BY THE MACHINERY — every flow-reading call site carries its own NODE-KIND gate, so a kind the whole narrowing stack supports can still read the declared type everywhere (round 780).** An `ElementAccessExpression` with a literal index has had a `getReferencePath` since round 461, gets a flow node from `Flow.kt`'s own arm, and the walk compares path STRINGS — yet `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature`'s four flow-reading arms all tested `arg is Identifier || arg is PropertyAccessExpression`, and `getTypeOfElementAccess` applied no narrowing at all until round 942 gave it the RECEIVER half (its `getTypeOfPropertyAccess` twin has had both halves since B1.1/17.34d; the RESULT half was measured INERT there and deliberately not added — see the entry below). So never conclude "this shape is not narrowable" from the shape: ask whether the READ happened, by probing the same subject as an identifier and as a property access in one scratch run.
- **A SUPPRESSION WRITTEN AT AN *EMITTER* IS INVISIBLE TO THE DIRECTIVE ACCOUNTING, AND SINCE (CHK.31) THAT MAKES IT A FALSE **TS2578** — `// @ts-ignore` / `// @ts-expect-error` ARE FILTERED ONCE, AT `Checker.getDiagnostics()`, AND NOWHERE ELSE (2026-08-25).** A diagnostic a walker declines to EMIT because a directive sits above it marks nothing, so the directive reads as unused and `Unused '@ts-expect-error' directive.` is reported at a line the author deliberately silenced — measured on `isolatedModulesExportDeclarationType`, where the commonjs relative-import branch pre-suppressed its own TS2307. **Never gate an emission on a `@ts-*` comment; emit, and let the funnel drop it.** Both halves of that funnel are scoped to `checkedResultsAll` (the files this checker WALKED) and not to the program: an unchecked file has no diagnostics for its `@ts-expect-error` to suppress, so program-wide scoping manufactures one false positive per directive on every narrowed build — the hazard is unreachable from `diagnose()` and is pinned by two `assignedFileNames` fixtures in `CommentDirectiveSuppressionTest`.
- **AN ABLATION THAT RESTORES THE *SOURCE* LEAVES THE *CLASS DIR* HOLDING THE LAST ARM, AND THE NEXT THING YOU RUN MEASURES THAT ARM — A DRAMATIC, FULLY REPRODUCIBLE "FRESH DEFECT" IN CODE THAT IS FINE (2026-08-25, ~15 minutes).** `git status` is CLEAN, so every check the standing protocol suggests passes; the tell is a CLI probe disagreeing with a suite that was green minutes earlier on the same shape. **Rebuild after the restore, in the driver**, and verify with round 947's positive control — `javap -p -cp <class dir> <Class> | grep <a method the round just added>` settles source-vs-binary in one second, where `./gradlew … -q 2>&1 | grep -E '^(e:|w:)'` prints nothing for a compile that never ran.
- **THE EIGHT DASHBOARD PROFILES HOLD **ZERO** `package.json` FILES BETWEEN THEM AND THE CORPUS HARNESS MATERIALISES NO DIRECTORY AT ALL — SO EVERY GATE IN THIS REPO IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO ANYTHING DECIDED BY A FILE THAT IS NOT A PROGRAM INPUT (2026-08-25, (CHK.29): 2,478 false positives on one library, 94.1% of its errors, from one absent lookup).** A file's module format under `nodenext` comes from the nearest enclosing `package.json` `"type"`; the consumer existed and its only producer read the CORPUS's parsed source set, which a real project has none of. **The instrument for that whole class is a `-project` fixture through `ProjectCompiler` + a `Vfs`** — a `diagnose()` pin is vacuous by construction (no directory, no `package.json`), and a green suite / `+0.00%` / `added=0 removed=0` are the EXPECTED answers, i.e. controls. Two rules the fix forced, both read out of tsgo rather than guessed: a manifest with NO `"type"` **establishes** the scope at CommonJS (the walk stops at the first one it meets — an ABSENT map key is the different fact that continues the walk), and it must be parsed as JSON, because a real manifest's FIRST `"type"` match is usually `repository.type: "git"`.
- **A FLOW-NARROWED REFERENCE READ IN AN *OBJECT LITERAL* AND READ AT A *VAR DECL* ARE TWO DIFFERENT INSTRUMENTS, AND THE WRITE PROBE CLAUDE.md RECOMMENDS IS THE **BLIND** ONE FOR AN ASSIGNMENT NARROW (2026-08-27, (CHK.62b)).** At one flow point `const q: T = p` was SILENT on a broken binary while `return { p }` was a TS2322 — so a pin written as a write probe measures nothing. The instrument that names the TYPE is a deliberately WRONG object-literal target (`return { p }` against `{ p: string }`): the message then prints the member type the checker actually built (`{ p: T | undefined; }` vs `{ p: T; }`), which is a VALUE pin and not an absence pin.
- **AN ACCEPTANCE RULE OVER AN INTERSECTION SOURCE MUST SPELL OUT EACH DECLARATION'S OPTIONALITY, OR IT IS UNSOUND — AND THE FAILURE SURFACES TWO STEPS LATER AS A `never` (2026-08-27, (CHK.61)(1)).** The merge rule is "the intersected member is a subtype of EVERY declaration, so ANY relating declaration suffices"; with an optional member modelled as plain `T` ((CHK.61)(b)) it picked `name?: Identifier` for `X & { name: undefined }` where the real member is `undefined`, the next NEGATIVE type-predicate narrow then subtracted that constituent, and the row appeared as `Property 'p' does not exist on type 'never'` in an unrelated file. **CENSUS WHAT A NEW RELATION RULE *ACCEPTS* BEFORE BLAMING ANYTHING DOWNSTREAM** — one debug diagnostic at the acceptance point named all four newly-accepted pairs on a whole profile in one run, and they were one shape.
- **A MATRIX CELL THAT IS SILENT FOR A *TRIVIAL* REASON IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ONE SILENT FOR THE REASON UNDER TEST, AND IT WILL NAME THE WRONG AXIS (2026-08-27).** `let p: T | undefined = mkNonNullish(); p ??= this.m()` is silent because the DECLARATION already narrowed `p`, not because the `??=` right-hand side is fine — read as evidence it made the declaration's initializer look like the axis when the receiver was. Vary ONE ingredient per row and check every silent cell has no second explanation.
- **A DASHBOARD-PROFILE ZERO BOUNDS A HAZARD'S FREQUENCY, NEVER ITS EXISTENCE — THE 13k-BASELINE CORPUS IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT SEES A SHAPE THE PROFILES DO NOT CONTAIN (round 792).** A whole-function pre-gate on `checkMemberAccessMissing` ("the property already resolves on the receiver, so nothing here can be true") measured **0 emitting calls in a 22,187-call skip set** on the compiler profile and still killed **7 corpus baselines**, because *resolves* and *legal* come apart in two places tsc's own sources never exercise: a later-lib member RESOLVES and is still an error (TS2550 says "not at this target" — excluded by `LIB_MIN_TARGET_PROPS`), and a CLASS receiver's two sides are not separated in our member tables, so an instance type resolves a STATIC member (TS2576 is that diagnostic — excluded when the raw or apparent type's symbol is a Class). **Any new suppression keyed on "the property resolves" inherits both.**

- **A TS2322/TS2345 message names the DECLARED type, not the flow type, and its elaboration can name a constituent the relation never rejected — so reducing from the message is how a real defect gets three wrong diagnoses in a row (round 744).** `declarations.ts:846` read "the truthiness narrow does not strip `undefined` from `T | undefined`"; the narrow was correct and the failing constituent (`NoSubstitutionTemplateLiteral`) appears nowhere in the message. **Probe the actual flow type with a deliberate mis-assignment** (`const z: number = x` at each flow point prints it) before believing any relation message. Reduce with the 1.3-second scratch-project CLI loop — a throwaway tsconfig dir plus `java -cp <build/classes:build/bench/cp.txt> MainKt --noEmit --listAll <dir>` — not the suite; and build the model from the REAL declarations in `build/bench/tsc-project-*/src/compiler/`, deleting ingredients one at a time, because a hand-written approximation of a tsc shape passes vacuously (round 726's flat `interface Box<T>`, round 743's missing generic overload).
- **AN INTERSECTION IS STORED UN-DISTRIBUTED WHILE tsc DISTRIBUTES AT CONSTRUCTION, SO EVERY CONSUMER NEEDING A MEMBER LIST MUST BUILD ITS OWN VIEW (round 777).** tsc's `getIntersectionType` rewrites `X & (A | B)` into `X & A | X & B` ("union types are always at the top level in type representations") and `getReducedType` drops the discriminant-disjoint combinations — so in tsc `HasModifiers & HasDecorators` IS a 7-member union everywhere. Ours is a `Type.Intersection`: the relation copes via `intersectionSourceDistributes`, flow narrowing via `distributedNarrowingType` (an on-demand view adopted ONLY where it subtracts, so identity/display are untouched otherwise). A NEW consumer that iterates union members will silently do nothing for such a subject; do NOT "fix it properly" in `getIntersectionType` — that changes every intersection's identity, display and relation behaviour at once.
- **To find out what a pass is really doing, ABLATE it with the PassLab instead of deleting it: write `build/pass-lab.txt` with `disable <passName>` and re-run — ZERO recompile.** Whole-suite (`./gradlew jvmTest --rerun`; the lab file is NOT a task input) gives the failing set; per-walker attribution is far cheaper through the scratch-project CLI, since the lab loads from the CWD for any process started at the repo root. **Remove the file afterwards — it silently applies to every later run from that directory.** Round 744 used this to falsify a queue item's "100% artifacts, deletable" list in one suite run plus six 2-second CLI runs.
- **THERE IS NO FRESH-LITERAL EXPRESSION TYPE: `getTypeOfExpression` answers the BASE PRIMITIVE for a literal NODE (`StringLiteralNode` → `stringType`, `NumericLiteralNode` → `numberType`, …), and a `Type.StringLiteral` is minted ONLY by the AST-based `literalTypeOfExpression` (round 781).** So a literal is already widened before any widener sees it — which is why gating `widenType`/`getVarDeclType`/`inferTypeFromInitializer` on const-ness measured BYTE-INERT, and why the const rule ((WIDEN.1), tsc's declaration-flag `getWidenedLiteralTypeForInitializer`) had to be applied where `currentLocalTypes` is RECORDED (`checkVarDeclAssignabilityCore`'s un-annotated branch, plus `spineArithRecordVarDecl`, which runs later and re-widens). Any rule that needs a literal type must read the AST, never `getTypeOfExpression`; and a name's type at every read site comes from `currentLocalTypes`, so that map — not a widener — is where a per-declaration typing rule belongs.
- **`getTypeOfExpression` NEVER flow-narrows — flow narrowing is OPT-IN PER EMISSION SITE via `getNarrowedTypeForReference`, and `getTypeOfIdentifier` answers only from `currentLocalTypes` + the declaration tables (round 743).** Consequence: an `asserts` narrow is NOT visible to a `getTypeOfExpression` consumer (it exists only in the flow graph), whereas an `if (isFoo(x))` type-guard call IS since round 785 — but only inside the THEN branch, and only for a bare-Identifier subject that is already a tracked local. So a new rule that reads an argument/operand type and is meant to respect narrowing must call `getNarrowedTypeForReference` itself; prefer a SECOND CHANCE on the rejecting path (it can only turn a rejection into an acceptance, so nothing that already passed can start failing) over swapping the primary type.
- **`Signature.parameters` IS NOT THE DECLARATION'S PARAMETER LIST — `getParameterSymbols` DROPS every BINDING-PATTERN parameter (unless `forSignatureDisplay`) and the builder then zips the declaration's type annotations onto the SURVIVORS **POSITIONALLY**, so for `f({a}: O, b: string)` the single surviving symbol is named `b` and typed `O` (round 921).** Anything that renders, counts or aligns parameters from `sig.parameters` is therefore one short AND shifted for such a signature, with no error and no diff to find it by; `minArgumentCount` counts the pattern parameter and so can legitimately EXCEED `parameters.size`. Read `sig.declaration`'s own list whenever it is longer (`typeCaptureSignatureParameters` is the reference), never "fix" the zip.
- **A REAL PROJECT BUILD uses the REAL libs; the CORPUS uses the embedded one — the split is `projectDefaults()` (round 730), NOT the `CompilerOptions.useRealLibs` constructor default.** Flipping that constructor default puts the whole ~13k-baseline corpus on real libs; flipping `projectDefaults()` moves every project build and every dashboard profile. When probing a real-lib behavior, note that name resolution proves NOTHING (an unknown name degrades to `any`, which is silent) — the decisive control is a MEMBER or REQUIREDNESS probe.
- **A NARROWING PROBE IN CALL-ARGUMENT POSITION READS THE DECLARED TYPE, NOT THE FLOW TYPE — use an ASSIGNMENT (round 777, five pins measured inert).** Flow narrowing is opt-in per emission site and the argument gate performs the read only on its special arms: a `never` parameter (rounds 441/462/766) and a bare-enum argument (round 764). So `declare function take(s: string); take(x)` after a guard chain reports the DECLARED type on a WORKING build — five pins written that way produced byte-identical output on a fixed and an ablated binary. `const probe: string = x` (TS2322) reads the real flow type; `assertNever(x)` works precisely because `never` is one of those arms.
- **…BUT A PRIMITIVE TARGET IS INERT AT THE RETURN PATH, WHICH GATES ITS WHOLE NARROWING BLOCK TO AN OBJECT-ISH/UNION TARGET (round 784).** `checkReturnAssignabilityCore` only attempts flow narrowing when `targetType` is `Type.Interface`/`Reference`/`Object`/`Union`/`Intersection`, so `function f(): string { … return x.p }` reports the UN-narrowed type on a fully working build — the probe reads as "narrowing is broken" when nothing is. Use a UNION target (`string | boolean`) there. General rule: **a probe shape validated at one site is not portable to another site's gate — re-validate it against a control whose answer you already know.**
- **A TS2339 FIXTURE IS VACUOUS UNLESS ITS RECEIVER IS A UNION AND THE MEMBER IS ON *SOME* CONSTITUENT — `c.nope` (ON NEITHER) IS SILENT FOR A BLOCK-SCOPED LOCAL WHATEVER THE COMPILER DOES, AND THREE OF (CHK.44)'s TWENTY PINS WERE GREEN AGAINST THEIR OWN ABLATION BECAUSE OF IT (2026-08-26, (CHK.44), superseding (CHK.41)'s entry).** A member on SOME constituent is decided by `cmamCheckUnionReceiverNarrowing`; a member on NONE falls to the general receiver path, which for a block-scoped local still bails. Two more in the same family: `A | undefined` + an EXISTING member reports **TS18048**, not TS2339, so a nullish pin written that way is green either way; and a "shadowing a global" fixture does not fire `applyNestedGlobalShadow` unless the name really is in the embedded lib's globals (`const isNaN` does not) — the discriminating suppression is `applyAmbiguousBlockScopedLocals`, which writes `currentLocalTypes` and NOT `currentShadowedNames`. **Run a control probe per NEGATIVE shape (the same fixture with the suppression removed must REPORT) before recording any refusal as pinned.**
- **…AND THE AXIS IS *DECLARED IN A BLOCK*, NEVER local-vs-parameter: A FILE-LEVEL `const`/`let` IS CHECKED AND ALWAYS WAS (2026-08-26, (CHK.44); (CHK.41)'s "3 of 4 shapes, only a parameter" is RETRACTED).** B83.5 is the cause — nothing binds a declaration nested in a block, so `getTypeOfIdentifier` answers `anyType`; `cmamBlockScopedReceiverType` now reads it from the INV.2(c) lexical tables for a UNION annotation only. **The probe that said otherwise named its receiver `top`, which collides with the DOM global `top: Window`** — a resolution experiment must not name its subject after a lib global, and three follow-up probes inherited that one mistake.
- **…AND THAT COLLISION IS NOT ONLY A PROBE-NAMING CAVEAT, IT IS A LIVE DEFECT: A MODULE-LOCAL `interface` WHOSE NAME MATCHES A LIB GLOBAL IS **MERGED INTO** THE LIB SYMBOL, BOTH WAYS (2026-08-26, (CHK.49), four lines reproduce it).** `interface Text { zzzUnique: number }` in a file with an `export` rejects `{ zzzUnique: 1 }` (TS2345, tsgo silent) AND answers the DOM `Text`'s members, so `t.wholeText` is silent where tsgo says TS2339 — the second direction is the dangerous one. `interface`-specific (a `type` alias is correct), so the suspect is the merge and not `getTypeFromTypeReference`'s lookup order; `Event` fails identically. **Known colliders to keep out of a fixture: `top`, `name`, `files`, `Text`, `Event`, `Body` (2026-08-27, a `class Body` — `const b: Body = new Body()` is TS2740 naming `bodyUsed`/`arrayBuffer`/`blob`).**
- **AND THE RECEIVER TYPE FOR THE PROPERTY-EXISTENCE CHECK MUST BE READ AT THAT CALL, NOT WRITTEN INTO `currentLocalTypes` — the map is read by EVERY consumer of the cpa pass, so an extra entry reaches the TS18048 emitters and B136's chaining arm and costs corpus baselines (2026-08-26, (CHK.44), reverted: `discriminateWithOptionalProperty4` + `narrowingPastLastAssignment`).** Both new rows were the same missing mechanism — narrowing of a block-scoped REFERENCE — reached through consumers the change was not fixing. Reading it at the one call also removes the anchoring problem for free: a nested arrow's statements are chain-excluded from `cpaSpineLeave`, so the write route needed a second call site in the legacy walk and the read route needs none.
- **AND `narrowByAssignmentRhs` REDUCES THE *DECLARED* TYPE, NEVER THE ANTECEDENT (round 416's rule; the identifier/property/literal arms still predate it).** In the then-branch of a guard the antecedent IS the constituent the assignment replaces, so filtering it answers `never` or itself and the branch join re-mints the declared union — i.e. a new arm written against `antecedent` reads as "no narrowing at all" rather than as a wrong type.
- **A "DOES THE RECEIVER HAVE THIS MEMBER" PIN IS VACUOUS FOR EVERY RECEIVER WITH AN `extends`, AND THE AXIS IS **HERITAGE** RATHER THAN "LIB" — `Date`/`Map`/`Promise`/`RegExp`/`Error`/`Symbol`/`EventTarget` AND EVERY PRIMITIVE REPORT, `Text`/`Node`/`Element`/`HTMLElement` AND A HAND-WRITTEN `interface D extends B` DID NOT (2026-08-26, (CHK.51); the lib half is now CLOSED, the rest is (CHK.52)).** Still silent after (CHK.51), so still vacuous: a PROGRAM interface with heritage, a MIXED closure (`interface Mine extends HTMLElement`), a CLASS instance with a base, an ARRAY or any numeric-index receiver (`number[]`, `Array<T>`, `Uint8Array`), and a bare function type — four different mechanisms, none of them the heritage firewall alone. **Census the shape ONE CASE PER FILE before writing such a pin**; a multi-shape fixture hides which mechanism answered.
- **AND THE FIREWALL BEHIND IT IS LOAD-BEARING FOR A REASON THAT IS NOT ITS OWN: `cmamCheckResolvedObjectType`'s "skip if class/interface has base types" IS STANDING IN FOR FLOW NARROWING — DELETING IT MEASURES **89 DIAGNOSTICS ON THE COMPILER PROFILE AGAINST 46**, AND ALL 43 ARE NARROWING GAPS (2026-08-26).** The dominant one is the INTERSECTION narrow tsc performs when a type predicate names a SIBLING of the receiver's declared type (`canHaveSymbol(node: Node): node is Declaration` applied to an `e: Expression`); a predicate naming a SUBTYPE narrows correctly here. Any relaxation of that line needs POSITIVE evidence that the member table is complete ((CHK.45)'s rule) — `cmamLibHeritageMembersComplete` is the shipped one, and its evidence is "every type in the transitive base closure is a lib type".
- **…SO A FALSE-POSITIVE PIN ABOUT A TYPE-GUARD NARROW MUST NAME A **SIBLING** PREDICATE TYPE, NOT A SUBTYPE — THE SUBTYPE FORM IS GREEN ON THE ABLATED BINARY TOO AND READS AS COVERAGE (2026-08-26, (CHK.51): the arm read `0 RED` with the compiler profile at 89).** Round 902's law on a fixture rather than on a guard, and the second time this session a pin's SHAPE rather than its assertion was the blind part.
- **A `declare global { interface X { … } }` DOES NOT REACH `globals` ((CHK.50), OPEN), SO A LIB SYMBOL'S DECLARATION LIST STILL READS "ALL LIB" AFTER SUCH AN AUGMENTATION — ANY RULE GATED ON "every declaration is a lib declaration" MUST ALSO CONSULT `globalAugmentedInterfaceNames` OR IT CONVERTS THAT SILENT FALSE NEGATIVE INTO A FALSE POSITIVE (2026-08-26).** A GLOBAL-SCRIPT augmentation (`interface HTMLElement { … }` in a non-module file) needs nothing: it merges, so the symbol carries a non-lib declaration.
- **A NULLISH UNION SOURCE IS REFUSED AGAINST A *PRIMITIVE* TARGET BY `canUseTypeEngine`, SO `T | undefined` IS SILENTLY ASSIGNABLE TO `T` AT A DECLARATION, AN ASSIGNMENT AND A RETURN — AND ANY CHANGE THAT *ADDS* `| undefined` TO A TYPE THEREFORE **DELETES** DIAGNOSTICS RATHER THAN ADDING THEM (2026-08-27, (CHK.61)(b)/(CHK.63)).** ARGUMENT position and a UNION target are the two that still fire, which is why a probe written either way reads as working. Opening the gate is 11 ours-only rows on the 8 profiles, all flow-narrowing gaps in the ASSIGNMENT/RETURN readers ((CHK.64)) — so the gate and the narrowing must move together, in that order.
- **AND A NARROWING GAP THAT LOOKS OPTIONALITY-DEPENDENT REPRODUCES WITH AN *EXPLICIT* `| undefined` MEMBER — NO PATCH, NO BUILD (same round).** Declare `p: number | undefined` instead of `p?: number` and the shipped binary shows the same five gaps the queue had recorded as reachable only under a probe patch. The mirror rule: the FLOW WALK handles `&&` correctly and the LEGACY if-arm helper (`extractNullNarrowing`, one `(name, type)` pair) does not, so **what varies across "does `&&` narrow here" fixtures is the READER, not the condition** — check a member access and an argument before concluding narrowing is broken.
- **THE CALL-ARGUMENT READER IS LIVE ONLY FOR A **PARAMETER** SOURCE, SO A NARROWING FIXTURE GRADED THROUGH `take(localVar)` READS ZERO ROWS ON A BROKEN *AND* A FIXED BINARY — AND A NULLISH-UNION ONE IS VACUOUS A SECOND TIME, BECAUSE IT SITS UNDER (CHK.63)'s OWN `canUseTypeEngine` GATE (2026-08-28).** `const r: string | number = mk(); take(r)` is silent here and TS2345 in tsc; only `function f(r: string | number) { take(r) }` reports. The instrument that always works is a deliberate MIS-ASSIGNMENT (`const p: boolean = r`), which PRINTS the flow type in its message; grade a loop/flow change on that, over `string | number` rather than `T | undefined`.
- **`getUnionType` PERFORMS NO SUBTYPE REDUCTION (INV.5(a) interns by member-id list), SO ANY FLOW JOIN THAT MIXES A NARROWED SUBTYPE WITH THE DECLARATION'S OWN CONSTITUENTS PRODUCES `A | B` WHERE tsc PRODUCES `B` — AND A LATER DISCRIMINANT TEST CAN NO LONGER FILTER IT (2026-08-28, (CHK.66)).** tsc's `getTypeAtFlowLoopLabel` unions with `UnionReduction.Subtype`; ours cannot, which is the whole residual price of the loop join (`ConditionalTypeNode | Node | undefined` for `Node | undefined`). No policy AT the label fixes it — three were measured and only the `never` refusal does anything.
- **A SUPPRESSION-ONLY SUBSTITUTION MUST REFUSE A `never` ANSWER, BECAUSE `never` RELATES TO EVERYTHING AND AN UNREACHABLE NODE'S FLOW TYPE *IS* `never` — SO ADOPTING IT DELETES THE DIAGNOSTIC (2026-08-28, (CHK.63); (CHK.69) hit the same thing at the loop label).** Every reader that narrows a source "only when it makes the assignment relate" inherits it: with the (CHK.63) gate open, `function f(): string { return ''; return undefined; }` suppressed its own row, and the ONLY instrument that saw it was the corpus baseline `functionReturn.ts` — the 8-profile grid was byte-identical.
- **A NARROWING probe must target a PRIMITIVE, never an object/branded type (round 762; round 760 lost an arc to the same class of error).** Every enum-flavored type is a member-LESS `Type.Object`, so `K`, `K.A` and a washed `never` are ALL silently assignable to any object-shaped probe target — silence conflates "narrowed", "not narrowed" and "collapsed". `declare function probe(x: string)` (or `number`) fires for all of them and NAMES the narrowed type in the message. Corollary: `const y: K.A|K.B = x` cannot detect a widened `x: K` either, because enum → MEMBER relates vacuously — that is (REL.2)'s leniency, so such a pin can never show the positive.
- **A per-file checker PASS loop iterates `checkedResults` (the INV.6 partition view); a COLLECTOR loop (populates cross-file maps/sets, no `diagnostics.add`) MUST iterate `binderResults`** — a gated collector starves partition workers of program-wide suppression context (round 609: 1,174 TS2339 partition FPs). The `--partitionCheck 2` harness on the 8 profiles is the gate for any new pass loop's choice; the sequential path is identical either way.
- **A `--partitionCheck`/`--workers` divergence is NOT automatically the round-609 collector class — it can be a resolution-ORDER accident in which the SEQUENTIAL run is the one that is right for the wrong reason (round 754).** Diagnose by printing the source type, the target type AND the relation verdict in both views before theorising: if the two views resolve the same types and disagree only on the verdict, no collector is starved and there is no cross-file context to restore. Confirm with a two-file scratch project — the round-754 divergence reproduced with no partitioning at all.
- **Symbol/Type id sequences are THREAD-LOCAL (INV.6(6c0), round 607) and `runWithDeepStack` (JVM) hands them off: capture caller's counters → seed the compile thread → WRITE THE ADVANCED VALUES BACK on join.** Removing the write-back (or spawning a compile on a fresh thread without seeding) restarts ids at 1 and COLLIDES with the singleton intrinsics' class-load-thread ids — 51 corpus failures whose listAll output stays IDENTICAL (id-keyed cache confusion, mostly silent). A parallel worker instead calls `Symbol.rebaseThreadIds`/`Type.rebaseThreadId` with a high base at thread start; ids must never cross a worker boundary. **Round 827: the NATIVE actual now spawns a thread too and carries the same seed/write-back plus `forceIntrinsicTypeInit()` — under Kotlin/Native's new memory model those cells are SHARED so the handoff is a no-op, and it is written that way precisely so the actual does not depend on which regime is in force.** `DeepStackHandoffTest` (commonTest) observes the counters directly, because this failure mode leaves every byte of output identical; measured native: `before=191 insideStart=191 insideEnd=196 after=196`, `anyType/stringType/neverType = 1/4/10` with identity preserved across the boundary.

- **Any NEW full-tree expression walker MUST handle `BinaryExpression` iteratively (worklist/right-spine), not recursively** — `binderBinaryExpressionStress` is a deeply-nested `a+b+c+…` chain that StackOverflows a naive `walk(left); walk(right)`, and a crash mid-check ABORTS JS emit, so the regression shows up as a JS-EMIT output diff (NOT in the `find_candidates` EXTRA bucket — only the failing-set diff catches it). B98b's `walkThisTypeExpr` learned this the hard way. Reference iterative patterns: B98b's worklist (`ArrayDeque` frontier) and `getTypeOfBinaryExpression`'s left-spine fold; the old right-spine reference `checkArgCountInExprCore` is deleted (round 535 — its depth-cap geometry lives on in `spineArgDepth`/`spineArgEdge`).
- **Do NOT add ANY `try/catch(StackOverflowError)` in the checker — fix the recursion instead.** The checker's overflows are eliminated (0/suite-run): cycles are guarded at the resolvers by in-progress sentinels (`symbolTypeResolutionInProgress`/`nodeTypeResolutionInProgress`/`memberResolutionInProgress`/`relationComparisonStack`/`functionElaborationStack`) plus `collectInheritedPropertyNames`' `visited` set, and the full-tree binary walkers iterate. A new overflow means a missing guard or a recursive walk that should be iterative — add the guard / iterate, don't swallow. All ~213 inline catches were REMOVED (2026-07-02); **the ONLY remaining catch is the `init` boundary guard (`reportCheckerStackOverflow` → TS2589), and it is LOAD-BEARING — do NOT remove it** (a "zero catches" attempt let a raw SOE escape to the test harness, and the guard had been silently absorbing a left-spine recursion whose TS2589 was invisible in a js-only subtest). A guardless full run doubles as the corpus overflow census (1 crash = 1 overflow site); ~218 `is BinaryExpression ->` sites remain unaudited. On Kotlin/Native the guard is inert (a native stack overflow is a hard crash — see StackOverflowError.kt). **The whole pipeline additionally runs on a 256 MB deep-stack thread** (`runWithDeepStack`, DeepStack.kt, wrapping `compileParsed`; virtual-reserved and lazily committed on BOTH backends since round 827, so it costs nothing on a zero-swap box — measured max RSS 107-111 MB) — but that is a SAFETY MARGIN, not a licence: new walkers still iterate.
- **Defensive `try/catch` in the checker/parser/Vfs uses `catch (_: Exception)`, NEVER `catch (_: Throwable)`** (the whole codebase was swept to Exception on 2026-07-04 — 135 sites). `Throwable` swallows `Error` subtypes too, which defeats the SOE doctrine above: a `StackOverflowError` caught by a `catch (Throwable) { null }` returns a silently-wrong default INSTEAD of reaching the `init` boundary guard (→ TS2589), and an `OutOfMemoryError`/`AssertionError` gets masked into wrong output rather than surfacing. `catch (Exception)` still catches the genuine recoverable cases these guards exist for (NPE / ClassCast / IllegalState are `RuntimeException ⊆ Exception`), so narrowing is behavior-preserving on the corpus (byte-identical) EXCEPT that `Error`s now propagate — which is the point. The narrowing is validated by the full suite (exercises all catch paths) + a byte-identical self-compile; `DeepExpressionChainTest` pins the invariant that a deep chain surfaces TS2589 through the boundary guard rather than being swallowed. Removing the defensive `catch (Exception)` ENTIRELY (to surface NPEs from incomplete modeling as crashes) is a separate, per-site root-cause effort — do not do it blind.
- **The whole checker runs inside `Checker`'s `init` block, so a `private val` PROPERTY declared BELOW that block is still null while any pass executes (round 726).** A debug helper gated on such a property therefore prints NOTHING and reads as "wrong code path" when the path is right — put instrumentation state in a top-level `private val`, or hook `Diagnostic`'s constructor (a filtered `init` + `Throwable().stackTraceToString()`), which names the true emission site in one run.
- **A NEW `Checker` FIELD THAT ANY PASS CONSULTS MUST BE DECLARED BEFORE THE `init` BLOCK** — Kotlin initializes properties in DECLARATION order, so a `private val m = HashMap<…>()` written next to its own helper further down the ~110k-line file is still **null** throughout every init-dispatch pass, and the tell is an impossible-looking NPE on a non-nullable `val` (`because "this.m" is null`). Declare checker state beside the existing cache fields (`canonicalEnumSymCache` & co, ~:5582), never beside its consumer.
- **THE TWO CONSTRUCTS THAT BLOCK A MECHANICAL METHOD SPLIT ARE SCOPING, NOT CONTROL FLOW (round 818, `transformClassBody`): a LOCAL `data class` constructed by a region you want to move (its type is un-nameable from a member function — LIFT it to a private nested class; behaviour-free while it captures nothing and never escapes), and a LOCAL `fun` CALLED FROM BOTH SIDES of the boundary (it can neither move nor be duplicated — pass it as a FUNCTION-TYPED parameter, `::localFun`, which leaves the moved call site textually untouched).** A local `fun` whose call sites are ALL inside one region just moves with it, so the test is where the call sites are, not what the construct is. **The order that makes the function-typed parameter sound — the stage that decides the `var`s it closes over must run first — is enforced by NOTHING in the types; ablate it.**
- **A `Checker.kt` stack-trace line number WRAPS modulo 65536** (the JVM LineNumberTable is u2; the file exceeds 90k lines): `Checker.kt:26287` is really line 91823 — add 65536 before trusting a frame that seems to point at unrelated code. **This bites EVERY reader of a `LineNumberTable`, not only a stack trace** — `javap -l`, and so `scripts/method_bytes_by_line.py`, report a method past line 65,536 at its WRAPPED line, and that range is usually a plausible-looking part of the file (round 820 read `tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs` as 49967–51030, i.e. inside the `companion object`). Add 65,536 back and ASSERT the corrected span contains the function, as `scripts/tisp_split_analyze.py --bytes` does — and note the third possibility, **+131,072**, plus INLINED stdlib appearing at SYNTHETIC lines PAST EOF, which makes a per-line JFR/`javap` attribution three guesses of which one is unfalsifiable (round 868): use line data to SHAPE a hypothesis, never to report a number.
- **`lexLevelHasName`'s TWO RULES — the UNTRUSTED Module/Enum skip and `scope.symbols`-only — ARE PROPERTIES OF *THAT* ASCENT, NOT OF THE LEXICAL TABLES, AND NEITHER SURVIVES A TRANSPLANT ONTO `spineScopeLookup`'s CHAIN (round 918, both measured by ablation).** The untrusted skip is sound there because the unresolved-names walk keeps a SECOND, export-filtered threaded population underneath it; `spineScopeLookup` has none, so skipping those levels answers NOTHING inside a namespace body. Round 748's `symbols`-only rule is about a RESOLVER whose soundness is that `symbols` excludes every main-bound name — an ENUMERATOR reading `symbols` only offers no file-level declaration and no import at all. Any new consumer of the INV.2(c) tables must ask which of the two chains it is on before inheriting either rule.
- **A PER-NODE HOOK ON THE SPINE SEES *NONE* OF THE CHECKING AMBIENT — THE ANCHORS INSTALL-AND-RESTORE IT PER DISPATCH, SO AT AN ARBITRARY NODE `currentLocalTypes` IS THE **FILE-LEVEL** MAP AND A BODY LOCAL ANSWERS WITH A SAME-NAMED GLOBAL'S TYPE (round 911, measured).** A function body is checked in one nested call from its declaration's anchor, so anything asking "what is the type HERE" from a spine hook must first reproduce `ctaM3StmtAnchorCore`'s prologue (`classForThis / inFn / inAsync / inGen / fnTpDecls / fnTpScope / currentFlowGraph / currentCheckFileName` + the namespace-chain push) and then `withCtaFrameLocals(ctaFrames.last())` — **the position's SCOPE is the cta frame; the ambient FIELDS are not.** Without it a body local read `string` (the global), and a parameter and a narrowed reference both read **`any`** — silently, since `any` is legal everywhere. Corollary measured the same round: **`checkTypeAssignabilityInStatements` is NOT on the spine path for ordinary function bodies** (a hook in its statement loop fired ZERO times over declaration/arrow/method bodies), so the natural guess that the legacy assignability walk owns body-scoped ambient is false.
- **…AND THE ABOVE DOES *NOT* GENERALISE TO NAME RESOLUTION — `currentLocalTypes` IS `String -> Type` AND HOLDS NO SYMBOL AND NO DECLARATION, so reinstalling the cta ambient cannot answer "what does this name refer to HERE" in either direction (round 913, the (API.3b) queue entry predicted the opposite and was wrong).** The walk-scoped input for THAT question is `spineCurrentScope` (the INV.2(c) chain, via `spineScopeLookup`), and `spineScopeEnterIfOwner` runs BEFORE `spineEnterNode`, so it is already correct at an arbitrary node and needs no reconstruction. **What the two share is the only thing that forces capture: `spineScopeClear` nulls the chain per file, so post-hoc a body local resolves to a same-named FILE-LEVEL binding and a parameter to nothing at all.** Corollary for any new name-position consumer: refuse a MEMBER name (`o.p`, a property signature's name) rather than scope-look it up — the lookup finds whatever unrelated binding shares the spelling, and the pin that proves the refusal is a refusal and not a miss is that the TYPE at the same span still captures.

- **A CONTEXT-FREE `Scanner.scan()` LOOP IS A SAFE APPROXIMATION OF THE PARSER'S LEXER ONLY WHERE IT *SPLITS*; WHERE IT *MERGES* IT DE-SYNCHRONISES THE STREAM FOR THE WHOLE REST OF THE FILE, AND THE ONLY MERGING CASE IS THE TEMPLATE SUBSTITUTION (round 919, `SourceIndex.scanTokens`).** `reScanSlashToken` / `reScanGreaterToken` only ever split one token into several, which adds token ends and leaves every real boundary present; skipping `reScanTemplateToken` makes the `}` of `${…}` a CloseBrace and the CLOSING BACKTICK open a literal running to the next backtick anywhere in the file — measured on tsc's `checker.ts` as **50,684 tokens for 3,151,772 characters, longest 62,089**, which made every `-project` position query answer about a huge enclosing node. **A merge is not a coarser answer, it is a wrong one, and no fixture catches it** unless it carries a substituting template AND asserts something AFTER it.
- **A `CtaFrame` DOES NOT THREAD `this` — `CtaFrame.classForThis` IS NULL INSIDE AN ARROW BODY, AND THE CHECKER IS RIGHT ANYWAY, BECAUSE `this` REACHES THE DIAGNOSTICS THROUGH B101's WALK-SCOPED INSTALLS, WHICH AN ARROW DELIBERATELY PRESERVES (round 923, measured byte-identical to tsc 7.0.2 on 17 `this` positions).** So a PULL-based reconstruction of `currentClassForThis` must be transparent to `ArrowFunction` and opaque to every other `this`-binder — and it may NOT be `spineCaClassCtx`, whose KDoc records that a nested `FunctionDeclaration` is BUG-COMPATIBLY transparent to it, nor `typeCaptureEnclosingClass`, which answers the lexical (accessibility) question and would offer a class's members inside a `function`. `typeCaptureThisClass` is the `this`-meaning one.
- **AND A CTA FRAME IS PUSHED AT A `Block` ENTER, SO AN EXPRESSION-BODIED ARROW PUSHES NONE — any defect attributed to "arrows" must be re-checked WITH BRACES, or the repro measures the enclosing method's frame and reads as working (round 923: go-to-definition on `this.p` was green in `() => this.p` and broken in `() => { return this.p }`).**
- **A CAPTURE THAT TYPES THE CARET'S *NARROWEST* NODE ASKS THE WRONG QUESTION AT ANY NODE WHOSE MEANING COMES FROM ITS PARENT, AND THE MEMBER CASE FAILS *SILENTLY AND PLAUSIBLY*: a member name is bound by NO SCOPE, so `getTypeOfExpression` of the `p` in `o.p` resolves the SPELLING — answering the type of whatever unrelated binding shares it, and `any` only where nothing does (round 924: 16 of 23 member positions read a collider).** The rule is tsc's `getTypeOfSymbolAtLocation` — move onto the ACCESS and type that — which also buys narrowing and every instantiation rule for free; the only receivers needing (API.3d)'s carrier are `this`/`super`, plain identifiers in this parser. **No gate here can see it**: the capture is off in production, so the corpus, `cost_gate.py` and the 8-profile grid are all green either way.
- **GROUND TRUTH FOR A LANGUAGE-SERVICE ANSWER IS OBTAINABLE DIRECTLY — `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc --lsp -stdio` IS AN LSP SERVER**, so a hover/definition/completion expectation can be READ OUT OF tsc 7.0.2 over the same fixture instead of reasoned from `typescript-go-repo/internal/ls/*.go` (round 924; the client is `scripts/lsp_hover.py`). Two predictions were wrong in the round that used it and were caught by it, so **do not hand-write expected values for an (API.\*) pin while this exists**.
- **AN EDIT-VARIANT FIXTURE BUILT FROM A CRLF SOURCE BY ANY ORDINARY TOOL BECOMES AN LF FILE, SO A "ONE-LINE EDIT" IS SILENTLY A WHOLE-FILE REWRITE — THE tsgo INCREMENTAL HARNESS SHIPPED THAT WAY FOR THREE ROUNDS (2026-09-01, (INC.90)).** `binder.orig.ts` carried 3,916 CRs and both edit variants carried none, so each cell the write-up described as "a local `const` inside an exported function" was that line PLUS a 3,916-line newline normalisation. It fails in the REASSURING direction — both compilers still classify it as body-only, because neither line endings nor whitespace survive into a declaration-emit hash or an exported-signature fingerprint — which is exactly why it survived: the numbers looked right. **Regenerate variants with the source's OWN line ending and assert the diff is the intended lines and nothing else**; `diff` reporting `1,Nc1,N` for a one-line change is the tell.
- **A REAL PROJECT'S SOURCES MAY BE CRLF — THE BENCH PROFILE'S tsc SOURCES ARE — AND PYTHON'S DEFAULT UNIVERSAL-NEWLINE TRANSLATION COLLAPSES `\r\n`, SHIFTING EVERY OFFSET, SO A CARET BUILT FROM THE COMPILER'S OWN `(start, end)` LANDS ON A *PLAUSIBLE WRONG IDENTIFIER* (2026-08-24, (INC.41)).** It never errors and never looks empty — the LSP answers a real type for the wrong name — so an oracle harness reads as working while every verdict it produces is about a different node. Open with `newline=""` in any script that maps our offsets onto a file (`scripts/lsp_hover_project.py`, `scripts/inc41_classify.py`), and prefer a project the compiler already parsed over a materialised fixture, which hides the whole class by being LF.
- **A DISPLAY DEFECT KEYED *FIRST-WINS* GETS WORSE THE LONGER A CHECKER LIVES, SO A DIFFERENTIAL TAKEN AFTER *ONE* QUERY UNDERSTATES IT — the re-entrant replay's captured types diverge in 43 of 75 files not from a defect of its own but because it carries every earlier query's `aliasDisplayMap` registrations (2026-08-24, (INC.41): 393 of 413 worse rows are that, against 20 real ones).** Any arm that RETAINS state — a replay, a reused `Checker`, a `prepare`d working set — inherits the accumulated first-wins answers of everything it has already resolved, so its divergence is a function of SESSION LENGTH and not of the mechanism. Before attributing such a gap to the new mechanism, ask what each arm had already resolved; and never grade a retaining arm on a two-query sweep.
- **AN EDIT SPAN AND AN IDENTITY KEY ARE DIFFERENT QUANTITIES THE MOMENT A NON-IDENTIFIER JOINS THE OCCURRENCE POPULATION, AND EVERY MAP KEYED BY ONE AND READ BY THE OTHER THEN FAILS SILENTLY IN THE CONSERVATIVE DIRECTION (round 926).** A capture answer is filed under the NODE's raw `pos`, while `o["p"]`'s rename edit begins one character later (the text between the quotes — tsc edits `[77,78)` for a literal at `[76,79)`); looking the occurrence's prior resolution up by the EDIT's start missed every time, read as an empty expectation, and refused every element-access rename as `WOULD_CHANGE_MEANING`. Carry the key explicitly (`PlannedEdit.nodePos`) rather than re-deriving it from the span.
- **`SourceIndex.identifiers()` AND `occurrenceNodes()` COINCIDE FOR EVERY FILE WITH NO MEMBER-NAME LITERAL, SO A PIN THAT TWO CONSUMERS ASK THE *SAME* POPULATION IS VACUOUS ON ALMOST EVERY HAND-WRITTEN FIXTURE — (INC.13)'s ablation of exactly that drift read **0 RED** (2026-08-23).** The two sets differ only by `{"p": v}` / `o["p"]` / `` [`p`] `` / a computed member declaration, and nobody writes one of those into a hover fixture; one `const third = o["p"];` is what makes the arm discriminate. Same shape one layer up: `documentHighlightsAt` and `fileSemantics` already shared a memo entry on `binder.ts` BEFORE they were made to, because that file has no such literal either.
- **WIDENING A CAPTURE REQUEST FROM ONE CARET TO A WHOLE FILE IS NEARLY FREE AND DOES NOT MOVE ANY ANSWER — +9 TO +17 ms AT THE MEDIAN FILE OVER TSC'S OWN 78 SOURCES, AND **0 DIVERGENCES IN 1,883 SAMPLED SPANS ACROSS TWO DISJOINT DRAWS** (2026-08-23, (INC.13)).** A narrowed build is mostly FLOOR (~345 ms), so extra spans are cheap beside it; and (INC.10)'s first-touch hazard does NOT fire, because a capture changes WHERE a walk records, not WHEN the compiler resolves a declaration. `scripts/caret-vs-file-capture.sh` is the differential and needs NO baseline (both arms answer the same question) — deliberately separate from `capture-equivalence.sh`, which varies the PARTITION at a fixed request where this varies the REQUEST at a fixed partition.
- **…BUT THAT IS TRUE OF THE *TYPE* CHANNEL ONLY: A REQUEST IS PRICED PER **ANCHOR** WHERE AN EDITOR NEEDS A PRICE PER **ANSWER**, SO WIDENING A FILE-WIDE HOVER TO ALSO SERVE MEMBER/SCOPE/SIGNATURE CARETS COSTS **+286 ms ON `binder.ts` AND +25.1 s ON `checker.ts`** TO SAVE A **204 / 2,078 ms** COMPLETION — break-even 1.40 / 12.1 completions per hover WITH NO EDIT SINCE, and a completion types a `.` first, which is an edit (2026-08-24, (INC.33), REFUSED).** Retention refuses it independently and harder: one widened entry is **48x / 205x** today's hover entry (54.4 M records for `checker.ts`), because `CapturedScope` repeats the lib globals at EVERY anchor — **O(anchors x globals)**. The only shape priced per ANSWER is a re-entrant capture against a retained checker ((INC.17)'s `ProgramRecheck`, blocked by (INC.41)'s 43 `DIVERGE-TYPE` files); re-take the price with `scripts/inc33-widen-cost.sh`.
- **A MEMBER'S RELATION TO THE BASE IT IMPLEMENTS IS A *REFERENCE* FACT AND NOT A *NAVIGATION* ONE — tsc 7.0.2 ANSWERS THE TWO DIFFERENTLY AND SO MUST WE (round 926, measured with `scripts/lsp_member_refs.py`).** `textDocument/definition` on the `p` of `class Impl implements Shape` answers THAT MEMBER; `textDocument/references` on it answers `Shape`'s whole group. Hence `CapturedDefinition.related` beside `locations`. Three further properties of that edge, each measured and each refuting a plausible design: **structural compatibility does not relate** (a same-shaped class with no `implements` is a different symbol), it **is transitive** (an `override` two edges down is in the group), and it **does not chain between siblings** (with `class C implements A, B`, a caret on `A.p` does not reach `b.p`) — so it is matched PER OCCURRENCE as "symbol + the bases that symbol implements, sets intersect", never as a transitive closure over the group, which merges the two interfaces.
- **A MEMBER NAME DERIVED FROM A *TYPE* IS NOT A FUNCTION OF THE PROGRAM HERE, SO tsc's `isTypeUsableAsPropertyName` (late binding) IS NOT PORTABLE AS WRITTEN — a FILE-LEVEL un-annotated `const K = "p"` answers the literal `"p"` in the assignability pass and the widened `string` in the pass behind TS2339, and `const obj = { [K]: 1 }; obj.p` then emits the correct TS2322 *and* `Property 'p' does not exist on type '{}'` in ONE compile (round 935, measured).** Same signature as round 933's two-extraction-sites drift, reached through ambient state (round 911) rather than through a second `when`. Any NAME/KEY a member table is built from must be resolved SYNTACTICALLY (`lateBoundComputedKeyName` — an enum member's value, or an innermost-first walk of the enclosing statement lists; `lookupPerFileForNode` cannot see a body local, B83.5) — and the pin for it asserts the two passes agree, because each pass alone is green.
- **`Node.end` IS NOT THE END OF THE NODE — IT IS THE END OF THE TOKEN *FOLLOWING* IT, FOR EVERY NODE BUT `SourceFile`, SO SIBLING SPANS **OVERLAP** AND `[pos, end)` IS NOT A CONTAINMENT TEST (round 910).** `Parser.getEnd()` is `scanner.getPos()` (`Parser.kt:746`), read after the parser's one-token lookahead: in `const abc = 1;\nlet d = abc;`, `abc` reads `[6,11)` where its text is `[6,9)` (past the ` =`) and statement 1 reads `[0,18)` where its text ends at 14 (past `let`). **So `end - pos` is not a length, a caret on the `=` tests as inside the preceding identifier, and a caret on a statement's first keyword tests as inside the PREVIOUS statement** — any position→node lookup must bound each end by the next sibling's `pos` (or by a `Scanner` re-scan). `SourceFile` is exact only because EOF is zero-width. **And `Node.pos` is the mirror trap in the other direction: it is tsc's `getStart()`, NOT tsc's `pos`** — `Scanner.scan` sets `tokenPos` AFTER `scanLeadingTrivia()` (`Scanner.kt:331-333`), so trivia is already skipped and leading comments hang off `leadingComments` BELOW the node's span; a routine ported from tsc that adds a `getStart()` skip double-skips past the node's own first token. Pinned by `NodeSpanSemanticsTest` in the `-project` module.

- **A HAND-WRITTEN FIXTURE FOR A LEXICAL OR POSITION API DOES NOT CONTAIN WHAT REAL SOURCE CONTAINS, AND NINE GREEN ROUNDS PROVED IT — `(BUG.2)` SHIPPED FROM ROUND 910 TO 919 AND `(GATE.2)` FOUND *FIVE MORE* DEFECTS THE MOMENT REAL TypeScript WAS POINTED AT THE SAME CODE (round 920).** Nobody writes a substituting template, a backtick inside a regex (tsc's own `utilities.ts` has one), or a parenthesis-less arrow parameter into a go-to-definition pin — yet the last of those is **328 sites in tsc's 78 compiler sources**. **So a position/lexical feature is gated by `TokenIndexInvariants` over real source, not by pins**: rules true of any correct implementation (tokens partition the text, gaps hold only trivia, every identifier the PARSER found starts a token of exactly its length, a descent reaches every identifier), with **the parse as the ORACLE** — it is the context-sensitive lexer, so a MERGE is expressible as "an identifier with no token starting at it" and needs no baseline. Hermetic corpus: the adversarial shape set plus `RealLibFiles.files` (2.39 MB of real TypeScript already embedded — no vendored tree, no licensing question). The local one (`build/bench/tsc-project-*`, where every defect actually showed) lives in `scripts/round920-token-gate.sh`, which **REFUSES with exit 2** rather than skipping, because a gate reading a local artifact that passes quietly where it is absent is rounds 853/873 again.
- **THE SAFE APPROXIMATION OF A CONTEXT-SENSITIVE LEXEME IS TO *ASK THE PARSE*, NOT TO GUESS — A `RegularExpressionLiteralNode` AND A `JsxText` EACH CARRY THEIR OWN **RAW** TEXT, SO `pos + text.length` IS EXACT AND HAS NONE OF `Node.end`'s OVERSHOOT (round 920).** Round 919's law (a SPLIT is safe, a MERGE de-synchronises to end of file) says which cases are dangerous; this says what to do about them, and it is strictly better than a heuristic: whatever the parser decided a `/` was, the index reproduces, so the index and the tree **cannot disagree** and the undecidable question is never asked. Two failure directions the law hides: a regex containing a QUOTE merges only to the line break (our `scanStringLiteral` stops there) while one containing a BACKTICK merges to the next backtick anywhere in the file — so the same defect is loud in one costume and near-silent in the other.
- **A NODE BUILT WITH `Parameter`/`VariableDeclaration`'s DEFAULT `pos = 0, end = 0`, OR WITH AN *EXACT* END WHERE EVERY OTHER NODE CARRIES THE FOLLOWING TOKEN'S, IS A NODE NO POSITION LOOKUP CAN EVER ENTER — AND NOTHING IN THE COMPILER NOTICES (round 920, five sites).** `SourceIndex.realEndOf` snaps an end back to the greatest token end strictly below it, so an already-exact end lands on the token BEFORE the node and clamps to `pos`: an empty span. The five were the paren-less arrow parameter, the index-signature parameter, the `catch` variable, `declare global`'s `global`, the JSX tag name and a construct signature's synthetic `new`. **A new synthesized name must either carry the span of the text it names or stay empty-texted** — the corpus cannot see the difference (core pins were unchanged by all eight fixes), only the invariant gate can.
- **TWO SILENT TRAPS FOUND BY THE FIRST PHASE-18 CONSUMERS (2026-09-01, (EXT.1)/(LSP.1)).** A `Parameter` list may contain `isCommentPlaceholder` entries — `f(/* nothing */)` grows a PHANTOM parameter for any consumer that does not filter them. And a FILE-FINAL token is unreachable by every `-project` position lookup when the file lacks a trailing newline (`SourceIndex.realEndOf` snaps STRICTLY below an EXACT end, clamping to an empty span — round 920's mechanism in a sixth costume; queued (API.18), pinned as a recorded edge in `XtscLspServerTest`).

- **A RAW `(pos, end)` PAIR DOES NOT ALWAYS IDENTIFY ONE NODE, AND THE CASE WHERE IT DOES NOT IS EXACTLY THE ONE A COMPLETION IS ASKED IN (round 917).** A dangling `.` makes `Parser.kt`'s `Dot ->` arm synthesize a ZERO-WIDTH `Identifier("")` (TS1003), so when the buffer ends IMMEDIATELY after the dot the `PropertyAccessExpression`'s `end` — read after a lookahead that sees only EOF — equals its RECEIVER's, and `o` and `o.<nothing>` become the same `TypeCaptureSpan`. **Any span-keyed recorder must let a DESCENDANT overwrite its ancestor** (`Checker.typeCaptureMemberNodes`); plain first-wins then answers `any`, and `holder. ` / `holder.;` / `holder.\n` all hide it — one buffer shape in five fails.

- **`StringLiteralNode.isUnterminated` IS *FALSE* FOR A LONE OPENING QUOTE — `Parser.kt` decides it as `startsWithQuote && raw.last() != quote`, and for a one-character raw text the first character IS the last, so `o["` at end of file parses as a TERMINATED empty string (round 929).** That is exactly the state an editor asks a completion in, and nothing else notices: the node is well formed and no diagnostic moves — only a consumer that BRANCHES on the flag answers wrongly. Check the arithmetic beside it (`end - pos < 2` cannot be a closed literal), or fix the parser and expect a new TS1002 on that shape.
- **EVERY OFFSET→LINE CONVERSION IN THIS COMPILER GOES THROUGH `lineBreakWidthAt` (`LineStarts.kt`) — `\n`, `\r\n` AND A LONE `\r` EACH END EXACTLY ONE LINE — AND A SIXTH PRIVATE COPY IS A SELF-INCONSISTENCY NOTHING HERE CAN SEE (round 910 found the split, round 915 unified it).** There were **six** implementations in **three** conventions, none referring to any other: the Parser's (correct), `Checker.lineStartsFor` and its INVERSE `posOfLineCol` (`\n` only, so the two directions did not round-trip), `TypeScriptCompiler.positionToLineCharacter` + its inline TS2688 twin, the `Transformer`'s JSX dev-runtime coordinates (EMITTED JavaScript, not a diagnostic), and `CompilerOptions.computeLineAndColumn` (which treated `\r` as **zero-width**). Add a converter only by delegating to `computeLineStarts` (memoized/indexed) or `lineAndCharacterAt` (bounded scan); `LineTerminatorConsistencyTest` + `ProjectPositionTest`'s lone-`\r` differential are the pins, and `-project`'s `LineMap` is the ONE sanctioned reimplementation (it also needs `lineContentEnds`).
- **…AND THE REASON THAT FAMILY WAS INVISIBLE IS NOT "no fixture has a lone `\r`" BUT "NO FIXTURE CAN": `parseMultiFileSource` — the `// @directive`/`@Filename` splitter behind `TypeScriptCompiler.compile(String)`, i.e. the WHOLE generated corpus and every `diagnose()` pin — opens with `.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n")` (round 915).** `String.trimIndent` in `diagnose` does the same one layer up. So a line-terminator pin written the normal way is VACUOUS AND PASSES (round 915's first one did); use `diagnoseVerbatim`, which hands the pipeline a `ParsedSource` directly. Only the project/`Vfs` path preserves terminators — which is the `(API.*)` embedding path, so anything about source TEXT SHAPE is corpus-invisible and `-project`-visible. A fixture BEGINNING with a break is vacuous for a second reason: blank lines before the first content line are dropped.
- **A DOC SECTION THAT SUMMARISES BEHAVIOUR IS NOT EVIDENCE OF IT, AND MEASURED HERE IT DECAYS AT ~ONE FALSE CLAIM PER THREE ROUNDS — `docs/language-service.md` § 14 was written in round 928 and audited by EXECUTION in round 930 with FOUR of its claims false, one a defect closed BEFORE the page was written and one a defect the page's own § 9 had been promising for two rounds.** It fails in BOTH directions (two of the four were wrong in the compiler's FAVOUR) and re-READING the source confirms a claim rather than testing it, which is how the stale one survived three readings. So: a behavioural claim a test can express belongs in a test the page NAMES (`LanguageServiceStateTest`), and a claim no test can express — a wall figure, "there is no X layer" — must be MARKED as such in the page. **Corollary from the same round: hover and go-to-definition are TWO mechanisms over one caret** (`typeCaptureThisMemberType` vs `typeCaptureMemberSymbols`), so a receiver leg added to one is absent from the other — `super.p` typed correctly and navigated NOWHERE — and a claim confirmed at § 8 is no evidence at all about § 9.

- **Every AST node class extends `NodeBase` (INV.2(a), round 495): `nodeId`/`parent` are stamped by `indexSourceFile` at the end of `Parser.parse()`; data-class `copy()` / Transformer-synthesized nodes stay UNINDEXED (−1/null) — never rely on nodeId/parent after a transform.**
- **M0.2 (round 621): every node class ALSO stamps `NodeBase.kindId` via `init { kindId = NodeKind.X }` (runs on `copy()` too — synthesized nodes STAY stamped, unlike nodeId/parent), and the hot dispatchers (`forEachChild`, the checkSpine when-chains) switch on it.** A NEW node class is compile-forced through `nodeKindIdOf` (sealed-exhaustive) into a dense `NodeKind` const, but the class's own stamp is pinned only at runtime (NodeKindIdTest + forEachChild's loud `else`); keep NodeKind ids DENSE (density buys forEachChild's tableswitch). When converting another `when(node)` dispatcher to `when(kindId)`, a MULTI-LINE `if`-header arm is the mangle hazard (an inserted arm-close can empty the gate and run the body unconditionally) — after any scripted conversion, scan for a line ending `{` followed by a DEDENTED bare `}`. A NEW node class needs `NodeBase(), ` first in its supertype list (else indexSourceFile's cast crashes every parse) plus a `forEachChild` arm (compile-enforced exhaustive `when`); a new NODE-TYPED property on an existing class must be walked in `forEachChild` — the jvmTest reflection oracle (`ForEachChildOracleTest`) diffs the enumeration against the data-class componentN properties and fails on the gap. LUB trap: the shared superclass makes an inferred-return `when`/`?:` mixing node classes degrade to `Any` — annotate the return type explicitly (cf. `parsePropertyName`).
- **Every option-derived `Parser(...)` constructor argument MUST flow through `ParserFlags`/`computeParserFlags` (INV.1(e), round 494)**: the project crawl pre-parses each file with those flags and the core's multi-file site REUSES the tree when recorded content+flags match (`ParsedSource.preParsed`) — a parse-affecting parameter computed inline at a parse site is INVISIBLE to the match, so the core silently reuses a tree parsed WITHOUT it (a wrong tree, not just lost perf). Extend `ParserFlags` + `computeParserFlags` instead; `Inv1PreParseReuseTest` pins the gate.
- **`lookupPerFileForNode` IS KEYED BY THE *FILE*, SO IT ANSWERS FOR A FILE-LEVEL DECLARATION HOWEVER DEEPLY THE REFERENCE IS NESTED — AND B83.5 KEEPS THE *SHADOWING* BLOCK-SCOPED DECLARATION OUT OF THE BINDER TABLES, SO NOTHING DOWNSTREAM CAN NOTICE (2026-08-26, (CHK.47); 17 false positives on knip, and the shipped message named the OUTER type).** A consumer that resolves a NAME must ask `lexicalScopeSymbol` first — round 748's `symbols`-only rule makes a non-null hit on the reference's parent chain *mean* "shadow", because a name the main binder bound is absent from its container's `symbols`. `getTypeOfIdentifier` is no safer than the per-file symbol here: it reads the same file/global tables. **And a walker with its OWN hand-written shadow test is a second, silent copy of this trap** — `spineExFnShadows` compared `(x.name as? Identifier)?.text` and was blind to every destructuring form.
- **Block-scoped declarations are NOT bound** (B83.5, 2026-05-27): `Binder.kt:bindStatement` dispatches symbol-declaration cases by explicit `when` arm, and its catch-all `else` silently ignores `Block`/`If`/`While`/`For`/`Try`/`Switch` — so any `class`/`interface`/`type`/`enum`/`function` declared INSIDE one is NEVER in file `locals` or `globals`, and every `globals[name]` / `result.locals[name]` / `perFileScope` lookup for it answers null. **The failure is not always a null, and that is the trap (round 747): in TYPE position a UNIQUE name degrades to `any` SILENTLY (no TS2304 — the INV.4(c)(iii) family finds it through the INV.2(c) lexical scopes while `getTypeFromTypeReference` does not), while a name that SHADOWS an outer declaration resolves to the OUTER symbol** — so the annotation is judged against the wrong type rather than an absent one, and a fix here cannot be a "when the lookup misses" fallback: a walker needing the inner declaration must read it from the AST. `computeAllEnumValues` has the same blind spot. **CLOSED FOR `enum` ONLY (round 748, `lexicalTypeSymbolForNode`); the other kinds are still unbound**, and a name-based enum reader must consult ALL THREE scopes — file locals, the enclosing-NAMESPACE exports (round 769), and the function-body SCOPE space (round 782, consulted FIRST, not as a miss-fallback, or the same enum is keyed through two `Symbol` instances = round 425's split key space). When the AST is in hand the surgical pattern is a TRANSIENT `Symbol` synthesized for `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol` and never published to the binder tables; the worked example and the still-open VALUE-position gap are in the archive.
